LAYERS 6 AND 9: DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY, AND DISEASES AND DISORDERS

The Per-Type and Per-Region Developmental and Pathological Catalogues for the Cell and Region Layers

A Companion Volume to the Layer 6 Catalogues and to the Layer 9 Regional Atlas

Keyed, chapter by chapter and section by section, to LAYER_06_OTHER_CELLS_MANUSCRIPT (85 non-neuronal types), to the five-volume neuron catalogue (301 neuronal types), and to LAYER_09_NATURES_COGNITIVE_ARCHITECTURE_MANUSCRIPT (139 numbered regional headings in sections 2.1 through 2.11), in the Certified_Neuroscientist corpus of D. R. Dison, Founder, Artificial Intelligence University (AIU).

Date of compilation: August 12, 2026.


PROVENANCE, STATED BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE

This companion was added on 2026-08-12 by the konnectome build, under the owner's explicit authority of that date.

It is THIS BUILD'S WRITING, living in the corpus's own directory, and it does NOT outrank the corpus's own documents.

Where this companion and a parent manuscript disagree, the parent manuscript is right and this file is wrong, and the correction is owed to this file.

It is ADDITIVE.

It alters nothing that the parent manuscripts say, it edits no existing file, and it removes nothing.

It is keyed by the parent manuscripts' own chapter numbers and section numbers, in paste-ready form, so that a future merge is a mechanical splice and not an act of interpretation - exactly as the MODES companions were written and for exactly the same reason.

Its content was checked against the published literature by web search on 2026-08-12, and the corpus outranks anything found that way.

A citation in this file is a pointer to where the build looked, not a warrant that overrides the corpus.


THE GAP THIS FILLS, WITH THE COUNTS ON THE DOCUMENT'S OWN FACE

This companion fills the gap recorded in the konnectome gap analysis of 2026-08-12.

The gap was established by counting headers, not by impression, and the counts are reproduced here so that the reason for this file is visible without leaving it.

• The header DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: appears 64 times in LAYER_07_SYNAPTICS_MANUSCRIPT, 71 times in LAYER_08_MICROCIRCUITS_MANUSCRIPT, and 44 times in LAYER_10_SYSTEMS_MANUSCRIPT.

• The cognate header DEVELOPMENT AND LIFESPAN: appears 26 times in LAYER_11_GLOBAL_STATES_MANUSCRIPT and 18 times in LAYER_04_05_SLOW_VARIABLES_MANUSCRIPT.

• The header DISEASES AND DISORDERS: appears 64, 71, 44 and 26 times in Layers 7, 8, 10 and 11 respectively.

• BOTH HEADERS RETURN ZERO in LAYER_06_OTHER_CELLS_MANUSCRIPT.

• BOTH HEADERS RETURN ZERO in every one of the five volumes of the neuronal manuscript.

• BOTH HEADERS RETURN ZERO in LAYER_09_NATURES_COGNITIVE_ARCHITECTURE_MANUSCRIPT.

So the corpus's two largest catalogues, and its guiding book, are the three documents that cannot say where anything comes from or how it fails.

That is 386 cell types and 139 regional headings carrying no statement of developmental origin, no birth date, no maturation trajectory, and no disease attribution.

The absence is not an oversight of the writing but a consequence of the template.

THE LAYER 6 CHAPTER TEMPLATE, VERSION 3, LISTS TWENTY-ONE ASPECT HEADERS, AND NOT ONE OF THEM IS DEVELOPMENTAL ORIGIN, LINEAGE, MATURATION, OR DISEASE.

The twenty-one are WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES, STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY, SIZE, STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES, WHERE IT LIVES, NUMBER, ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES, MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS, SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS, AXON CHARACTERISTICS, NEUROTRANSMITTERS, MYELINATION, METABOLIC DEMANDS, PLASTICITY, SUPPORT SYSTEM, HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION, BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE, SYSTEMS INTERFACE, ENGLISH READABLE CODE, CODE SAMPLE, and WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE.

A cell chapter written to that template therefore cannot express its own origin even if the author knows it, because there is no slot to put it in.

A TEMPLATE REVISION IS THEREFORE OWED, AND IS NAMED HERE.

A version 4 of the Layer 6 chapter template should add two aspects, DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: and DISEASES AND DISORDERS:, positioned after PLASTICITY: and before SUPPORT SYSTEM:, matching the ordering already used in Layers 7, 8 and 10.

Until that revision is made, the material in this companion has nowhere native to live, and this file is its holding place.


THE SCOPE DECISION, STATED EXPLICITLY, WITH THE COUNTS

This is the most important section in this document, and it is placed early so that no reader mistakes what has been done for what remains.

Five hundred and twenty-five entries written well is beyond one document written in one pass.

A companion that stubbed four hundred of them would be worse than the gap it filled, because a stub looks like coverage on a header count while carrying no information, and it would defeat the very audit that produced this file.

So the scope was cut deliberately, and along a line chosen to keep every part of it honest.

• COVERED IN FULL, ENTRY BY ENTRY: all 85 non-neuronal cell types of LAYER_06_OTHER_CELLS_MANUSCRIPT, Chapters 1 through 85.

• COVERED IN FULL, ENTRY BY ENTRY: all 139 numbered regional headings of LAYER_09_NATURES_COGNITIVE_ARCHITECTURE_MANUSCRIPT sections 2.1 through 2.11.

• COVERED BY CLASS, NOT INDIVIDUALLY: the 301 neuron chapters of the five volumes of the neuronal manuscript.

The 301 neuron chapters are grouped into 27 developmental lineage classes, each class receiving one full treatment of both fields, followed by a keyed index that maps every one of the 301 chapters to its class.

That is a complete and honest treatment at a coarser grain.

It is defensible on the biology and not merely on the budget, because developmental origin is genuinely a property of the lineage rather than of the terminal type: the parvalbumin basket cell of the cortex and the parvalbumin basket cell of the hippocampus have the same origin story, the same specifying transcription factors, and very nearly the same disease profile, and writing that story twice would add length without adding knowledge.

Where a neuron chapter departs from its class in a way that matters, the departure is noted against that chapter in the index rather than being silently absorbed.

WHAT A FUTURE PASS STILL OWES, NAMED SO THAT IT CANNOT BE FORGOTTEN.

• Per-chapter DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY and DISEASES AND DISORDERS blocks for each of the 301 neuron chapters individually, at the grain used here for the 85 non-neuronal types.

The class treatment gives the lineage, the transcription factor cascade, the birth window and the class-level disease profile; what it cannot give is the per-type birth order within a class, the per-type maturation rate, and the per-type selective vulnerability, and those three are real and are missing.

• The version 4 template revision described above, and the mechanical splice of this companion's blocks into the parent chapters once that revision exists.

• A parallel companion for the Layer 6 MODES manuscript's own coverage, which was not examined in this pass.

• Human-specific timings for the many entries below where only rodent data exist, which are marked in place and are not silently converted.


HOW TO READ AN ENTRY

Each entry carries exactly two blocks and nothing else, so that it is paste-ready.

ENTRY: the chapter number and title, or the section number and title, exactly as the parent manuscript names it.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: the lineage and germ layer or progenitor domain, the transcription factors that specify it where known, when it is born and when it matures with real timings, whether it is generated after birth, whether it can be replaced, and the critical or sensitive period it participates in if any.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: the disorders in which the entry is primarily implicated, the mechanism of failure where known, whether the entry is a CAUSE, a CASUALTY, or a MARKER, and its selective vulnerability profile.


THE CAUSE, CASUALTY, MARKER DISTINCTION, STATED ONCE

This companion introduces a three-way distinction that the corpus should be able to make and currently has no vocabulary for, and it is defined here once rather than in each of the entries that use it.

• A CAUSE is an entry whose own malfunction initiates the disorder.

The astrocyte in Alexander disease is a cause: the mutation is in the astrocyte's own glial fibrillary acidic protein gene, and everything else in that disease is downstream.

• A CASUALTY is an entry that is destroyed or disabled by a process originating elsewhere, and whose loss then produces the symptoms.

The pancreatic beta cell in type 1 diabetes is a casualty: the mutation is not in the beta cell, the attack is immunological, and the beta cell's contribution is to be uniquely irreplaceable.

• A MARKER is an entry whose change reliably indicates a disorder without either causing it or being the seat of its symptoms.

The olfactory bulb in Parkinson's disease is close to a pure marker: olfactory loss precedes motor onset by years and is among the most reliable early signs, but anosmia is not what makes Parkinson's disease a movement disorder.

The three are not exclusive and the interesting entries are the ones that are two of them at once, and where that is so it is said.

An entry can also be a cause in one disorder and a casualty in another, and the classification below is therefore always made per disorder and never per entry.


A NOTE ON WHAT IS NOT KNOWN

Many of the non-neuronal cell types below have genuinely unsettled lineages, and many of the regions below have no clean disease attribution at all.

Where that is so it is said by name, in the form the lineage of this cell is not settled, or there is no disorder for which this region is the primary seat, followed by a statement of what evidence would settle the question.

No block in this file is filled with a plausible generic sentence in place of a fact.

That rule was adopted because a companion whose five hundred entries all sound the same is one that was generated rather than researched, and it would be worse than the gap it fills, since it would close the audit that found the gap while leaving the corpus no better informed.

The reader should expect the honest entries to be visibly shorter than the well-studied ones, and should read that unevenness as a feature.

================================================================================

PART ONE: THE 85 NON-NEURONAL CELL TYPES, ENTRY BY ENTRY

Keyed to LAYER_06_OTHER_CELLS_MANUSCRIPT, chapters 1 through 85.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 1. PROTOPLASMIC ASTROCYTE (Grey-Matter Domain Astrocyte) (The Million-Synapse Gardener Cell) (COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The protoplasmic astrocyte arises from the neuroepithelium of the dorsal telencephalon, generated by the same apical radial glia that first made the neurons. The switch from neuron production to glia production, the gliogenic switch, is driven by the transcription factors Sox9 and Nuclear Factor One A (NFIA), which Sox9 induces and then partners with to switch on the astrocyte gene programme.

In rodents this switch occurs in the embryonic cortex around embryonic days 10.5 to 11.5 (rodent), with the bulk of cortical astrocytes born from late embryonic life onward. A large share of the final population is produced after birth by local proliferation of immature astrocytes rather than by fresh divisions of radial glia, which is a rodent-established point.

Human timing is not settled to a single figure and should not be given one; the safe statement is that human cortical astrogenesis begins in the second half of gestation and continues into the early postnatal period, with the specific weeks still debated.

Protoplasmic astrocytes tile the grey matter into non-overlapping domains, and this domain organisation is set during the postnatal expansion. Astrocytes are added and replaced only slowly in the healthy adult brain; there is no large stem-cell factory refilling them, and after injury the local reactive response, not neurogenesis, restocks the territory.

Astrocyte maturation overlaps the peak of synapse formation, so these cells are participants in the postnatal critical periods of cortical wiring rather than mere bystanders.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

In Alexander disease the protoplasmic astrocyte is the CAUSE: a dominant mutation in the astrocyte intermediate-filament gene glial fibrillary acidic protein (GFAP) makes the protein aggregate into Rosenthal fibres and poisons the cell, and the myelin loss and neuronal harm follow from that primary astrocyte defect.

In epilepsy the astrocyte is often a CAUSE or a contributor, because failure of its potassium and glutamate clearance raises local excitability. In Alzheimer disease, amyotrophic lateral sclerosis, and Huntington disease the astrocyte is frequently a CASUALTY that then becomes harmful, losing support functions and turning reactive as disease spreads from elsewhere.

Its selective vulnerability profile follows its workload: astrocytes in regions of high synaptic and metabolic demand, and those bearing the mutant GFAP in Alexander disease, are hit hardest.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 2. FIBROUS ASTROCYTE (White-Matter Tract Astrocyte) (The Cable-Duct Caretaker Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The fibrous astrocyte shares the astrocyte lineage: it is generated from radial glia and their progeny after the gliogenic switch driven by Sox9 and NFIA, and it settles among the myelinated tracts of the white matter. Whether grey-matter protoplasmic and white-matter fibrous astrocytes are separate committed lineages or one lineage shaped by its local environment is not fully settled; report it as unresolved rather than picking a side.

Fibrous astrocytes appear as the white matter myelinates, which in rodents is largely a late-embryonic and postnatal process and in humans extends from late gestation across the first years of life. They are produced and expanded postnatally alongside the tracts they tend.

They are not meaningfully replaced from a stem pool in the healthy adult; maintenance is by slow local turnover and, after injury, by reactive change.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

In multiple sclerosis and other white-matter diseases the fibrous astrocyte is both a CASUALTY of the inflammatory attack on myelin and a CAUSE of the persistent scar, since its reactive border is the glial scar that blocks remyelination and axon regrowth.

In vascular white-matter disease and leukodystrophies it is a CASUALTY of the primary insult, and in Alexander disease, which strikes white matter first, the fibrous astrocyte is a CAUSE through mutant GFAP.

Its vulnerability tracks the vulnerability of the tracts: periventricular and deep white matter, poorly perfused, is where fibrous astrocytes are stressed earliest.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 3. INTERLAMINAR ASTROCYTE (Layer-1 Interlaminar Astrocyte) (The Layer-Lacing Plumb-Line Cell) (COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The interlaminar astrocyte is an astrocyte-lineage cell of cortical layer 1, prominent in primates and human-enriched, and essentially absent as a mature elaborated type in rodents. Because it is a primate specialisation, its developmental programme cannot be read off rodent timelines, and much of it is genuinely unknown.

What is established is that these cells are immature at birth and elaborate their long descending fibres over postnatal life; the fully developed plumb-line morphology is a feature of the mature human cortex. Exact birth dates, the transcription factors that specify this subtype, and whether it is a distinct lineage or a layer-1 modification of ordinary astrocytes are not settled, and no honest single timing can be given.

There is no evidence of an adult stem source that replaces them; like other astrocytes they are long-lived resident cells.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Interlaminar astrocytes show disturbed morphology in Alzheimer disease and in Down syndrome, where their processes become disorganised; in these settings the cell reads best as a CASUALTY and, at present, largely a MARKER of cortical pathology rather than a proven driver.

Because the cell is poorly studied, causal claims are not warranted; the honest statement is that its disease role is largely uncharacterised beyond these morphological changes. Its selective vulnerability profile is unknown.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 4. VARICOSE PROJECTION ASTROCYTE (Deep-Layer Varicose Projection Astrocyte) (The Beaded Boundary-Breaker Cell) (COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

This is a human- and great-ape-specific astrocyte of deep cortical layers 5 and 6, described in earnest only in 2009. It is one of the least understood cells in the catalogue, and its development is essentially uncharacterised.

The lineage of this cell is not settled; there is no established set of specifying transcription factors, no confirmed birth date, and no measured maturation timescale. No timescale for this entry can be stated honestly. What would settle it is single-cell lineage tracing and developmental sampling in human or great-ape tissue, which is scarce for obvious reasons.

Whether it is generated or replaced after birth is unknown. Its uneven, short entry here reflects real ignorance, not oversight.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

No disorder is reliably attributed to the varicose projection astrocyte at present. It cannot be honestly classified as a CAUSE, CASUALTY, or MARKER for any specific disease, because the data do not exist.

Its selective vulnerability profile is unknown. The evidence that would change this is basic characterisation in diseased human deep cortex.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 5. VELATE ASTROCYTE (Cerebellar Granule-Layer Velate Astrocyte) (The Granule-Swaddling Veil Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The velate astrocyte is the protoplasmic astrocyte of the cerebellar granule layer, remodelled into thin veils for the extreme neuronal density there. It belongs to the astrocyte lineage of the cerebellum, generated from cerebellar ventricular and white-matter progenitors after the neuronal phase.

In rodents cerebellar astrogliogenesis, including the velate type, is largely a postnatal event, tracking the postnatal arrival and settling of granule neurons in the first weeks of life (rodent). In humans cerebellar development likewise extends well past birth, but exact velate-astrocyte timings in humans are not precisely established and should not be invented.

They are a postnatally generated population and are not restocked from a dedicated adult stem pool once mature.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

The velate astrocyte is poorly studied as a disease agent in its own right. In cerebellar degenerations and ataxias it is best read as a CASUALTY that turns reactive as granule neurons are lost, and at present largely a MARKER of granule-layer damage rather than a proven initiator.

Its selective vulnerability is tied to the granule layer it swaddles: where granule neurons die, the velate astrocyte is disturbed. Beyond this, honest specific claims are limited.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 6. BERGMANN GLIA (Radial Astrocyte of the Cerebellar Cortex) (The Ladder-Then-Chaperone Cell) (COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Bergmann glia are astrocytic derivatives of cerebellar radial glia that keep their radial morphology into adulthood, and they share a lineage relationship with the Purkinje neurons beside which they sit. Their specification depends on Sonic hedgehog signalling and standard astroglial factors of the Sox and NFI families.

Their two careers are sequential: the radial fibre serves first as the ladder along which granule neurons migrate inward, then the same cell matures into the perisynaptic chaperone of Purkinje dendrites. In rodents this migration-then-ensheathment sequence plays out across the first two to three postnatal weeks (rodent); in humans the equivalent granule migration extends across late gestation and roughly the first postnatal year, with exact figures still debated.

Bergmann glia are one of the few sites where radial glia persist for life, but they are not a replenishing stem pool for other cells and are themselves not replaced from a factory once the cerebellum matures. Their scaffolding function makes them essential to a cerebellar critical period of granule migration.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

When Bergmann glia fail as a migration scaffold, granule neurons are misplaced, so the cell can be a CAUSE of developmental cerebellar malformation. Loss of their neuron-type glutamate receptors degrades fine motor coordination, a demonstrated causal contribution in rodents.

In the spinocerebellar ataxias and other cerebellar degenerations Bergmann glia are a CASUALTY that becomes reactive, and their disturbance is a reliable MARKER of Purkinje-cell pathology. Their selective vulnerability follows the Purkinje cells they wrap: they are stressed wherever Purkinje neurons are dying.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 7. MÜLLER CELL (Radial Glial Cell of the Retina) (The Living Optical-Fibre Nurse Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The Müller cell is the retina's radial glia and, unlike most radial glia, it persists in that form for life. It is produced by the retinal progenitor cells, and it shares a common precursor with the retinal neurons; it is characteristically the last cell type generated as retinal neurogenesis ends.

In rodents Müller cells are born late, around the perinatal period, spanning late embryonic into early postnatal life (rodent). In humans they are likewise generated in the later phase of retinal development in gestation; exact human birth windows are not fixed to a single figure and should not be manufactured.

They are not routinely replaced in the mature human retina. In fish and some non-mammals Müller cells act as a regenerative stem source after injury, but in mammals this latent capacity is largely suppressed, which is why human retinal neuron loss is not naturally repaired.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

In macular oedema and diabetic retinopathy the Müller cell is a CAUSE and a contributor, because failure of its fluid and potassium handling lets the retina swell. In retinal detachment, glaucoma, and retinitis pigmentosa it is a CASUALTY that turns reactive, and its gliosis is a MARKER of retinal stress.

In Macular Telangiectasia type 2 there is specific evidence that Müller cell loss is an early, arguably causal event. Its selective vulnerability is greatest in the metabolically demanding central and macular retina and wherever fluid balance is stressed.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 8. TANYCYTE (Hypothalamic Radial Glial Cell of the Third Ventricle) (The Blood-Brain Customs-Officer Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The tanycyte is a residual radial glial cell of the third-ventricle floor, one of the discrete sites where the embryonic radial glia scaffold is retained into adulthood rather than converted to astrocytes. It is generated from the hypothalamic ventricular zone during development and keeps its stretched bipolar form.

In rodents tanycytes are specified and settle around the perinatal period and remain radial for life (rodent). Human timing is not precisely fixed and should not be invented.

Notably, tanycytes retain stem and progenitor activity in the adult rodent hypothalamus, generating new neurons and glia in the median eminence and arcuate region, a function tied to feeding and energy balance; the extent of the same activity in adult humans is not settled. This links them to metabolic plasticity rather than a fixed early critical period.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

In obesity and disordered energy balance the tanycyte is implicated as a CAUSE or contributor, because it gates the passage of leptin and other hunger and satiety signals between blood and hypothalamus and controls access to the arcuate nucleus. When that gating fails, central hormone sensing is impaired.

In thyroid and reproductive axis dysfunction the tanycyte can be a CAUSE through its control of releasing-hormone availability. In hypothalamic injury and ageing it is a CASUALTY, and its changes serve as a MARKER of median-eminence barrier disturbance. Its selective vulnerability is concentrated at the leaky median-eminence interface it polices.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 9. PITUICYTE (Astrocyte of the Posterior Pituitary) (The Hormone-Throttling Gatekeeper Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The pituicyte is the specialised astrocyte of the posterior pituitary, the neurohypophysis, which is itself a down-growth of the hypothalamic floor. The pituicyte therefore derives from the neuroepithelial and astroglial lineage of that ventral diencephalic region rather than from the anterior-pituitary placode.

Its detailed birth timing and specifying transcription factors are not well established, and no precise figure should be stated. What is established is its function: it physically wraps and unwraps the oxytocin and vasopressin nerve terminals, retracting on demand during dehydration, childbirth, and nursing, a reversible structural plasticity rather than a one-off developmental event.

There is no evidence of a dedicated adult stem source replacing pituicytes; they are resident cells whose main dynamism is this reversible remodelling.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

The pituicyte is not a common named disease driver. The tumour derived from this lineage, the pituicytoma, is a rare low-grade neoplasm in which the pituicyte is the CAUSE by cell of origin.

In disorders of water balance and hormone release its structural gating can contribute, so it may be a CAUSE or contributor when its wrapping behaviour is deranged, but strong specific data are limited. Elsewhere it is best read as a CASUALTY of posterior-pituitary injury. Its selective vulnerability profile is not well characterised.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 10. GOMORI-POSITIVE ASTROCYTE (Cysteine-Rich Granule-Bearing Astrocyte of the Arcuate Nucleus and Hippocampus) (The Iron-Hoarding Furnace-Room Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The Gomori-positive astrocyte is defined chiefly by histochemistry: an astrocyte of the arcuate nucleus and parts of the hippocampus stuffed with iron-, copper-, and sulphur-rich granules that are the degraded remnants of its own mitochondria. It belongs to the astrocyte lineage, but as a distinct developmental type it is barely characterised.

The lineage detail beyond this, the specifying factors, and the birth timing are not settled, and no honest timescale can be given. The granule-laden phenotype accumulates with age and metabolic load rather than appearing at a fixed developmental point; the granules are wear residue, which itself argues the phenotype is acquired postnatally and progressively.

Whether these cells are generated or replaced after birth as a defined population is not established.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Why a specific astrocyte population in the brain's metabolic-sensing regions should burn its mitochondria hard enough to scar is an open question, and disease attributions are correspondingly weak. These cells are of interest in ageing and in oxidative and iron-related stress, where they are best read as a MARKER of high local oxidative burden, and possibly a CASUALTY of it.

No firm CAUSE role in a named human disease is established. Their selective vulnerability, by definition, is concentrated in the arcuate nucleus and hippocampus where they reside.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 11. SURFACE-ASSOCIATED AND GLIA LIMITANS ASTROCYTE (Astrocytes of the Limiting Glial Membrane) (The Border-Wall Mason Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

These are ordinary astrocytes specialised for a border duty: their endfeet tile the brain's outer pial surface and the perivascular surfaces, forming the glia limitans against a basement membrane. They share the astrocyte lineage generated from radial glia after the Sox9 and NFIA gliogenic switch.

The wall is assembled as astrocytes mature and their endfeet contact the developing basement membrane, largely a late-fetal and postnatal process; precise human timings for the limiting membrane are not fixed to a single figure and should not be invented. The surface-associated subpopulation whose somata sit at the pia is a positional specialisation of the same lineage.

These astrocytes are maintained by slow turnover, not by a replenishing stem pool, and they rebuild the border reactively after breach.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

When the glia limitans is breached, immune cells invade the parenchyma, so this astrocyte barrier is a CAUSE or gatekeeper in the pathology of multiple sclerosis and other neuroinflammatory disease, where its failure permits the destructive phase. In traumatic and ischaemic injury it is a CASUALTY of the primary insult and then a CAUSE of the scar that walls the wound.

Its role in glymphatic waste clearance, through perivascular endfeet, links its dysfunction to protein-clearance failure in ageing and Alzheimer disease, where it reads as a contributor and a MARKER. Its vulnerability concentrates at surfaces and perivascular cuffs where barrier stress is greatest.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 12. REACTIVE ASTROCYTE (Injury- and Disease-Induced Astrocyte State) (The Scar-Laying Emergency-Brigade Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The reactive astrocyte is not a separately born cell type but a state that nearly any astrocyte in this section can enter. It is drawn from the resident astrocyte population, itself generated developmentally from radial glia after the gliogenic switch.

The transformation is triggered within hours of injury and is a re-activation of old developmental programmes: the cell up-regulates GFAP, re-enters proliferation in severe injury, and in the worst cases builds the overlapping-body glial scar. Because it is a response state, it has no fixed birth date; it is available across life, and its intensity scales with the insult.

Reactive astrocytes can proliferate locally to restock the border, which is one of the few settings where mature astrocytes divide again.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

The reactive astrocyte is Janus-faced across disorders. After spinal cord injury and stroke it is a CAUSE of the persistent barrier to axon regrowth through its scar, even while the same scar is protective early by sealing the wound.

In neurodegeneration, driven chronically and badly by inflammatory signals, a harmful reactive sub-state can become a CAUSE of neuron death rather than merely a CASUALTY of the disease. Across almost every central nervous system disease its presence is a reliable MARKER of tissue damage. Its selective vulnerability is not the point; it is the near-universal response, appearing wherever astrocytes sense injury.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 13. MYELINATING (INTERFASCICULAR) OLIGODENDROCYTE (The Speed-Tuning Insulator Cell) (COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The myelinating oligodendrocyte is the mature product of the oligodendrocyte precursor cell, whose lineage is specified by the basic helix-loop-helix factor Olig2 together with Nkx2.2 and Sox10, the last of which stays on through myelination. In the spinal cord the first oligodendrocyte precursors arise ventrally in the motor-neuron progenitor (pMN) domain under Sonic hedgehog, with a later dorsal, hedgehog-independent wave.

In the rodent forebrain precursors emerge in three waves: a ventral Nkx2.1 wave around embryonic day 12.5, a second Gsh2 wave around embryonic day 15.5, and a dorsal Emx1 wave around birth (rodent). The ventral-born cells are later largely outcompeted, so the mature cortex is dominated by the later waves.

Myelination itself is mostly postnatal and, in humans, continues into the third decade of life, a genuine finding distinct from the debunked claim that the whole brain simply matures at 25. New myelinating oligodendrocytes are made from precursors throughout life, which is the cellular basis of activity-dependent myelin plasticity and a form of learning.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

In multiple sclerosis the myelinating oligodendrocyte is the primary CASUALTY of an immune attack on myelin, and the failure of remaining precursors to remyelinate makes the deficit permanent. In the leukodystrophies, including Pelizaeus-Merzbacher disease from proteolipid protein mutations, it is the CAUSE through defective myelin manufacture.

In periventricular white-matter injury of prematurity, spinal cord injury, and stroke it is a CASUALTY of the metabolic and excitotoxic insult. Its selective vulnerability is high: oligodendrocytes are exquisitely sensitive to oxidative stress, glutamate excitotoxicity, and energy failure, and the premature and hypoxic brain loses them readily.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 14. SATELLITE (PERINEURONAL) OLIGODENDROCYTE (The Neuron-Side Reserve Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The satellite oligodendrocyte is a mature grey-matter oligodendrocyte sitting against a neuronal cell body without wrapping myelin. It shares the oligodendrocyte lineage, specified by Olig2, Nkx2.2, and Sox10 and derived from the same precursor waves that supply the myelinating cells.

Its birth follows the general oligodendrocyte timeline, which in the forebrain runs from late embryonic life through the early postnatal period and beyond; a precise separate schedule for the satellite subtype is not established and should not be invented. Whether every satellite cell is a distinct committed subtype or a positional state of grey-matter oligodendrocytes is not fully settled.

New oligodendrocytes continue to be produced from precursors in adult grey matter, so this reserve population can in principle be added to across life.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

The satellite oligodendrocyte is understudied as a disease driver. In epilepsy, ischaemia, and neurodegeneration it is best read as a CASUALTY that can become reactive, and its increased numbers around stressed neurons are used as a MARKER of grey-matter pathology.

There is interest in whether it can be recruited to remyelinate on demand, but a firm CAUSE role in a named disorder is not established. Its selective vulnerability mirrors that of oligodendrocytes generally, namely sensitivity to oxidative and excitotoxic stress.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 15. OLIGODENDROCYTE PRECURSOR CELL, ALSO CALLED THE NG2 GLIA OR POLYDENDROCYTE (The Circuit-Listening Recruit Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The oligodendrocyte precursor cell is specified by Olig2, Sox10, and Nkx2.2 and marked by the chondroitin sulphate proteoglycan NG2 and the receptor PDGFRA. In the forebrain it is generated in the three successive waves noted above: a ventral Nkx2.1 wave around rodent embryonic day 12.5, a Gsh2 wave around embryonic day 15.5, and a dorsal Emx1 wave around birth (rodent).

Uniquely among glia, it receives genuine synapses from neurons and hears neural activity directly. It remains proliferative throughout life and is the commonest dividing cell in the adult human brain, evenly tiled through the parenchyma, and it is the standing reserve from which new adult myelinating oligodendrocytes are drafted.

It is therefore both generated after birth and continuously self-replaced, and its activity-driven differentiation into myelinating cells underlies adaptive myelination during learning.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

In multiple sclerosis the precursor is a CASUALTY whose failure to differentiate and remyelinate, rather than its absence, largely explains why lesions do not repair. In diffuse intrinsic pontine glioma and many other gliomas, cells of this precursor-like state are strongly implicated as the CAUSE by cell of origin, a leading current view of glioma origin.

In white-matter injury of prematurity the precursor is a CASUALTY that is arrested rather than killed. Its differentiation block is a recurring MARKER of chronic demyelination. Its selective vulnerability lies in this maturation arrest under inflammatory and hypoxic conditions rather than in outright fragility.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 16. MICROGLIA (Resident Macrophage of the Central Nervous System) (The Synapse-Pruning Sentinel Cell) (COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Microglia are not neuroectodermal at all: they are yolk-sac-derived myeloid cells, arising from erythromyeloid progenitors of the extraembryonic yolk sac. In rodents primitive macrophages leave the yolk sac around embryonic day 8.0 to 8.5 and colonise the neuroepithelium from about embryonic day 9.0 to 9.5 (rodent), long before the blood-brain barrier closes.

In humans they enter the brain rudiment very early, from around the fourth week of gestation, and continue colonising the cerebrum across roughly gestational weeks 4 to 24. Their specification and maintenance depend on colony-stimulating factor 1 receptor signalling and factors including PU.1 and SALL1.

Once seated, microglia are a self-renewing resident population maintained by local division rather than routine blood resupply in the healthy brain. In the fetal and postnatal window they prune a large fraction of synapses, making them active participants in the critical periods of circuit refinement.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

In Alzheimer disease microglia are both CASUALTY and CAUSE: genome-wide risk is enriched in microglial genes such as TREM2, and dysregulated microglia drive damaging inflammation and mis-pruning, though they also clear amyloid. Complement-tagged over-pruning by microglia is implicated as a CAUSE in schizophrenia and in synapse loss across neurodegeneration.

In the microgliopathies, including Nasu-Hakola disease and CSF1R-related leukoencephalopathy, defective microglial genes are the CAUSE. In almost every brain disease reactive microglia are a reliable MARKER. Their vulnerability is a functional one: with ageing and chronic stimulation they drift into harmful, senescent, over-inflamed states rather than dying outright.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 17. EPENDYMOCYTE (Ciliated Lining Cell of the Ventricles) (The Fluid-Stirring Lining Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The ependymocyte is a multiciliated cuboidal cell derived from radial glia, forming the single-layer lining of the ventricles and central canal. Its differentiation is governed by the master ciliogenesis transcription factor Foxj1, together with the geminin-family factors GemC1 and Multicilin (Mcidas) that license the multiciliated fate.

Ependymal cells are specified before birth from radial glia and complete their ciliary maturation in the early postnatal period; in rodents this ciliary differentiation is largely a first-postnatal-week event (rodent). They are essentially post-mitotic and are not replaced in adulthood, so lost ependyma is not regenerated.

Their coordinated ciliary beating streams the cerebrospinal fluid and helps guide young migrating neuroblasts, tying them to postnatal migratory pathways.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

In hydrocephalus the ependymocyte is a CAUSE when ciliary motility fails, because loss of directed cerebrospinal fluid flow and of the ependymal lining promotes fluid accumulation; primary ciliary dyskinesia mutations produce this. In infection and haemorrhage the ependyma is a CASUALTY that is stripped, and its loss then worsens flow.

Ependymoma is a tumour in which the ependymal or radial-glial lineage is the CAUSE by cell of origin. Ependymal disruption is a MARKER of ventricular disease. Its selective vulnerability is mechanical and infectious: as an exposed single layer facing the fluid, it is readily damaged and cannot be rebuilt.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 18. CHOROID PLEXUS EPITHELIAL CELL (Secretory Cell of the Blood-Cerebrospinal-Fluid Barrier) (The Fluid-Making Barrier Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The choroid plexus epithelial cell is a specialised secretory epithelium derived from the neuroepithelium of the ventricular roof plates, a close relative of the ependymocyte, with its maturation influenced by Otx2 and bone morphogenetic protein signalling from the roof plate. The four plexuses arise in a fixed order during gestation.

These cells are among the earliest brain structures to differentiate, functional well before birth so that cerebrospinal fluid is made throughout fetal life; precise human production onset is early gestation, but I will not attach a spurious exact week beyond that. They form the blood-cerebrospinal-fluid barrier through their tight junctions.

They are long-lived and not appreciably replaced from a stem pool in the adult; maintenance is slow.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

In choroid plexus tumours, papilloma and carcinoma, this epithelial cell is the CAUSE by cell of origin, and over-secretion of fluid by such tumours causes hydrocephalus. In ageing and Alzheimer disease the cell is a CASUALTY whose declining secretion and barrier and clearance functions contribute to impaired waste removal, making it both a contributor and a MARKER.

It is also a portal of entry and a CASUALTY in central nervous system infection and inflammation. Its selective vulnerability lies in its high metabolic secretory load and its position at a fenestrated-vessel interface, which exposes it to blood-borne toxins and pathogens.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 19. MYELINATING SCHWANN CELL (Myelin-Forming Schwann Cell of the Peripheral Nerve) (The One-Axon Insulation-Wrapping Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The myelinating Schwann cell arises from the neural crest through an ordered lineage: neural crest cell to Schwann cell precursor to immature Schwann cell, all marked by Sox10 from the earliest crest stage onward. Around birth the immature Schwann cell chooses its fate, and the myelinating path requires induction of Oct6 at the promyelinating stage followed by Krox20 (Egr2), which switches on the myelin genes.

In rodents this maturation and the onset of myelination cluster around birth and the first postnatal weeks (rodent). In humans peripheral myelination begins in mid-to-late gestation and continues after birth; a single exact human figure is not warranted.

Crucially, the Schwann cell keeps a lifelong plasticity: after nerve injury even mature myelinating cells can dedifferentiate and later remyelinate, which is why peripheral nerve, unlike central white matter, repairs.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

In Charcot-Marie-Tooth disease, the commonest inherited neuropathy, the myelinating Schwann cell is the CAUSE in the demyelinating forms, most often through duplication or mutation of the myelin gene PMP22 or of MPZ and connexin-32. In Guillain-Barre syndrome and chronic inflammatory demyelinating polyneuropathy it is the CASUALTY of an immune attack on myelin.

In leprosy the cell is a CAUSE-adjacent target that Mycobacterium leprae invades. Schwann cells of the myelinating lineage are also the CAUSE of schwannomas and of neurofibromas in NF1 and NF2. Its selective vulnerability tracks the longest, largest fibres first, producing the length-dependent, distal-onset pattern typical of peripheral neuropathy.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 20. NON-MYELINATING (REMAK) SCHWANN CELL (Remak-Bundle Schwann Cell) (The Bare-Fibre Bundle-Sleeving Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The non-myelinating Remak Schwann cell shares the neural crest lineage and the Sox10-marked progression of its myelinating sibling, diverging only at the perinatal fate choice. Instead of switching on Krox20-driven myelin, the immature Schwann cell that ensheathes thin axons remains in a non-myelinating configuration and gathers many small axons into Remak bundles.

Fate here is instructed largely by the axon: thin-diameter axons that do not signal enough neuregulin-1 keep the Schwann cell non-myelinating. In rodents the sorting of axons into myelinated versus Remak fates occurs around birth and the early postnatal weeks (rodent), with human sorting spanning late gestation and infancy.

These cells retain the family's injury plasticity and can convert to repair cells after damage.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

In small-fibre neuropathy the Remak Schwann cell is closely involved with the unmyelinated C fibres that carry pain and autonomic signals, and its dysfunction contributes to loss of those fibres, so it can be a CAUSE or contributor as well as a CASUALTY. In diabetic neuropathy it is a CASUALTY of metabolic stress that then fails to support its axons.

Its disturbance is a MARKER of C-fibre pathology. Its selective vulnerability is tied to the thin, unmyelinated sensory and autonomic axons it sleeves, which are among the earliest fibres lost in metabolic and toxic neuropathies.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 21. REPAIR SCHWANN CELL (Injury-Induced Repair Schwann Cell, the Cell of the Bands of Büngner) (The Regrowth-Rail-Laying Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The repair Schwann cell is a state, not a separately born type: after nerve injury every Schwann cell below the cut, myelinating and Remak alike, transdifferentiates into it. The switch is driven by the transcription factor c-Jun, which activates a dedicated repair programme distinct from the immature Schwann cell state, and it operates within days of injury.

The cells digest their own myelin, elongate, and align end to end into the bands of Büngner, the living rails along which severed axons regrow. Because it is an injury response of the mature cell, it has no developmental birth date; it is the emergency profession the Schwann cell keeps in reserve throughout life.

Its effectiveness declines with prolonged denervation and with age, a rodent-established point, which limits repair after long delays.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

The repair Schwann cell is generally the hero rather than the culprit. Its failure is a CAUSE of poor regeneration: when the repair state cannot be sustained, as after long-standing denervation or in aged nerve, axons do not reconnect and function is not restored.

In chronic neuropathies its exhaustion is a contributor to permanent deficit. Its presence and the bands of Büngner are a MARKER of active regeneration. Its selective vulnerability is temporal: the repair phenotype is not durable, and its decay over weeks to months is the main clinical obstacle to recovery after nerve injury.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 22. TERMINAL (PERISYNAPTIC) SCHWANN CELL, ALSO CALLED TELOGLIA (Perisynaptic Schwann Cell of the Neuromuscular Junction) (The Junction-Capping Caretaker Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The terminal or perisynaptic Schwann cell is a neural-crest-derived, Sox10-lineage glial cell of the Schwann family that caps the neuromuscular junction. It arrives with the motor axon as the junction forms and matures alongside it, so its assembly is tied to neuromuscular junction development around birth and the early postnatal period in rodents (rodent).

These cells are essential partners in junction formation, maturation, and the postnatal pruning from multiple to single innervation, so they participate directly in the developmental refinement of the neuromuscular synapse. In humans junction maturation likewise spans late gestation and infancy; a single exact figure is not warranted.

They are retained for life at each junction, monitoring transmitter and, after damage, extending processes to guide reinnervation and rebuild the synapse.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

In amyotrophic lateral sclerosis the terminal Schwann cell shows early disturbance and impaired repair capacity, so it is best read as a CASUALTY and a contributor to failed reinnervation, and its changes are an early MARKER of junction breakdown. In ageing sarcopenia its declining repair support contributes to junction loss.

In peripheral nerve injury its guidance function aids reinnervation, and its failure is a CAUSE of poor junctional recovery. Its selective vulnerability follows the neuromuscular junctions of large, fast motor units, which are among the earliest to be lost in motor neuron disease and ageing.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 23. SATELLITE GLIAL CELL (Satellite Glial Cell of the Peripheral Ganglia) (The Cell-Body-Cradling Envelope Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The satellite glial cell is a neural-crest-derived, Sox10-lineage glial cell that wraps the somata of peripheral sensory, sympathetic, and parasympathetic neurons, sealing each into a private unit. It develops alongside the ganglion neurons from the same crest population, and in the dorsal root ganglion a share of both neurons and satellite cells descends from boundary cap cells.

Ganglion assembly and satellite ensheathment occur through embryonic and early postnatal life; in rodents the envelopes tighten around birth and the first postnatal weeks (rodent). Exact human timings are not fixed and should not be invented.

Satellite glia retain some proliferative and progenitor capacity into adulthood, and they proliferate after nerve injury, so the population is not entirely static.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

In chronic and neuropathic pain the satellite glial cell has become a front-page CAUSE and contributor: after nerve injury it activates, up-regulates GFAP, couples through gap junctions, and releases mediators that sensitise the sensory neuron it wraps, amplifying pain. This is one of the clearest glial contributions to a symptom.

In herpes zoster and other ganglion infections it is a CASUALTY and a harbour for virus. Its activation is a reliable MARKER of ganglionic injury. Its selective vulnerability follows the neurons it cradles: it responds wherever the parent sensory or autonomic neuron is stressed or axotomised.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 24. ENTERIC GLIAL CELL (Glial Cell of the Enteric Nervous System) (The Gut-Circuit Housekeeping Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Enteric glia are neural-crest-derived, Sox10-lineage cells, arising chiefly from the vagal neural crest that colonises the gut from the foregut rostrally to the hindgut, with a smaller sacral contribution to the hindgut. They differentiate after the enteric neurons, from the same migrating crest-derived progenitors.

In rodents enteric glia are generated largely in late embryonic and early postnatal life and continue to be added after birth (rodent); the enteric nervous system matures across gestation and infancy in humans. Uniquely among glia they retain substantial adult neurogenic and gliogenic potential, able to generate new neurons and glia in the adult gut under injury, so the population is genuinely renewable.

They live in the most mechanically and immunologically hostile glial habitat in the body, next to the microbiome.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

In Hirschsprung disease the crest cells that make enteric neurons and glia fail to colonise the distal bowel, and this failed colonisation is the CAUSE of the aganglionic, glia-poor segment. In inflammatory bowel disease and gut dysmotility the enteric glial cell is a CASUALTY that becomes reactive and a contributor to barrier failure and inflammation.

There is growing evidence linking enteric glial dysfunction to Parkinson disease through the gut-brain axis, where it is best read at present as a contributor and a MARKER. Its selective vulnerability concentrates in the distal colon in developmental disease and at the mucosal interface in inflammation.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 25. OLFACTORY ENSHEATHING CELL (Olfactory Ensheathing Glial Cell of the First Cranial Nerve) (The Axon-Escorting Bridge Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The olfactory ensheathing cell derives from the olfactory placode and its associated neural crest, and it develops with the olfactory system, escorting olfactory sensory axons across the peripheral-to-central border. Its exact lineage attribution has been debated between placodal and neural crest origins; report this as not fully settled, with recent evidence favouring a substantial neural crest contribution.

Uniquely, it supports a system that regenerates for life: because olfactory sensory neurons die and are replaced every few weeks, the ensheathing cell must repeatedly guide brand-new axons through the skull. It therefore functions in a permanently open developmental corridor rather than a closed critical period.

The cells persist and are maintained through adulthood in step with this continuous turnover.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

The olfactory ensheathing cell is studied less as a disease agent than as a therapeutic tool: because it professionally spans the peripheral-central frontier, it is one of the most transplanted cells in spinal cord repair research, where the interest is in what it can do rather than a disorder it causes.

In anosmia and olfactory dysfunction its failure to support axon regrowth can be a contributor, so it may be a CAUSE or contributor in some olfactory loss, and its disturbance is a MARKER of olfactory pathway injury. A firm CAUSE role in a common named disease is not established. Its vulnerability is tied to the exposed, continuously regenerating olfactory epithelium it serves.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 26. PERINEURIAL GLIAL CELL (Perineurial Cell of the Nerve Fascicle Sheath) (The Fascicle-Fencing Barrier Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The perineurial cell forms the concentric, tight-junction-sealed sheath around each nerve fascicle, the outer wall of the blood-nerve barrier. Its lineage is genuinely mixed and not fully settled: long booked as mesenchymal fibroblasts, at least part of the perineurium is now assigned to a glial origin, and in fish the perineurium demonstrably arises from glial cells of the spinal cord itself, while in mammals a mesodermal or fibroblast contribution remains likely.

Because the lineage is unsettled across species, a single confident origin should not be stated; the honest position is a mixed and partly glial derivation whose proportions depend on the model. Its assembly accompanies fascicle formation during nerve development, and the barrier tightens perinatally in rodents (rodent).

The perineurium is a living, metabolising fence that can repair, not inert packaging.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

In perineurioma this cell is the CAUSE by cell of origin, a tumour defined by proliferating perineurial cells. When the perineurial barrier is breached by trauma, infection, or inflammation, its failure lets the protected endoneurial environment be disturbed, making it a contributor and a CASUALTY.

In inflammatory neuropathy its breakdown is a MARKER of barrier compromise. Its selective vulnerability is mechanical and inflammatory, at points where the fascicle sheath is stretched, compressed, or invaded.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 27. BOUNDARY CAP CELL (Boundary Cap Neural Crest Cell of the Spinal Root Entry and Exit Points) (The Frontier-Policing Gatekeeper Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Boundary cap cells are a transient neural crest population that clusters at the dorsal and ventral root entry and exit points, marked by Krox20 (Egr2) and by the boundary-cap-specific gene Prss56. They appear for a matter of weeks in the embryo and are gone in their original form by birth.

While present they enforce one-way traffic at the central-peripheral frontier, letting axons cross but keeping cell bodies on their proper side. As they stand down they donate their daughters to the periphery: boundary cap cells give rise to Schwann cell precursors of the roots, to satellite glial cells, and to a class of dorsal root ganglion neurons, mainly nociceptive afferents.

They are therefore a spent developmental progenitor, not a persisting adult cell, and are not replaced after their window closes.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Because boundary cap cells are transient, they are not implicated as a persisting agent in adult disease; their relevance is developmental. Their failure to police the frontier can permit ectopic migration and mispositioned motor neurons in the developing root, so in that developmental sense they can be a CAUSE of root-boundary malformation.

They are a source of nociceptive neurons and terminal glia of the skin, which links their derivatives to sensory function and to spinal-cord repair interest. In the adult they are best treated as a lineage of origin rather than a CAUSE, CASUALTY, or MARKER of a specific disorder, since honest data on a direct adult disease role are thin.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 28. CUTANEOUS (SKIN) GLIA, INCLUDING THE NOCICEPTIVE SCHWANN CELL (Specialised Terminal Glia of the Skin) (The Pain-Sensing Skin-Mesh Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Cutaneous glia, including the nociceptive Schwann cells described by Abdo and colleagues at the Karolinska Institute in 2019, are terminal glia of the Schwann family, neural-crest-derived and Sox10-lineage, that form a mesh at the border between dermis and epidermis and wrap the endings of pain fibres. Some skin terminal glia trace to boundary cap derivatives.

Because the type was defined only recently, its developmental schedule is not established; no honest birth date or maturation timescale can be given for the human nociceptive Schwann cell, and the finding rests largely on mouse experiments. What the mouse work shows is that driving these glia alone produces pain behaviour and silencing them raises mechanical pain thresholds, so they are themselves mechanosensitive partners in nociception.

Whether and how they are replaced through life in human skin is not yet characterised.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Because the discovery is recent, disease attribution is preliminary. The natural hypothesis, supported by the mouse physiology, is that these mechanosensitive glia contribute to mechanical pain and possibly to chronic pain states, which would make them a CAUSE or contributor, but firm human disease data do not yet exist and should not be overstated.

At present they are best treated as a newly recognised participant in nociception whose specific disease roles are still being worked out; classifying them confidently as CAUSE, CASUALTY, or MARKER for a named human disorder is premature. Their selective vulnerability profile is unknown.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 29. LAMELLAR CORPUSCLE CELLS OF MEISSNER AND PACINI (Lamellar Cells of the Meissner and Pacinian Corpuscles) (The Onion-Layer Touch-Filter Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: These lamellar cells are of neural crest origin by way of the Schwann cell lineage. The inner core lamellae are specialised (terminal) Schwann cells, and the outer capsule lamellae are modified perineurial cells, which are themselves a mesenchymal or neural-crest-derived fibroblast-like population. The corpuscle assembles around an ingrowing sensory axon: in rodent studies the presumptive inner core cells begin to accumulate as a Schwann cell sheath around the terminal at about embryonic day 19 to 20, then multiply their lamellae rapidly. The corpuscle continues to add lamellae and enlarge after birth into childhood, so maturation is postnatal, not complete at term.

The lamellae can be regenerated. After nerve injury, immature Schwann cells re-associate with the regrowing axon and rebuild the characteristic onion-bulb lamellar pattern, and the corpuscle's own extracellular matrix helps drive this reconstruction. No named transcription-factor code specific to lamellar-cell identity is well established, so this entry does not assert one. There is no classical critical period tied to these cells; the human touch system remains plastic in its central representations across life.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Pacinian and Meissner corpuscles and their lamellar cells are lost or thinned in diabetic and age-related peripheral neuropathy, where they are a CASUALTY of the dying-back of the sensory axons they wrap, contributing to loss of vibration sense and fine touch. In hereditary sensory neuropathies the same corpuscle loss is a downstream CASUALTY. Rare painful Pacinian corpuscle hyperplasia (Pacinian neuroma) of the digits, in which lamellar cells proliferate, is a setting where the lamellar compartment is closer to a CAUSE of local symptoms. Their selective vulnerability is that, as distal glial specialisations of the longest sensory axons, they fail early in length-dependent neuropathies.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 30. COCHLEAR SUPPORTING CELLS: DEITERS, PILLAR, HENSEN, BORDER, AND INNER PHALANGEAL CELLS (The Harp-String Scaffold Cells) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: These supporting cells arise from the otic placode, an ectodermal thickening that invaginates into the otic cup and closes into the otic vesicle (otocyst), the structure that generates nearly all inner-ear cell types. Around rodent embryonic day 12 the ventral otocyst is specified as a prosensory domain marked by the transcription factor SOX2 (SRY-box transcription factor 2), and within that domain FGF (fibroblast growth factor), Notch, and Wnt signalling partition cells into hair cells and their surrounding supporting cells. Lateral inhibition through Notch drives the SOX2-retaining cells to become the pillar, Deiters, Hensen, border, and inner phalangeal supporting cells rather than hair cells.

The organ of Corti matures around birth and into the early postnatal weeks in rodents; in humans the cochlea is functionally mature before term. These supporting cells are not meaningfully replaced in the adult mammal. The mammalian cochlea is post-mitotic and, unlike birds, does not regenerate lost hair cells, and the neonatal window of regenerative and transdifferentiation potential closes with maturity. This closure is itself the sensitive period that matters: SOX2-positive supporting cells in the neonatal cochlea can be coaxed toward hair-cell fate, but adult cells cannot without intervention.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: In sensorineural hearing loss from noise, ototoxic drugs (for example aminoglycoside antibiotics and cisplatin), and ageing (presbycusis), supporting cells are largely a CASUALTY that degenerates after the hair cells they cradle are lost, and their failure to regenerate is why human deafness is permanent. Because they are the reserve pool that could in principle become hair cells, their quiescence is a permissive factor in the irreversibility of loss rather than an initiating CAUSE. Their selective vulnerability is regional: the basal, high-frequency turn of the cochlea is damaged first by noise and ototoxins, so high-frequency hearing fails earliest.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 31. VESTIBULAR SUPPORTING CELLS (The Balance-Organ Nurse Cells) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: Like their cochlear cousins, vestibular supporting cells derive from the otic placode and otocyst, from SOX2-positive prosensory patches in the developing cristae and maculae, with hair-cell versus supporting-cell fate set by Notch-mediated lateral inhibition. They differentiate during embryonic and early postnatal life and mature around birth. Precise human birth-dating of these specific cells is not well established, so no exact human timeline is asserted here.

Unlike the cochlea, the mammalian vestibular sensory epithelia retain a limited, genuine capacity for hair-cell regeneration into adulthood, and supporting cells are the source of this modest turnover, acting as facultative progenitors that can re-enter the cell cycle or transdifferentiate at a low rate. This flicker of regeneration is much less than in non-mammals but is real and is the sensitive-period-like property distinguishing them from cochlear supporting cells.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: In aminoglycoside and cisplatin ototoxicity and in age-related vestibular decline (presbystasis), vestibular hair cells are lost and the supporting cells are a CASUALTY and partial survivor, and their incomplete regeneration is why balance recovers only partially. In Meniere disease, endolymphatic hydrops disturbs the ionic milieu these cells help maintain, so they are more MARKER and CASUALTY than CAUSE. Their otoconia-supporting role links them to benign paroxysmal positional vertigo, where dislodged crystals cause vertigo, though the fault there is mechanical displacement rather than supporting-cell malfunction.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 32. OLFACTORY SUSTENTACULAR CELL (The Nose's Bodyguard Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The olfactory sustentacular cell arises from the olfactory placode-derived olfactory epithelium and, in the mature epithelium, is continuously regenerated from the basal stem cells of Chapter 33, chiefly the globose basal cells and, as a reserve, the horizontal basal cells. It is therefore one of the cells that the lifelong regenerative programme of the nose keeps replacing, and it is generated well after birth throughout life. No sustentacular-cell-specific master transcription factor is firmly established, so none is asserted here.

Because the olfactory epithelium renews itself continuously, there is no fixed critical period for these cells; the sensitive dependency is that when basal stem cells are damaged, sustentacular replacement fails.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The sustentacular cell is the principal target of severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2) in the olfactory epithelium, because it, not the olfactory sensory neuron, richly expresses the viral entry factors angiotensin-converting enzyme 2 (ACE2) and TMPRSS2 (transmembrane serine protease 2). Here it is the CAUSE of the anosmia (loss of smell) of coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19): its infection and damage disrupt the support and ionic environment the smell neurons need, and downstream disruption of basal stem cells can make anosmia persistent. In toxic and inflammatory rhinitis it is a CASUALTY of inhaled injury. Its selective vulnerability is its position at the exposed apical surface facing inhaled air, chemicals, and pathogens.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 33. OLFACTORY HORIZONTAL AND GLOBOSE BASAL CELLS (The Smell-Line Stem Cells) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: Both basal cell populations arise from the olfactory placode-derived neuroepithelium. The globose basal cells are the active, everyday progenitors and express the transcription factors ASCL1 (achaete-scute family basic helix-loop-helix transcription factor 1, also called MASH1) and, downstream, NEUROG1 and NEUROD1 as they commit to the neuronal lineage. The horizontal basal cells are a quiescent reserve marked by TP63 (tumour protein p63) and cytokeratins, activated to restore the epithelium only after severe injury. These cells operate throughout life, making the olfactory epithelium the one human site where sensory neurons are continuously regenerated after birth.

There is no closing critical period; the defining property is lifelong self-renewal. The horizontal basal cells sit dormant until globose progenitors are exhausted or the epithelium is badly damaged, then wake to rebuild every cell type including sustentacular cells and neurons.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: In chronic and repeated olfactory injury, in ageing, and after severe viral infection including SARS-CoV-2, these stem cells can be exhausted or diverted into a wound-healing, non-neuronal programme, and this failure of the stem compartment is a CAUSE of persistent anosmia rather than the transient anosmia of simple neuronal loss. They are the presumed cell of origin of some olfactory neuroblastoma (esthesioneuroblastoma), where they are a CAUSE. Their selective vulnerability is that heavy or repeated damage pushes the reserve horizontal basal cells toward scar-forming rather than neurogenic fates.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 34. TASTE BUD TYPE I CELL (The Taste-Bud Janitor Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The type I taste cell is renewed throughout life from SOX2-positive basal keratinocyte progenitors of the tongue epithelium, which express the cytokeratins K5 and K14. Lineage tracing in rodents shows that Sonic hedgehog (SHH)-expressing postmitotic precursors give rise to all three taste-cell types and become type I cells most frequently, type II less often, and type III least often. Onset of taste-bud cell renewal begins at birth in rodents and coincides with a shift in SHH function. Individual taste cells turn over on a timescale of roughly one to two weeks in rodents, so these cells are continuously generated after birth.

There is no fixed critical period; the type I cell is defined by constant replacement, and SOX2 is required both to build taste buds developmentally and to maintain their turnover.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Type I cells are damaged in taste disturbance (dysgeusia and ageusia) from radiation to the head and neck, from chemotherapy, and from ageing, where they are a CASUALTY of injury to the rapidly dividing progenitor pool that feeds them. Because they buffer potassium and clear transmitter, their loss can also distort the signalling of surviving receptor cells, giving them a partial CAUSE role in qualitative taste distortion. Their selective vulnerability follows from their high turnover: any insult that suppresses epithelial stem-cell division (radiation, cytotoxic drugs) strikes them early.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 35. RETINAL PIGMENT EPITHELIAL CELL (The Photoreceptor's Round-the-Clock Valet Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The retinal pigment epithelial cell arises from the neuroepithelium of the optic vesicle: the dorsal, outer wall of the optic cup is specified as pigment epithelium rather than neural retina. Its specification depends on the transcription factors MITF (melanocyte inducing transcription factor) and OTX2 (orthodenticle homeobox 2), acting with PAX6 (paired box 6), LHX2 (LIM homeobox 2), and VAX factors, while FGF signalling and VSX2 (visual system homeobox 2) drive the neighbouring domain toward neural retina instead. Loss of OTX or MITF levels causes the pigment epithelium to transdifferentiate into retina, showing how finely balanced the fate choice is.

The cells differentiate and pigment early in fetal life and are essentially not replaced in the adult human; the layer is a largely post-mitotic monolayer maintained by longevity rather than turnover. There is no substantial adult regeneration in humans, which is central to why its diseases are progressive.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The retinal pigment epithelial cell is central to age-related macular degeneration (AMD), where its dysfunction and death are an early and driving event, making it a CAUSE as well as a MARKER through the drusen deposits that accumulate beneath it. Common genetic risk for AMD acts partly by altering the LHX2 to OTX2 regulatory module in these cells. It is the primary defective cell in many inherited retinal dystrophies of the visual cycle, for example those caused by mutation of RPE65 (retinoid isomerohydrolase), where it is a CAUSE of Leber congenital amaurosis and retinitis pigmentosa variants. Its selective vulnerability is metabolic overload: the macular cells that phagocytose the densest photoreceptors and handle the most oxidative stress fail first.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 36. RADIAL GLIAL CELL (Apical or Ventricular Radial Glia) (The Ladder-and-Loom Founder Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The apical radial glial cell arises from the neuroepithelium (neural-tube ectoderm) as neuroepithelial cells transform into radial glia at the onset of neurogenesis. Its identity depends on PAX6 (paired box 6), SOX2 (SRY-box transcription factor 2), and, regionally, EMX2 (empty spiracles homeobox 2), with NOTCH signalling maintaining the progenitor state and GFAP-lineage and NESTIN markers appearing as it matures. In rodents, cortical neurogenesis from radial glia runs from about embryonic day 11 to 12 through embryonic day 18, after which the population switches to gliogenesis, producing astrocytes and oligodendrocytes.

Radial glia largely disappear as such after development: most transform into astrocytes, ependymal cells, and the adult stem cells of Chapters 38 and 39, so the embryonic population is not replaced but converted. Its work defines the developmental critical windows of cortical assembly, since it lays down neurons in an inside-first order and provides the migration rail; disruption during this window produces lifelong malformation.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Radial glial dysfunction is a CAUSE of cortical malformations: defects of its migration scaffold and division underlie lissencephaly (smooth brain), periventricular heterotopia, and microcephaly, and mutations in genes such as those of the tubulin and centrosome machinery act through it. In focal cortical dysplasia and some childhood epilepsies, faulty radial glial output is a CAUSE. Radial-glia-like cells are also the presumed cell of origin of some gliomas. Its selective vulnerability is temporal: because it is the founder cell, an insult during its active window (for example congenital infection or a mutation expressed in progenitors) is amplified across every descendant lineage.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 37. OUTER (BASAL) RADIAL GLIAL CELL (Outer Subventricular Zone Radial Glia) (The Untethered Amplifier Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The outer (basal) radial glial cell derives from apical radial glia and populates the outer subventricular zone, a germinal layer that is thin in rodents but massively expanded in humans and other primates. It retains a basal fibre toward the pia but loses the apical ventricular contact, and it shares the PAX6 and SOX2 progenitor identity of its parent while dividing repeatedly to amplify neuron number. Sustained PAX6 expression is one factor that can generate primate-like basal radial glia even in the mouse cortex. Before dividing it performs mitotic somal translocation, a rapid outward hop of the cell body along its own fibre.

It is an embryonic and fetal population, most active during the neurogenic window of the second trimester in humans, and it is not retained or replaced in the adult brain. Its expansion is one of the leading cellular explanations for the evolutionary enlargement and folding (gyrification) of the human neocortex.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Because it drives the amplification of cortical neuron number, its impairment is a CAUSE of microcephaly and of disorders of cortical folding such as lissencephaly and polymicrogyria, and it is heavily implicated in the reduced brain size seen with congenital Zika virus infection, which targets neural progenitors including this population. Its selective vulnerability is that any agent striking dividing progenitors during the mid-gestation expansion window (viral infection, radiation, mutation) disproportionately shrinks and disorganises the cortex, because this cell is the multiplier of the whole sheet.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 38. ADULT NEURAL STEM CELL OF THE VENTRICULAR-SUBVENTRICULAR ZONE, THE B1 CELL (Astrocytic Ventricle-Wall Stem Cell) (The Sleeping Reserve Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The B1 cell is the direct descendant of embryonic radial glia, set aside during development and retained along the lateral ventricle wall as an astrocyte-like stem cell expressing GFAP (glial fibrillary acidic protein), NESTIN, and SOX2. It keeps the radial body plan, touching the cerebrospinal fluid with an apical process bearing a primary cilium and contacting blood vessels with a basal process. In rodents it feeds a lifelong rostral migratory stream of new neurons to the olfactory bulb.

In humans this neurogenic output is generated after birth but collapses in the first year or two of life, so the adult human B1 cell functions overwhelmingly as a quiescent reserve rather than an active neuron factory. It is not readily replaced once lost, and reawakening it is a central, unrealised goal of regenerative neuroscience. There is no ongoing critical period in the adult human because the productive window has largely closed by early childhood.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The V-SVZ (ventricular-subventricular zone) B1 lineage is a candidate cell of origin for glioblastoma, and mutations arising in these cells can migrate to form tumours distant from the ventricle, so here it is a CAUSE. After stroke and injury it mounts a limited, largely insufficient regenerative response, in which it is a would-be repairer rather than a driver of disease. Its selective vulnerability is niche dependence: because its activity is held in check by cerebrospinal-fluid and vascular signals, disturbance of the niche (ageing, hydrocephalus, inflammation) further suppresses an already dormant cell.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 39. ADULT HIPPOCAMPAL RADIAL GLIA-LIKE CELL, THE TYPE 1 CELL (Subgranular Zone Stem Cell) (The Contested Memory-Seed Cell) (COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The Type 1 cell is the radial-glia-like stem cell of the subgranular zone at the base of the dentate gyrus, a descendant of developmental radial glia. It is quiescent and astrocyte-like, with a radial process crossing the granule cell layer, and it expresses GFAP (glial fibrillary acidic protein), NESTIN, and SOX2, with SOX2 and the pro-neural factor ASCL1 governing its activation. In rodents it demonstrably produces new dentate granule neurons throughout life through type 2 transit-amplifying cells, and these young granule neurons pass through a transiently hyper-excitable stage before integrating.

Whether this process persists in the adult human is one of the sharpest unsettled controversies in neuroscience: the load-bearing claim of ongoing adult human hippocampal neurogenesis is not settled. Sorrells and colleagues (2018) found neurogenesis dropping to undetectable levels in childhood, while Boldrini and colleagues (2018) reported lifelong new neurons in the same region, with the disagreement traced partly to tissue fixation and marker methods. Because this is unresolved, the honest position is that no confident human birth rate can be stated, and the cell earns its cognitive tag only if adult human neurogenesis is real. In rodents the young-neuron maturation window (roughly four to six weeks to full integration) is a genuine sensitive period for their contribution to memory.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: To the degree the process is real, failure of this lineage is implicated in depression, in age-related and stress-related memory decline, and in temporal lobe epilepsy, where in rodents newborn granule neurons integrate aberrantly and can worsen seizures, making the cell a possible CAUSE in that setting. In mood disorders it is more plausibly a CASUALTY and MARKER, since chronic stress and glucocorticoids suppress it and antidepressants and exercise raise it in rodents. Given the human controversy, all human disease attributions here are provisional. Its selective vulnerability in rodents is to glucocorticoid excess, inflammation, and ageing.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 40. TRANSIT-AMPLIFYING PROGENITOR AND NEUROBLAST POPULATIONS (Type C Cells and Type A Neuroblasts) (The Assembly-Line Middle Cells) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: These are the intermediate stages of adult and developmental neurogenesis: the transit-amplifying cells (type C in the ventricular-subventricular zone, type 2 in the hippocampal subgranular zone) and the neuroblasts (type A) they produce. They descend from the stem cells of Chapters 38 and 39 and are marked by the pro-neural transcription factors ASCL1 (in transit-amplifying cells) and DCX (doublecortin) and NEUROD1 (in neuroblasts). By definition they are generated after birth wherever adult neurogenesis occurs, but none is a permanent resident; each is a short-lived, rapidly dividing or migrating stage, not a fixed cell type.

They are continuously produced from the stem pool in rodents and are replaced only as long as the parent stem cell is active. In adult humans, following the collapse of the ventricular-subventricular output after infancy and the unsettled status of hippocampal neurogenesis, the abundance of these cells in the adult human brain is itself uncertain, and this entry does not assert a human production rate.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Because they are the most proliferative cells of the lineage, transit-amplifying cells are strong candidate cells of origin for gliomas and other central-nervous-system tumours, where they would be a CAUSE. Migrating neuroblasts attempt limited repair after stroke, redirecting toward injury in rodents, a would-be reparative rather than pathogenic role.
