Their selective vulnerability is intrinsic to their fast cycling: they are the stage most sensitive to radiation, cytotoxic chemotherapy, and inflammation, which is one reason cranial radiation impairs neurogenesis.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 41. BRAIN MICROVASCULAR ENDOTHELIAL CELL (Continuous Blood-Brain Barrier Endothelium) (The Gatekeeper Wall Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The brain microvascular endothelial cell is of mesodermal origin, arising from angioblasts that invade the neural tube by angiogenic sprouting from the surrounding perineural vascular plexus during embryonic development. It is not born with barrier properties: the neural environment induces them, chiefly through Wnt/beta-catenin signalling from the neural tissue, which switches on barrier genes and suppresses the fenestrations and transcytosis of ordinary endothelium. Pericyte recruitment and later astrocyte contact are required to mature and maintain the barrier.

Barrier maturation continues across late gestation and early postnatal life. In the healthy adult these endothelial cells turn over slowly but can be replaced and can proliferate during angiogenesis and repair. The developmental induction of the barrier is a sensitive process; disruption of Wnt or pericyte signals during it produces a leaky barrier.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Endothelial barrier breakdown is a shared mechanism across stroke, multiple sclerosis, brain infection, and vascular dementia. In ischaemic stroke it is largely a CASUALTY whose breakdown then worsens oedema, and in multiple sclerosis its junctional failure permits immune-cell entry, a contributing CAUSE of lesion formation. It is the physical obstacle that causes most central-nervous-system drug delivery to fail. In cerebral cavernous malformation, endothelial mutations are a direct CAUSE. Its selective vulnerability is that it depends on continuous support from pericytes and astrocytes, so loss of those partners, or intense inflammation and hypoxia, opens the barrier.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 42. PERICYTE (Mural Cell of the Capillary) (The Vessel-Hugging Regulator Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: Brain pericytes have a dual embryonic origin: forebrain pericytes come from the cranial neural crest, while pericytes of the midbrain, hindbrain, and spinal cord come from mesoderm. They are recruited onto nascent vessels by PDGF-BB (platelet-derived growth factor BB) signalling to the receptor PDGFRB (platelet-derived growth factor receptor beta), and they express markers such as PDGFRB, NG2 (neuron-glial antigen 2), and CD13. They are essential during embryonic and early postnatal life for inducing and sealing the blood-brain barrier.

Pericytes persist in the adult and can proliferate to some extent, but they are progressively lost with age and in disease, and the brain is not efficient at replacing them. There is a developmental window in which pericyte coverage must be established for a competent barrier.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Pericyte degeneration is an early and causal event in Alzheimer's disease and in cerebral small-vessel (white-matter) disease, where their loss opens the barrier, reduces clearance of amyloid-beta, and stiffens and constricts capillaries, making the pericyte a CAUSE and an early MARKER. In cerebral autosomal dominant arteriopathy with subcortical infarcts and leukoencephalopathy (CADASIL), NOTCH3 mutation acts on mural cells and the pericyte is both CAUSE and CASUALTY. After ischaemia, pericytes can constrict and die in rigor, trapping blood in capillaries (the no-reflow phenomenon), a CAUSE of persistent hypoperfusion. Their selective vulnerability is high metabolic and oxidative sensitivity; the brain carries more pericytes per vessel than other organs, so their failure is felt acutely.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 43. VASCULAR SMOOTH MUSCLE CELL OF THE BRAIN VESSELS (Arteriolar Myocyte) (The Blood-Summoning Muscle Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The vascular smooth muscle cells of the brain arteries share the mural-cell dual origin: those of the forebrain vessels derive from cranial neural crest, and those of more caudal vessels from mesoderm. They differentiate onto the arterial wall through PDGF and Notch and transforming-growth-factor-beta signalling, expressing contractile markers such as alpha smooth muscle actin (ACTA2) and myosin heavy chain. They mature onto the resistance arterioles during late gestation and early postnatal life as the cerebral circulation is built.

They persist in the adult and retain some capacity to proliferate and to switch between contractile and synthetic states, but they are not rapidly renewed. Their contractile tone sets resting cerebral blood flow and its second-by-second adjustment to neural activity.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: These cells are the primary defective cell in CADASIL (cerebral autosomal dominant arteriopathy with subcortical infarcts and leukoencephalopathy), caused by NOTCH3 mutation, where their degeneration is a CAUSE of small-vessel stroke and vascular dementia. In hypertensive small-vessel disease and cerebral amyloid angiopathy they are a CAUSE and CASUALTY, as amyloid-beta deposits in and around them and impairs their contraction. Mutations in ACTA2 cause a syndrome of moyamoya-like cerebrovascular disease, a direct CAUSE. Their selective vulnerability lies in the deep penetrating arterioles supplying subcortical white matter, which fail earliest in small-vessel disease.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 44. PERIVASCULAR FIBROBLAST (Fibroblast of the Vascular Sleeve) (The Brain's Plumbing Fibroblast Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The perivascular fibroblast is a recently defined stromal cell, distinguished from pericytes and smooth muscle by single-cell sequencing, that lines the perivascular (Virchow-Robin) spaces around larger penetrating vessels. Its developmental lineage is not fully settled; it is a mesenchymal or fibroblast-lineage cell related to meningeal fibroblasts and is thought to enter the brain along vessels during development, but a clean transcription-factor code and human birth-dating are not established, so none is asserted here.

It persists in the adult and expands in disease. Because it was only recently characterised, its normal turnover and replacement are not well quantified; this entry is deliberately short to reflect that its developmental biology remains thin.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Perivascular fibroblasts proliferate and lay down fibrotic scar after brain and spinal cord injury and in neurodegeneration, and fibroblast-derived scarring is increasingly seen as a CAUSE of impaired regeneration and of the stiffening of perivascular spaces. Enlarged perivascular spaces on imaging are a MARKER of small-vessel disease and of impaired glymphatic (cerebrospinal-fluid-driven waste) clearance, a system these cells help build. In amyotrophic lateral sclerosis and other conditions they have been implicated in barrier and matrix changes. Their exact causal weight is still being worked out.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 45. MENINGEAL FIBROBLAST POPULATIONS: DURAL, ARACHNOID, AND PIAL CELLS (Fibroblasts of the Three Coverings) (The Three-Cloak Barrier Cells) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The meninges have a mixed embryonic origin that varies by layer and by brain region. The meninges over the forebrain derive largely from cranial neural crest, whereas those over the posterior brain and spinal cord derive substantially from mesoderm; the dura, arachnoid, and pia each mature into a molecularly distinct fibroblast type. Development is guided by signalling between the meninges and the underlying brain, including retinoic acid and bone-morphogenetic-protein signals that pattern the growing cortex and its vasculature. The arachnoid barrier cell layer acquires its tight junctions during this period to form the meningeal blood-cerebrospinal-fluid barrier.

They differentiate prenatally and persist through life, retaining fibroblast proliferative capacity for repair. A precise single set of specifying transcription factors is not cleanly established across all three layers, so none is asserted here beyond the neural-crest and mesodermal origins.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Arachnoid-lineage cells are the cell of origin of meningioma, the most common primary intracranial tumour, where they are the CAUSE. Meningeal fibroblasts drive fibrotic scarring after subarachnoid haemorrhage, meningitis, and traumatic injury, which can obstruct cerebrospinal-fluid flow and cause hydrocephalus, a CAUSE in that setting. In meningitis they are a CASUALTY of infection and inflammation. Their barrier layer, when breached, is a route of infection spread. Their selective vulnerability tracks their exposure at the fluid and vascular borders of the brain.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 46. ARACHNOID CAP AND GRANULATION CELLS (Cells of the Arachnoid Villi) (The One-Way Drain Cells) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: Arachnoid cap and granulation cells are specialised arachnoid cells and share the meningeal fibroblast lineage of Chapter 45, of mixed neural-crest and mesodermal origin depending on region. The arachnoid granulations themselves (also called arachnoid villi or Pacchionian bodies) are not prominent at birth; they enlarge and become numerous through childhood as intracranial venous pressure and cerebrospinal-fluid production rise, so this structure matures substantially after birth. A specific transcription-factor programme for cap cells is not established, so none is asserted here.

The cells persist through life and retain fibroblast proliferative capacity, which is relevant to their tumour biology.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Arachnoid cap cells are the cell of origin of meningioma, where their whorled clusters give rise to the tumour and they are the CAUSE. When granulation function is impaired, cerebrospinal-fluid outflow falls and pressure rises, so these cells are implicated in communicating hydrocephalus and in idiopathic intracranial hypertension, where reduced outflow makes them a contributing CAUSE or a CASUALTY of upstream venous or inflammatory disease. Their selective vulnerability is that any process scarring or occluding the villi (past haemorrhage, meningitis) blocks the drain.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 47. CHOROID PLEXUS STROMAL CELLS: FIBROBLASTS AND PERICYTES OF THE PLEXUS (Core Cells of the Fluid Factory) (The Leaky-Vessel Support Cells) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The stromal cells of the choroid plexus are mesenchymal: fibroblasts and pericytes filling the core of each villus beneath the secretory epithelium. The choroid plexus develops early, as the epithelium (of neuroepithelial origin) folds into the ventricles and mesenchyme carrying fenestrated capillaries invades the core; the pericytes are recruited by PDGF-BB to PDGFRB signalling as elsewhere. A distinct transcription-factor identity for the plexus stromal cells specifically is not well established, so none is asserted here.

They persist through life as structural support. This is a thinly studied population, and this entry is short accordingly.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: As support cells for a deliberately leaky, fenestrated vascular bed, they are involved in inflammation and infection of the choroid plexus and in the altered fluid production and immune trafficking seen in ageing and in neuroinflammatory disease, where they are largely a CASUALTY and a facilitating element rather than a primary CAUSE. Choroid plexus tumours arise chiefly from the epithelium rather than the stroma, so the stromal cells are not the usual tumour origin here. Firm, cell-specific disease attributions are limited by how little these cells have been studied.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 48. BORDER-ASSOCIATED MACROPHAGES (Perivascular, Meningeal, and Choroid Plexus Macrophages) (The Border-Guard Engulfing Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: Border-associated macrophages are among the oldest cells in the body by lineage, seeded from the embryonic yolk sac in the first weeks of development, before definitive haematopoiesis, and they depend on the transcription factor PU.1 and on CSF1R (colony-stimulating factor 1 receptor) signalling like other tissue macrophages. They take up residence at the central-nervous-system borders (perivascular spaces, meninges, and choroid plexus stroma) before the barriers close. Unlike microglia, some border populations, especially at the choroid plexus and dura, are partly replenished from blood-derived monocytes across life, while others self-renew locally.

They are generated before birth and, for the self-renewing populations, maintained by local proliferation thereafter. Their prenatal seeding is their defining developmental feature.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Border-associated macrophages regulate perivascular fluid drainage, and their dysfunction is implicated in impaired clearance of amyloid-beta in Alzheimer's disease and in cerebral amyloid angiopathy, where they act as both would-be clearer and, when overwhelmed, a contributor, so they are CAUSE and CASUALTY depending on stage. In stroke and neuroinflammation they shape the immune response and can worsen or limit injury. They are a MARKER of border inflammation. Their selective vulnerability is functional exhaustion: chronic amyloid or debris load can impair their clearance capacity.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 49. EPIPLEXUS (KOLMER) CELL (The Fluid-Riding Watchman Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The epiplexus (Kolmer) cell is a specialised border-associated macrophage stationed on the apical, cerebrospinal-fluid-facing surface of the choroid plexus epithelium. Like its border-macrophage relatives it is of yolk-sac origin, seeded prenatally, and depends on CSF1R signalling; the apical choroid-plexus population is also partly maintained by blood-derived cells across life. It takes its post during fetal development as the choroid plexus forms.

It is generated before birth and partly replenished thereafter. This is a small, historically neglected population (first drawn by Walter Kolmer in 1921), and this entry is short to match.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: As the first responder facing the ventricular fluid, the epiplexus cell reacts to intraventricular haemorrhage, infection, and inflammation, engulfing blood products and debris, where it is a responder and MARKER of ventricular insult rather than a primary CAUSE. Its numbers and activation rise in neuroinflammatory and infectious states. Firm, cell-specific causal disease links are limited by how little it has been studied in isolation.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 50. MENINGEAL LYMPHOCYTES, DENDRITIC CELLS, AND MAST CELLS (The Frontier Council Cells) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: These are not brain-derived cells. The T and B lymphocytes and dendritic cells arise from haematopoietic stem cells in the bone marrow (lymphocytes maturing in the thymus and bone marrow), and mast cells arise from bone-marrow progenitors that mature in tissues; all reach the meninges from the circulation and the associated lymphatics. They populate the meninges progressively after birth as the adaptive immune system develops, and they are continuously replaced from the circulation throughout life. No single neural transcription-factor code applies, since these are conventional immune lineages.

They are generated and renewed after birth by definition, being turned-over immune cells. Their meningeal residence and the meningeal lymphatic vessels that drain to the deep cervical lymph nodes are a comparatively recent discovery.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Meningeal immune cells drive neuroinflammatory disease: T lymphocytes are central to multiple sclerosis and its meningeal follicle-like structures, where they are a CAUSE of cortical damage, and dendritic cells present antigen to launch such responses. In meningitis they are responders and CASUALTIES of infection. Mast cells contribute to migraine and neuroinflammation through histamine and other mediators, a partial CAUSE. A frontier finding is that meningeal T-cell signals (for example interferon-gamma and interleukin-4) modulate mood, sociability, and learning in the underlying brain, blurring the boundary of the non-cognitive tag. Their vulnerability and behaviour follow ordinary immune regulation rather than a neural selective profile.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 51. SOMATOTROPE (Growth-Hormone Cell of the Anterior Pituitary) (The Body-Building Broadcast Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The somatotrope arises from Rathke's pouch, an invagination of oral (surface) ectoderm, from PROP1 (PROP paired-like homeobox 1)-expressing progenitors, and it belongs to the PIT1 lineage, requiring the transcription factor POU1F1 (POU class 1 homeobox 1, also called PIT1) for its maturation, alongside its sibling lactotropes and thyrotropes. In rodents, somatotropes differentiate at about embryonic day 15.5 to 16, after the corticotropes; in humans growth-hormone-producing cells appear in the first trimester. Beta-catenin acting with PROP1 helps specify this PIT1 lineage.

The somatotrope is the most numerous hormone cell of the anterior pituitary. It is generated mainly prenatally, and the adult pituitary retains SOX2-positive stem cells that can produce new endocrine cells at a low rate, so limited replacement is possible. Growth hormone is released in pulses that peak in the first hours of deep sleep, and the childhood-to-puberty window is the critical period for its skeletal-growth action.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The somatotrope is the CAUSE of acromegaly and of pituitary gigantism when a somatotroph adenoma oversecretes growth hormone; somatotroph adenomas are roughly 10 to 15 percent of pituitary adenomas. Its failure, from POU1F1 or PROP1 mutation or from pituitary damage, is a CAUSE of growth-hormone deficiency and short stature, and PROP1 mutation causes combined deficiency of the whole PIT1 lineage. It is a CASUALTY in hypopituitarism from tumour compression, surgery, radiation, or Sheehan syndrome (postpartum pituitary infarction). Its selective vulnerability includes being among the cells lost early in pituitary apoplexy and compression.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 52. LACTOTROPE (Prolactin Cell of the Anterior Pituitary) (The Milk-Command Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The lactotrope arises from Rathke's pouch (oral ectoderm) via PROP1-expressing progenitors and is part of the POU1F1 (PIT1) lineage. It is the last of the anterior-pituitary cell types to appear, differentiating late in gestation and expanding substantially after birth; many lactotropes arise postnatally, including by transdifferentiation from somatotropes (mammosomatotropes), and the population enlarges markedly under oestrogen, especially in pregnancy. In rodents lactotrope numbers rise sharply in the perinatal and postnatal period.

It is therefore a cell that is notably generated after birth and that remodels with reproductive state; the adult SOX2-positive pituitary stem cells and somatotrope-to-lactotrope conversion allow ongoing replacement and expansion. Its productive critical period is functionally lactation, when the dopamine brake is lifted.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The lactotrope is the CAUSE of prolactinoma, the most common hormone-secreting pituitary adenoma (roughly a quarter to two-fifths of secreting adenomas), producing hyperprolactinaemia with infertility, loss of menstruation, and inappropriate milk production. Because it is under tonic dopamine inhibition, loss of that brake (pituitary stalk compression by any mass, or dopamine-blocking drugs) raises prolactin, so it is also a MARKER of stalk disruption (the stalk-effect). It is a CASUALTY in hypopituitarism. Its selective vulnerability is its dependence on the dopaminergic brake, which makes its output rise with almost any interference with the stalk.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 53. THYROTROPE (Thyroid-Stimulating-Hormone Cell of the Anterior Pituitary) (The Metabolic-Thermostat Relay Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The thyrotrope arises from Rathke's pouch (oral ectoderm) via PROP1-expressing progenitors and is part of the POU1F1 (PIT1) lineage, additionally requiring the transcription factor GATA2 (GATA binding protein 2) for its specification. In rodents thyrotropes differentiate around embryonic day 15.5; in humans thyroid-stimulating-hormone cells appear in the first trimester and the fetal thyroid axis matures across the second and third trimesters. It is a rare cell, only about one in twenty of the anterior-pituitary hormone cells.

It is generated chiefly prenatally, with limited adult replacement from pituitary stem cells. There is a genuine developmental critical period downstream of it: adequate thyroid hormone in fetal and early postnatal life is essential for brain development, which is why congenital hypothyroidism is screened at birth.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Thyrotrope failure, from POU1F1, PROP1, or thyroid-stimulating-hormone-beta mutation or from pituitary damage, is a CAUSE of central (secondary) hypothyroidism. Thyrotroph adenomas exist but are rare (under one percent of pituitary adenomas) and are a CAUSE of central hyperthyroidism. In primary hypothyroidism the thyrotrope enlarges and oversecretes because the negative feedback is lost, so its high output is a MARKER of a failing thyroid rather than a primary fault. It is a CASUALTY in hypopituitarism. Its selective vulnerability is unremarkable relative to the other pituitary cells.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 54. GONADOTROPE (Gonad-Stimulating Cell of the Anterior Pituitary) (The Reproductive-Rhythm Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The gonadotrope arises from Rathke's pouch (oral ectoderm) via PROP1-expressing progenitors, but unlike the PIT1 lineage it depends on the transcription factors SF1 (steroidogenic factor 1, also called NR5A1) and GATA2, with EGR1 (early growth response 1) needed for luteinising-hormone expression. In rodents gonadotropes differentiate around embryonic day 16.5 to 17; in humans they appear in the first trimester. They mature functionally under the pulsed drive of gonadotropin-releasing hormone from the hypothalamus, and the pulse frequency biases them toward luteinising or follicle-stimulating hormone.

Gonadotropes are generated prenatally, then pass through a quiet childhood and are reactivated at puberty, so puberty is their principal critical period, driven by the awakening of the gonadotropin-releasing-hormone pulse generator. Adult pituitary stem cells allow limited replacement.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Gonadotrope failure is a CAUSE of hypogonadotropic hypogonadism and infertility; when it stems from failed gonadotropin-releasing-hormone neuron migration with anosmia it is Kallmann syndrome, though there the primary fault is upstream and the gonadotrope is a CASUALTY of missing drive. Most clinically non-functioning pituitary macroadenomas are of gonadotrope origin, where the cell is the CAUSE of a mass that compresses neighbours even without a clinical hormone syndrome. In the postmenopausal or castrate state it shows characteristic signet-ring vacuolation, a MARKER of lost gonadal feedback. It is a CASUALTY in hypopituitarism.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 55. CORTICOTROPE (Adrenocorticotropic-Hormone Cell of the Anterior Pituitary) (The Stress-Axis Trigger Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The corticotrope arises from Rathke's pouch (oral ectoderm), and it is the first anterior-pituitary hormone cell to differentiate, appearing in rodents around embryonic day 12.5 to 13.5 and in humans in the first trimester (about the seventh week). Its identity depends on the transcription factor TPIT (T-box pituitary transcription factor, also called TBX19), acting with PITX1 and NEUROD1, and it lies outside the POU1F1 (PIT1) lineage. It carves adrenocorticotropic hormone out of the larger precursor pro-opiomelanocortin (POMC).

It is generated chiefly prenatally, with limited adult replacement from pituitary stem cells. There is no simple critical period, but the fetal hypothalamic-pituitary-adrenal axis is programmable by prenatal stress, with lasting effects on later stress reactivity.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The corticotrope is the CAUSE of Cushing disease when a corticotroph adenoma oversecretes adrenocorticotropic hormone; corticotroph adenomas are roughly ten percent of pituitary adenomas. TPIT mutation is a CAUSE of isolated adrenocorticotropic-hormone deficiency and neonatal adrenal crisis. After removal of a cortisol-secreting adrenal tumour, corticotropes that were suppressed can expand and pigment (Nelson syndrome), a CAUSE of an aggressive tumour. It is a CASUALTY in hypopituitarism, and its loss is the most dangerous, since secondary adrenal insufficiency can be fatal. Its selective vulnerability is that it can be silently suppressed by exogenous steroids, leaving the axis unable to respond to stress.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 56. MELANOTROPE OF THE INTERMEDIATE LOBE (Melanocyte-Stimulating-Hormone Cell) (The Vestigial Twilight Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The melanotrope arises from Rathke's pouch (oral ectoderm), from the same PROP1-derived progenitors, and forms the intermediate lobe between the anterior and posterior pituitary. It is a cousin of the corticotrope and shares TPIT (TBX19) dependence, processing the same precursor pro-opiomelanocortin, but toward melanocyte-stimulating hormone rather than adrenocorticotropic hormone; its specification also involves the transcription factor PAX7 (paired box 7), which distinguishes the intermediate-lobe fate, and HES1 is required for melanotrope specification. In rodents melanotropes differentiate around embryonic day 14.5 to 16.5.

In the adult human the intermediate lobe is rudimentary, so this cell is largely vestigial, present as scattered cells and cysts, though it is active in the human fetus. Its main activity is in fetal life, so its functional window is essentially prenatal in humans.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Because the human intermediate lobe is vestigial, this cell is rarely a primary cause of human disease, and this entry is short accordingly. In other species and occasionally in humans it can give rise to intermediate-lobe adenomas; in dogs and horses corticotroph-melanotrope tumours of the intermediate lobe cause a Cushing-like disease, where the cell is a CAUSE, but the human equivalent is uncommon. In the human its principal disease relevance is as a MARKER of pro-opiomelanocortin processing, and firm human causal attributions are limited by its vestigial status.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 57. FOLLICULOSTELLATE CELL (Sustentacular Cell of the Anterior Pituitary) (The Gland-Wide Wave Relay Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The folliculostellate cell is the star-shaped, non-hormonal supporting cell of the anterior pituitary, and its developmental origin is genuinely unsettled: it has been proposed to arise from Rathke's pouch epithelium alongside the endocrine cells, and alternatively to be an immigrant of neural-crest or bone-marrow (immune) origin, and current evidence does not decisively resolve this. Because the lineage of this cell is not settled, no single origin or transcription-factor code is asserted here; what would settle it is definitive lineage tracing in a mammalian model.

It expresses the marker protein S100 and behaves like astroglia, forming a gap-junction-coupled network that relays calcium waves across the gland. The adult pituitary's SOX2-positive stem-cell niche is associated with folliculostellate-like cells, so a stem or progenitor relationship is likely but not fully defined, and its rate of postnatal generation and replacement is not precisely established.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Folliculostellate cells modulate hormone output through paracrine signals (for example interleukin-6 and follistatin), so they are increasingly implicated as regulators in pituitary inflammation and in the behaviour of pituitary adenomas, where they are best regarded as a modulating or MARKER cell rather than a clearly established primary CAUSE. They are the likely origin of the rare pituitary tumours folliculo-stellate-derived spindle cell oncocytoma and some non-endocrine sellar tumours, where they would be a CAUSE. Given how incompletely this cell is characterised, confident disease attributions are limited, and this uncertainty is itself the honest finding.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 58. PINEALOCYTE (Melatonin Cell of the Pineal Gland) (The Hormone-of-Darkness Cell) (NON-COG) - CROSS-REFERENCE, neuron catalogue Chapter 283

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The pinealocyte is a neuroepithelial derivative, not a neural-crest or endodermal cell. It arises from the roof of the diencephalon, the same dorsal midline neuroepithelium that produces the epithalamus, and it shares its deepest ancestry with the retinal photoreceptor: the two cell types are thought to descend from a common ancestral photodetector.

Its specification runs through the same photoreceptor transcription-factor cassette. The orthodenticle homeobox factor Otx2 is required, and its loss produces a pineal gland with no pinealocytes at all; Otx2 in turn switches on the cone-rod homeobox factor Crx, which drives terminal differentiation. Paired-box (Pax) family factors participate in the early pineal anlage.

Timings here are rodent. In the rat, pineal Otx2 expression peaks around embryonic day 20, with Crx following after a short lag, so the cell is specified prenatally and matures across the perinatal and early postnatal window. Human timings for pinealocyte terminal differentiation are less precisely mapped, and I will not convert the rat figures into human days.

Melatonin output is not fixed at birth: the nocturnal melatonin rhythm strengthens over the first months of infancy and later declines with age. Whether adult human pinealocytes are meaningfully replaced is not established, and I decline to state a turnover rate.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The pinealocyte is a CASUALTY in pineal region tumours and in surgical or radiation damage, where loss of the cell abolishes the nocturnal melatonin signal and disrupts circadian and sleep timing. Its output is a MARKER of circadian phase, and blunted or phase-shifted melatonin is used as a readout in circadian rhythm sleep disorders and in seasonal and mood conditions rather than as their cause. Age-related pineal calcification is common and correlates with reduced melatonin, but a clean causal claim that calcified pinealocyte loss drives a specific disease is not settled. The rare pinealocyte-derived tumours (pineocytoma, pineoblastoma) are the clearest disease in which the cell itself is the CAUSE.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 59. PINEAL INTERSTITIAL (GLIAL) CELL (Astrocyte-Like Support Cell of the Pineal Gland) (The Clock-Tower Keeper Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: This cell is an astrocyte proper, and like other central-nervous-system astrocytes it is a neuroectodermal derivative arising from the neuroepithelium of the diencephalic roof alongside the pinealocytes it supports. It expresses the astrocyte marker glial fibrillary acidic protein (GFAP), which places it firmly in the astroglial lineage rather than the secretory pineal lineage.

The specific transcription-factor program that commits a pineal interstitial cell, as distinct from astrocytes elsewhere, is not separately characterised, and I will not invent one. It is reasonable to say it follows the general astroglial gliogenic program, which in rodents peaks late in gestation and in the early postnatal period, after the neuronal and pinealocyte waves.

Astrocytes retain proliferative and reactive capacity throughout life, so this population can expand and be maintained in the adult; a precise birth date or turnover figure for the human pineal interstitial cell is not established and I decline to state one.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: This cell is the CAUSE in pineal astrocytoma and related glial tumours of the pineal region, which arise from the interstitial glial compartment rather than from pinealocytes. In pineal calcification and in reactive gliosis around pineal cysts or tumours it is better read as a CASUALTY or a reactive MARKER. It has no strong selective-vulnerability profile of its own; it shares the general resilience of astrocytes.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 60. THYROID FOLLICULAR CELL (Thyrocyte) (The Thermostat-Setting Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The thyrocyte is an endodermal cell. It buds from the floor of the primitive pharynx (the foregut endoderm) as the median thyroid anlage, then descends along the thyroglossal duct to its final position in the neck. Its identity is set by a small combination of transcription factors: NKX2-1 (also called TTF-1), PAX8, FOXE1 (TTF-2), and HHEX, whose combined expression marks the committed thyroid precursor and drives thyroglobulin and thyroid peroxidase.

In humans the gland is anatomically formed by roughly the end of the first trimester, and fetal thyroid hormone synthesis begins in the second trimester; before that the fetus depends on maternal thyroid hormone crossing the placenta. This dependence defines a genuine critical period: adequate thyroid hormone and iodine during fetal and early infant life is required for normal brain development, and deficiency in that window produces irreversible neurological damage.

Thyrocytes are long-lived and turn over slowly in the adult, but the gland retains regenerative capacity and can be driven to proliferate, for example in goitre.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The thyrocyte is the CAUSE in congenital hypothyroidism from thyroid dysgenesis or dyshormonogenesis, and in thyroid nodules and thyroid carcinomas (papillary and follicular) that arise from it. It is a CASUALTY in autoimmune thyroiditis (Hashimoto disease), where the immune system destroys it, and in Graves disease it is a driven target of stimulating antibodies rather than the initiating fault. In iodine deficiency the resulting hypothyroidism and, in the fetus, cretinism, reflect the cell as a CASUALTY of a missing substrate. Its selective vulnerability is to iodine supply, autoimmunity, and radiation, the last being the basis of radioiodine-induced thyroid cancer.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 61. PARAFOLLICULAR (C) CELL (The Calcium-Lowering Coast-Guard Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The lineage of the C cell is currently unsettled, and this entry reports the disagreement rather than resolving it. The long-standing textbook account is that C cells are neural-crest-derived, arriving via the ultimobranchial body that fuses with the thyroid. Recent genetic lineage tracing in mice (Johansson and colleagues, 2015) instead traced differentiated C cells and their progenitors to Sox17-positive anterior (pharyngeal-pouch) endoderm, with the neural crest contributing only surrounding stroma. Comparative work extends an endodermal origin for calcitonin cells to pre-vertebrate chordates.

Because the two camps use different tracing tools and species, the honest statement is that the germ-layer origin of the mammalian C cell is not settled; what would settle it is concordant high-resolution lineage tracing in human tissue. Either way the mature cell is neuroendocrine, part of the diffuse neuroendocrine system, and expresses calcitonin.

Rodent data show C cells present and secreting from late gestation onward. A firm human birth-date and any postnatal replacement rate are not well established and I decline to state them.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The C cell is the CAUSE in medullary thyroid carcinoma (MTC) and its precursor C-cell hyperplasia, driven in the inherited forms by activating RET mutations (multiple endocrine neoplasia type 2). Calcitonin and carcinoembryonic antigen serve as MARKERS of MTC tumour burden. Notably the cell is largely dispensable: total thyroidectomy removes all C cells with no clinically important calcium disorder, so it has no major CASUALTY role in calcium disease. Its selective vulnerability is essentially oncogenic, through the RET pathway.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 62. PARATHYROID CHIEF CELL (Principal Cell) (The Calcium-Thermostat Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The chief cell is an endodermal derivative. The parathyroid glands develop from the third and fourth pharyngeal (branchial) pouch endoderm; the inferior parathyroids come from the third pouch (migrating with the thymus) and the superior from the fourth. The master transcription factor is GCM2 (glial cells missing homolog 2), whose loss abolishes parathyroid formation; GATA3 and other factors cooperate upstream.

In humans the glands form during the first trimester and are functional in fetal life, contributing to fetal calcium regulation. The chief cell senses calcium through the calcium-sensing receptor and secretes parathyroid hormone (PTH); this machinery is in place before birth.

Chief cells are long-lived with low baseline turnover but can proliferate markedly under a chronic low-calcium or high-phosphate stimulus, as in secondary hyperparathyroidism, so the population is expandable rather than fixed.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The chief cell is the CAUSE in primary hyperparathyroidism, where a clonal adenoma or hyperplasia oversecretes PTH and raises blood calcium, and it is the CAUSE, through proliferation, in secondary and tertiary hyperparathyroidism of chronic kidney disease. It is a CASUALTY in DiGeorge syndrome (22q11.2 deletion), where failed third and fourth pouch development leaves too few chief cells and produces neonatal hypocalcaemia, and in surgical damage or autoimmune hypoparathyroidism. Its selective vulnerability is to disturbances of the calcium-sensing receptor and to any insult to pharyngeal-pouch development. It is the endocrine cell whose acute total loss kills fastest, through tetany.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 63. PARATHYROID OXYPHIL CELL (The Idle Mitochondrial Furnace Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The oxyphil cell shares the parathyroid's third and fourth pharyngeal-pouch endodermal origin. The prevailing view is that oxyphil cells are not a separate lineage but a transformed, aged state of chief cells, accumulating mitochondria over time. This is why they are essentially absent in young children, appear around puberty, and increase steadily with age, reading more as a marker of the passing years than as a developmentally specified type.

Because the cell is defined by an organelle rather than a settled lineage, its transcription-factor program is simply that of the parathyroid; no distinct specification factor for the oxyphil state is established, and I decline to invent one. Whether it secretes hormone under normal conditions is genuinely debated: some studies find PTH and PTH-related peptide machinery in oxyphils, especially in kidney disease, but its everyday function remains unclear.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The oxyphil cell is best classed as a MARKER of ageing and of chronic parathyroid stimulation, its numbers rising in chronic kidney disease and secondary hyperparathyroidism. Oxyphil-predominant parathyroid adenomas exist, in which the cell is the CAUSE of hyperparathyroidism, but these are uncommon. Given the unsettled function, a confident selective-vulnerability profile cannot be stated honestly.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 64. ZONA GLOMERULOSA CELL (The Salt-and-Pressure Governor Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: This is a mesodermal cell. The adrenal cortex arises from the adrenogonadal primordium, a condensation of intermediate/coelomic mesoderm marked by steroidogenic factor 1 (SF-1, the nuclear receptor NR5A1), which is the master regulator of adrenocortical fate. In humans the adrenogonadal primordium is detectable at about 28 to 30 days after conception, and a distinct fetal adrenal cortex forms early, later remodelling into the adult zones.

The glomerulosa is the outermost band and expresses the terminal enzyme aldosterone synthase (CYP11B2), which no other cell carries. A striking developmental fact is that the cortex is maintained by centripetal renewal: glomerulosa cells behave as a progenitor pool that converts, by lineage conversion, into fasciculata cells as they move inward. This continues postnatally and through adult life, so glomerulosa cells are both a differentiated type and a renewing source for the deeper zones.

Because renewal is lifelong, the cell is continuously replaced rather than made once. Its regulation shifts to full angiotensin and potassium responsiveness across late fetal and postnatal maturation.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The glomerulosa cell is the CAUSE in primary aldosteronism (Conn syndrome), whether from an aldosterone-producing adenoma or bilateral hyperplasia, now recognised as one of the commonest curable causes of hypertension; somatic mutations (for example in KCNJ5) drive many adenomas. It is a CASUALTY in Addison disease and in autoimmune or infectious adrenalitis, where destruction of the cortex causes aldosterone deficiency with salt wasting. Its selective vulnerability is to autoimmune cortical destruction and to gain-of-function channel and pump mutations that lock it into oversecretion.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 65. ZONA FASCICULATA CELL (The Stress-Steroid Factory Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The fasciculata cell shares the SF-1-dependent adrenogonadal-primordium origin from intermediate/coelomic mesoderm. It is not, for the most part, born directly: under the centripetal-renewal model it is generated postnatally and throughout life by lineage conversion of outer zona glomerulosa cells as they migrate inward, then is eventually cleared at the reticularis-medulla boundary. SF-1 is required both for its steroidogenic identity and for the glomerulosa-to-fasciculata conversion.

The fasciculata is the final effector of the hypothalamic-pituitary-adrenal (HPA) axis and makes cortisol under adrenocorticotropic hormone (ACTH) drive. The fetal adrenal makes cortisol late in gestation, and the surge of fetal cortisol is part of the signalling that matures the lungs and helps time birth, so this cell participates in a genuine perinatal developmental window.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The fasciculata cell is the CAUSE in adrenal Cushing syndrome from a cortisol-producing adenoma or carcinoma, and in ACTH-driven hyperplasia it is the overworked effector. It is a CASUALTY in Addison disease (autoimmune adrenalitis, tuberculosis, adrenoleukodystrophy) and in adrenal haemorrhage, where its loss causes glucocorticoid deficiency and adrenal crisis. In congenital adrenal hyperplasia (for example 21-hydroxylase deficiency) the cell is the site of a blocked biosynthetic pathway, a CAUSE of impaired cortisol output with shunting to androgens. Its selective vulnerabilities are autoimmune destruction, the peroxisomal defect of adrenoleukodystrophy, and haemorrhagic infarction.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 66. ZONA RETICULARIS CELL (The Androgen-Precursor Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The reticularis cell shares the SF-1-dependent mesodermal adrenogonadal origin and is the innermost, last-formed cortical band. Under centripetal renewal it is the terminal stage of the migrating cortical cell, generated late and continuously from outer-zone precursors before clearance at the medullary junction.

Its defining developmental feature is timing. The reticularis is functionally near-silent at birth (the distinct fetal zone that made large amounts of dehydroepiandrosterone-sulphate involutes after birth), and the adult-type reticularis producing dehydroepiandrosterone (DHEA) and its sulphate switches on in mid-childhood at adrenarche, typically from around ages 6 to 8 in humans, peaks in early adulthood, and then declines across the rest of life (the fall sometimes called the adrenopause). This makes the cell one of the body's clearest endocrine clocks of ageing.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The reticularis cell is the CAUSE in adrenal androgen excess, contributing to premature adrenarche and to the androgen output of some adrenal tumours and of congenital adrenal hyperplasia. Its age-related decline in DHEA is a MARKER of adrenal ageing rather than a proven cause of disease, and I decline to assert a firm causal role for that decline. It is a CASUALTY, along with the other cortical zones, in Addison disease. Its selective vulnerability tracks that of the cortex as a whole plus its unique dependence on the adrenarche program.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 67. ADRENAL CHROMAFFIN CELL (The Wireless Alarm Cell) (NON-COG) - CROSS-REFERENCE, neuron catalogue Chapter 261

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: This is a neural-crest cell, and its full developmental treatment lives in the neuron catalogue at Chapter 261; this entry is a brief cross-reference. It derives from the sympathoadrenal lineage of trunk neural crest, cells that migrate to the dorsal aorta and then invade the developing adrenal cortex, where local glucocorticoid and other signals steer them toward the chromaffin (rather than sympathetic neuron) fate. Specification runs through the neural-crest and autonomic program, including factors such as ASCL1 (MASH1), PHOX2B, and HAND2, with PNMT (the enzyme making adrenaline) induced late under cortical glucocorticoid influence.

In humans chromaffin cells populate the medulla in fetal life. A notable feature is that the medulla harbours immature neuroblast-like cells that persist and can retain proliferative capacity, relevant to childhood tumours.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The chromaffin cell is the CAUSE in phaeochromocytoma (adrenal) and, through its lineage relatives, in extra-adrenal paraganglioma, and in the childhood tumour neuroblastoma that arises from immature sympathoadrenal neuroblasts. Hereditary forms involve germline mutations in the succinate dehydrogenase (SDH) genes, RET, VHL, and NF1. Its secretory products (catecholamines and metanephrines) are diagnostic MARKERS. Its selective vulnerability is oncogenic, tied to pseudohypoxia and SDH-pathway defects.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 68. ADRENAL MEDULLARY SUSTENTACULAR CELL (The Medulla's Glial Nursemaid Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The sustentacular cell is a glial-type cell of neural-crest origin, arising from the same sympathoadrenal/Schwann-cell precursor stream as the chromaffin cells it surrounds; recent work identifies Schwann-cell precursors as a source of adrenal medullary cells. It expresses the glial marker S100 protein (and GFAP-like features), marking it as the medulla's astrocyte-and-Schwann-cell analogue rather than a secretory cell.

It serves as a local support and reserve stem cell able, under the right signals, to generate new chromaffin cells, so the medulla retains some renewal capacity into postnatal life. Precise human birth-date and turnover figures for this specific cell are not established, and I decline to state them.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The sustentacular cell is chiefly a MARKER: S100-positive sustentacular cells form a characteristic rim around the cell nests of phaeochromocytoma and paraganglioma, and their pattern and loss help pathologists grade these tumours. It is not the primary CAUSE of those tumours (the chromaffin cell is), though its reduction correlates with more aggressive behaviour. It has no well-defined independent disease of its own, and I will not manufacture one.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 69. BETA CELL (Insulin-Secreting B Cell of the Pancreatic Islet) (The Glucose-Tasting Insulin Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The beta cell is an endodermal derivative. It arises from the foregut endoderm that forms the pancreatic buds, passing through a PDX1-positive pancreatic progenitor, then a transient NEUROG3 (NGN3) endocrine-progenitor state that is required for all islet cells; beta-cell identity and maturation then depend on NKX6-1, PAX4, NEUROD1, and the maturation factor MAFA. In human development a single wave of endocrine differentiation is described, in contrast to the two waves in mice, so mouse timing should not be transferred wholesale.

In humans insulin-positive cells appear in the first trimester and islets form through fetal life, but functional glucose-responsive maturation (linked to MAFA) continues into the postnatal period. Human beta-cell mass is largely established early; adult human beta cells turn over very slowly and regenerative capacity is limited, which is why lost beta-cell mass is not readily replaced, a central problem in diabetes.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The beta cell is the CASUALTY in type 1 diabetes, where autoimmune T-cell attack selectively destroys it (autoantibodies to insulin, GAD65, IA-2, and ZnT8 are MARKERS, not the primary cause). In type 2 diabetes it is both a CASUALTY and a partial CAUSE: chronic insulin resistance drives it, and progressive beta-cell dysfunction and loss (with glucotoxicity, lipotoxicity, and amyloid deposition) drive the disease forward. Monogenic MODY forms are CAUSES arising from mutations in its own transcription factors (for example HNF1A, HNF4A, PDX1) or the glucose sensor glucokinase. Its selective vulnerability is to autoimmunity, endoplasmic-reticulum and oxidative stress, and its low regenerative reserve.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 70. ALPHA CELL (Glucagon-Secreting A Cell of the Pancreatic Islet) (The Fasting-Alarm Sugar-Raising Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The alpha cell shares the beta cell's foregut-endoderm origin and passes through the same PDX1 progenitor and the obligatory NEUROG3 endocrine-progenitor state. Its divergence toward the glucagon fate is set by the transcription factors ARX and MAFB; ARX in particular pushes progenitors to alpha (and PP) identity and away from beta/delta, so the ARX-versus-PAX4 balance partitions the islet.

Human alpha cells appear early in fetal islet development. Adult alpha cells show a degree of plasticity: under extreme beta-cell loss a fraction can be reprogrammed toward insulin production (shown experimentally, including with PDX1 and MAFA), which makes them a candidate reserve, though this is not a normal replacement route.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The alpha cell is a contributing CAUSE in the hyperglycaemia of both type 1 and type 2 diabetes, because inappropriate glucagon excess and loss of glucose-suppressibility worsen fasting hyperglycaemia. In type 1 diabetes it is also a CASUALTY of defective counter-regulation: impaired glucagon responses leave patients vulnerable to hypoglycaemia. The rare glucagonoma is a tumour in which the alpha cell is the CAUSE, marked by high glucagon and a characteristic rash. Its selective vulnerability is comparatively low; alpha cells are relatively spared in type 1 diabetes even as beta cells are destroyed.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 71. DELTA CELL (Somatostatin-Secreting D Cell of the Pancreatic Islet) (The Islet's Local Referee Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The delta cell is a foregut-endoderm derivative arising through the shared PDX1 progenitor and the NEUROG3 endocrine-progenitor step. Its identity is associated with the transcription factor HHEX (and PAX4 in the beta/delta branch), which is required for normal delta-cell development. Delta cells appear in fetal islets and are present at birth.

Precise human delta-cell birth-timing and turnover are not tightly quantified in the literature, and I decline to invent numbers. Its distinctive dendritic, process-bearing morphology, reaching somatostatin to neighbouring cells, develops as the islet organises.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The delta cell is chiefly a modulator, and disease attributions are modest. Loss or dysfunction of delta-cell somatostatin tone is implicated as a contributing CAUSE of dysregulated glucagon secretion in diabetes, since somatostatin normally restrains alpha cells. The rare somatostatinoma is a tumour in which the delta cell is the CAUSE, producing diabetes, gallstones, and steatorrhoea. Its selective-vulnerability profile is not well defined, and I will not overstate it.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 72. GAMMA (PP) CELL (Pancreatic-Polypeptide-Secreting F Cell of the Islet) (The After-Dinner Satiety Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The gamma (PP or F) cell shares the foregut-endoderm origin and the NEUROG3 endocrine-progenitor step of the islet, and like the alpha cell it depends on ARX for its non-beta identity. It is concentrated in the ventrally derived (posterior head) part of the pancreas, a regional bias set during the fusion of the dorsal and ventral pancreatic buds. Gamma cells are present in fetal and neonatal islets.

Firm human timing and turnover figures for this specific cell are not well established, and I decline to state them.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The gamma cell has weak direct disease attributions. Pancreatic polypeptide is a MARKER: elevated levels flag some pancreatic neuroendocrine tumours and can serve as a general islet-tumour marker. A pure PPoma (a tumour of this cell) is described but rare, in which case the cell is the CAUSE. Because the cell is poorly studied and its pathology thin, this entry is deliberately short.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 73. EPSILON CELL (Ghrelin-Secreting Islet Cell) (The Rare Hunger-Hormone Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The epsilon cell is a foregut-endoderm islet cell arising through the shared NEUROG3 endocrine-progenitor pathway. Its lineage relationships are still being worked out, and this is a genuinely under-settled area: ghrelin-expressing cells are relatively abundant in the fetal and neonatal pancreas and decline after birth, and some studies suggest transient ghrelin expression in progenitors that later become other islet types. The transcription-factor code that uniquely specifies the mature epsilon cell is not firmly established, and I decline to invent one.

Because the cell was only firmly identified in the early twenty-first century and is very rare (well under one percent of islet cells), reliable human birth-timing, maturation, and turnover figures are not available, and I will not manufacture them.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Disease attributions for the epsilon cell are sparse and uncertain. Its ghrelin may act locally to restrain glucose-stimulated insulin release, so a modulatory contribution to islet tone is plausible but not established as a named disorder. There is no well-defined disease in which this cell is a proven primary CAUSE or CASUALTY, so honesty requires keeping this entry brief and refusing firm claims.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 74. LEYDIG CELL (Interstitial Cell of the Testis) (The Testosterone Furnace Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The Leydig cell is a mesoderm-derived interstitial cell of the testis, arising from the gonadal ridge (coelomic epithelium and adjacent mesenchyme) under SF-1 (NR5A1); its steroidogenic differentiation depends on signals from the Sertoli-cell-organised testis cords, notably Desert hedgehog. There are two distinct populations in a lifetime. Fetal Leydig cells appear in the first trimester (driven initially by placental human chorionic gonadotropin, later by fetal luteinising hormone) and make the testosterone that masculinises the fetus during the genital critical period; they then regress after birth. Adult Leydig cells arise anew at puberty from an interstitial stem/progenitor pool under luteinising hormone (LH), and these supply lifelong testosterone.

Adult Leydig cells have limited but real regenerative capacity: after chemical ablation (studied in rodents) a stem Leydig cell population can regenerate the adult set, so the lineage is replaceable in principle. Testosterone output declines gradually with age.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The Leydig cell is the CAUSE in primary hypogonadism when it fails (Leydig-cell dysfunction, and in Klinefelter syndrome where the cells are dysfunctional despite high LH), and in the rare Leydig cell tumour. It is a CASUALTY of testicular damage from radiation, chemotherapy, ischaemia, or mumps orchitis. In fetal life, disrupted Leydig testosterone (or its signalling) is a CAUSE of disorders of sex development and undermasculinisation. Its selective vulnerability is to gonadotoxic insult and to the pituitary LH signal it depends on.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 75. SERTOLI CELL (Sustentacular Cell of the Seminiferous Tubule) (The Sperm-Nurse Barrier Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The Sertoli cell is the pivotal cell of testis determination and derives from the coelomic-epithelium/gonadal-ridge somatic lineage of the bipotential gonad. The Y-chromosome gene SRY, acting on SOX9, commits these supporting cells to the Sertoli fate; SOX9 (with FGF9 and prostaglandin D2) then locks in and amplifies the male program and organises the testis cords. Sertoli cells are therefore the first cell type whose specification decides testis versus ovary.

Fetal Sertoli cells secrete anti-Mullerian hormone (AMH), which regresses the female (Mullerian) ducts during a fetal critical window, and they proliferate heavily in fetal and prepubertal life; the final adult Sertoli-cell number, which sets the ceiling on sperm output, is fixed around puberty, after which they are largely non-dividing and not readily replaced. They build the blood-testis barrier at puberty and secrete inhibin B.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The Sertoli cell is the CAUSE in Sertoli-cell-only syndrome (germ cells absent, azoospermia), in AMH-related persistent Mullerian duct syndrome, and in the rare Sertoli cell tumour. It is central, through SRY/SOX9 failure, as a CAUSE in 46,XY disorders of sex development (gonadal dysgenesis). Inhibin B and AMH are MARKERS of Sertoli-cell mass and function. It is a CASUALTY of gonadotoxins, heat, and radiation. Its selective vulnerability centres on the fetal determination window and on the fixed, non-renewing adult pool.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 76. GRANULOSA CELL (Follicular Cell of the Ovary) (The Oestrogen-Finishing Egg-Nurse Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The granulosa cell is the ovarian counterpart of the Sertoli cell and derives from the somatic supporting lineage of the bipotential gonad (coelomic epithelium and gonadal ridge, SF-1 dependent). Its female identity is specified and, crucially, actively maintained by the transcription factor FOXL2 (with WNT4 and RSPO1/beta-catenin signalling), which suppresses the SOX9 male program; FOXL2 loss can even trans-differentiate granulosa cells toward a Sertoli-like state, showing the fate must be held open for life.

Granulosa cells surround oocytes to form primordial follicles late in fetal life (in humans, follicle assembly occurs in the second and third trimesters). They proliferate as follicles grow across the reproductive years, and after ovulation they luteinise into the progesterone-secreting cells of the corpus luteum, a repeated cyclic differentiation. The finite follicle pool is largely set before birth and is not replenished (the classic no-new-oocytes framework), so the follicular reserve declines with age toward menopause.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The granulosa cell is a contributing CAUSE in polycystic ovary syndrome, where its aromatisation and follicle maturation are dysregulated, and it is the CAUSE in adult and juvenile granulosa cell tumours (adult tumours carry a characteristic FOXL2 C134W mutation, a diagnostic MARKER). FOXL2 mutation is the CAUSE of blepharophimosis-ptosis-epicanthus-inversus syndrome (BPES) with premature ovarian insufficiency. It is a CASUALTY in chemotherapy- and radiation-induced ovarian failure and in autoimmune oophoritis. Its selective vulnerability is to gonadotoxins and to the lifelong requirement to maintain FOXL2-based female identity.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 77. THECA CELL (Theca Interna Cell of the Ovarian Follicle) (The Androgen-Supplier Partner Cell) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The theca cell is the ovarian analogue of the Leydig cell and arises from ovarian stromal/interstitial mesenchyme recruited around growing follicles; it is SF-1-dependent and steroidogenic. Unlike granulosa cells, which are present from fetal follicle assembly, a defined theca layer forms only once follicles reach the secondary (pre-antral) stage after puberty, recruited by signals (including from the oocyte and granulosa) as the follicle grows. Theca cells make androgen (chiefly androstenedione) under luteinising hormone, the substrate the granulosa cell aromatises.

Because theca cells are recruited afresh with each cohort of growing follicles, the population is continuously regenerated across the reproductive years rather than made once. Firm human quantitative timing figures are limited and I decline to invent them.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The theca cell is a central CAUSE in polycystic ovary syndrome, where intrinsic theca-cell androgen overproduction is a well-documented driver of hyperandrogenism. It is the CAUSE in the rare androgen-secreting thecoma and in ovarian hyperthecosis (marked ovarian androgen excess, often postmenopausal). Its products (testosterone, androstenedione) are MARKERS of ovarian androgen excess. Its selective vulnerability is metabolic and endocrine (insulin and LH drive its oversecretion) rather than a distinct destructive process.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 78. THE ENTEROENDOCRINE CELL FAMILY (Gut Epithelial Hormone Cells) (The Gut's Scattered Hormone Militia) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: Enteroendocrine cells are endodermal, arising from the same intestinal-crypt stem cells (LGR5-positive) that produce all the gut epithelial lineages. Commitment to the secretory branch runs through ATOH1 (MATH1), and then, as in the pancreas, the transcription factor NEUROG3 (NGN3) is the obligatory switch that commits a progenitor to the enteroendocrine fate; downstream factors (NEUROD1, PAX4, PAX6, ARX, and others) then diversify the family into its many hormone subtypes (for example gastrin, secretin, cholecystokinin, GIP, GLP-1, serotonin-secreting cells).

A defining feature is continuous replacement: like the rest of the gut epithelium, enteroendocrine cells are among the most rapidly renewed cells in the body, turning over on the order of days as they are generated in the crypt and shed at the villus tip. So this family is made and remade throughout life, and its regenerative capacity is essentially unlimited within the healthy epithelium.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The family is the CAUSE in gastrointestinal neuroendocrine tumours (carcinoids), including gastrinomas (causing Zollinger-Ellison syndrome) and serotonin-secreting tumours (causing carcinoid syndrome); serotonin and chromogranin A are diagnostic MARKERS. Loss of NEUROG3 function is a CAUSE of congenital malabsorptive diarrhoea from enteroendocrine-cell deficiency. Its incretin cells (GLP-1) are a therapeutic target rather than a disease cause. Its selective vulnerability is oncogenic transformation and, rarely, the loss-of-function developmental defects.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 79. NEUROPOD CELL (Synapse-Forming Enteroendocrine Cell) (The Gut's Hard-Wired Taste Reporter) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The neuropod cell is a subtype of enteroendocrine cell and shares that endodermal, intestinal-stem-cell (LGR5, ATOH1, NEUROG3) origin. What is distinctive is that it expresses neuronal genes and grows a basal axon-like process (the neuropod) that forms a true fast synapse onto vagal afferent fibres; it was described and named by Diego Bohorquez and colleagues at Duke University. The exact transcriptional program that gives an enteroendocrine cell its neuropod and synaptic machinery is still being defined, so I decline to assert a settled specification code.

As an enteroendocrine subtype it is continuously renewed from crypt stem cells on the same days-long timescale, and it must rebuild its synaptic connection to the vagus as cells are replaced, an unusual feature that is still being studied.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: This is a young field and disease attributions are preliminary. The neuropod cell is being investigated as a route by which the gut drives brain states, so it is implicated (not yet as a proven CAUSE) in appetite and reward signalling, in vagal sugar-sensing, and potentially in mood and gut-brain disorders. No disorder yet cleanly assigns it CAUSE or CASUALTY status, and honesty requires flagging that the clinical picture is not yet established. This entry is deliberately short.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 80. PULMONARY NEUROENDOCRINE CELL (Airway Neuroendocrine Cell) (The Airway's Oxygen Sentinel) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The pulmonary neuroendocrine cell (PNEC) is an endodermal derivative of the airway epithelium, not a neural-crest cell. It arises from the foregut-derived lung epithelium, and its specification requires the proneural transcription factor ASCL1 (MASH1); loss of ASCL1 abolishes PNECs. During fetal and early postnatal lung development PNECs are among the first differentiated airway cells and cluster at airway branch points into innervated neuroepithelial bodies (NEBs), which act as oxygen sensors especially important in the perinatal period when the newborn transitions to air breathing.

PNECs are rare in the mature airway but the NEB niche retains stem/progenitor capacity and PNECs can expand (hyperplasia) after injury, so the population is replaceable and reactive rather than fixed.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The PNEC is the CAUSE, as cell of origin, in small cell lung cancer and other high-grade pulmonary neuroendocrine tumours (carcinoids and large-cell neuroendocrine carcinoma), with ASCL1 acting as a lineage oncogene and TP53/RB1 loss and MYC amplification driving the malignant form. It is the CAUSE in neuroendocrine cell hyperplasia of infancy (NEHI). PNEC hyperplasia is also a MARKER accompanying asthma, cystic fibrosis, and bronchopulmonary dysplasia. Its selective vulnerability is a strong propensity to neuroendocrine malignant transformation once tumour-suppressor brakes are lost.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 81. MERKEL CELL (Merkel-Ranvier Tactile Cell) (The Fingertip's Fine-Print Reader) (NON-COG) - CROSS-REFERENCE, neuron catalogue Chapter 282

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The origin of the Merkel cell was debated for over a century and is now settled in favour of an epidermal, not a neural-crest, origin; its full treatment is in the neuron catalogue at Chapter 282. Lineage-tracing experiments (2009) showed that Merkel cells arise from epidermal progenitors of the skin, and that the transcription factor ATOH1 (MATH1) is required for their differentiation: deleting Atoh1 from the skin lineage removes all Merkel cells, while deleting it from the neural crest has no effect (these deletion experiments were in mice). SOX2 and POU4F3 act with ATOH1 in the program.

Merkel cells form in fetal skin and, importantly, are continuously replenished in adult skin: epidermal stem cells generate new Merkel cells as older ones die, so unlike many neural cells this touch cell is renewed throughout life.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The Merkel cell is the presumed CAUSE, as cell of origin, in Merkel cell carcinoma, an aggressive skin cancer driven in most cases by clonal integration of the Merkel cell polyomavirus (a viral CAUSE) and in the remainder by ultraviolet-mutation. Merkel cell numbers decline with age and in some neuropathies, where the cell is more a CASUALTY or MARKER of impaired fine touch. Its selective vulnerability is to polyomavirus-driven and ultraviolet-driven transformation, and it is notably sun-exposure and immunosuppression associated.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 82. GLOMUS (TYPE I) CELL OF THE CAROTID AND AORTIC BODIES (Arterial Chemoreceptor Cell) (The Blood's Oxygen Taster) (NON-COG) - CROSS-REFERENCE, neuron catalogue Chapter 281 context

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The glomus type I cell is a neural-crest-derived, neuron-like secretory cell; related afferent detail is treated in the neuron catalogue at Chapter 281 and this is a cross-reference. It arises from the sympathoadrenal branch of the neural crest, cells that migrate to the carotid bifurcation and aortic arch and settle around the vasculature to form the carotid and aortic bodies. Recent work also implicates Schwann-cell precursors as a source of glomus cells. Specification uses the autonomic neural-crest program (ASCL1, PHOX2B).

The carotid body forms in fetal life and undergoes a well-documented postnatal resetting of its oxygen set-point in the days after birth, a genuine sensitive period as the newborn adapts to air. The organ retains a resident stem population (the type II cells) that can generate new type I cells in adult life, notably expanding under chronic hypoxia, so the sensor is replaceable and can grow.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The glomus type I cell is the CAUSE, as cell of origin, in carotid body paraganglioma (chemodectoma), driven in hereditary cases by succinate dehydrogenase (SDHB, SDHD, SDHC) germline mutations that mimic hypoxia. Its hyperactivity is a contributing CAUSE and a MARKER in the raised sympathetic drive of chronic hypoxia, obstructive sleep apnoea, heart failure, and some hypertension. Carotid body hypertrophy is a MARKER of chronic hypoxaemia (as at altitude). Its selective vulnerability is to the SDH-pathway pseudohypoxia tumours and to chronic hypoxic overstimulation.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 83. GLOMUS TYPE II (Sustentacular) Cell (Chemoreceptor Supporting and Stem Cell) (The Oxygen Sensor's Glial Nurse) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The glomus type II cell is a glia-like supporting cell of neural-crest origin, closely related to Schwann-cell precursors, and it is the astrocyte-equivalent of the carotid body, expressing the glial marker S100 (and GFAP). It develops alongside the type I cells as the carotid and aortic bodies form in fetal life.

Its defining developmental role is as the organ's resident stem cell: under chronic hypoxia, type II cells proliferate and differentiate into new type I chemoreceptor cells, so they drive the growth (hypertrophy) of the carotid body when oxygen demand rises. This gives the arterial oxygen sensor a genuine, lifelong renewal capacity centred on this cell.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The type II cell is chiefly a MARKER, forming the S100-positive sustentacular rim used to identify and grade paragangliomas, and it is the source of the organ's adaptive hypertrophy in chronic hypoxia, sleep apnoea, and heart failure, where its proliferation is a driver of enhanced chemoreflex sensitivity (a contributing CAUSE of the raised sympathetic drive). It is not usually the primary transformed cell in paraganglioma. It has no well-defined destructive disease of its own, so this entry stays brief.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 84. PARAGANGLION CHIEF CELLS, SYMPATHETIC AND PARASYMPATHETIC (Extra-Adrenal Paraganglion Cells) (The Body's Scattered Adrenal Outposts) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: Paraganglion chief cells are neural-crest-derived, from the same sympathoadrenal and autonomic crest streams (and Schwann-cell precursors) that give rise to the adrenal medulla and the carotid body; they use the autonomic program (ASCL1, PHOX2B, HAND2). They form scattered clusters along the sympathetic chains and around the great vessels. The largest, the organ of Zuckerkandl on the fetal aorta, is prominent and catecholamine-producing before birth and then involutes in early childhood, a clear example of a developmentally timed, regressing endocrine tissue.

The sympathetic (chromaffin) paraganglia store catecholamines; the parasympathetic head-and-neck paraganglia are largely non-chromaffin chemoreceptors like the carotid body. These tissues are most prominent perinatally; residual cells persist into adult life and can proliferate to form tumours.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Paraganglion chief cells are the CAUSE, as cell of origin, in extra-adrenal paraganglioma (and the closely related phaeochromocytoma), which are strongly heritable: germline succinate dehydrogenase mutations (SDHB, SDHD, SDHC, SDHA), and also VHL, RET, and NF1, are the major drivers, with SDHB carrying the highest metastatic risk and SDHD showing parent-of-origin (paternal) inheritance. Catecholamines and metanephrines are diagnostic MARKERS in the secretory tumours. Its selective vulnerability is pseudohypoxic, SDH-pathway-driven neoplasia.

ENTRY: CHAPTER 85. JUXTAGLOMERULAR CELL OF THE KIDNEY (Renin-Secreting Granular Cell) (The Kidney's Pressure-Reading Alarm) (NON-COG)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The juxtaglomerular cell is a modified vascular smooth muscle cell of mesodermal (metanephric mesenchyme) origin. In development, renin-expressing cells appear early and broadly in the undifferentiated metanephric mesenchyme before mature vessels form; as the kidney matures, renin expression retreats to its final restricted position in the wall of the afferent arteriole at the glomerulus. The renin-cell lineage also gives rise to arteriolar smooth muscle, mesangial cells, pericytes, and a subset of tubular cells, so the mature juxtaglomerular cell is one settled fate of a broad developmental lineage.

A defining property is lifelong plasticity rather than a fixed birth date. When the body needs more renin (chronic low-salt diet, volume depletion, blockade of the renin-angiotensin system), cells that expressed renin earlier in life re-acquire the renin phenotype, a process called recruitment: smooth muscle, mesangial, and interstitial cells of the renin lineage dedifferentiate back into renin cells and then redifferentiate when homeostasis returns. So this cell is continuously replaceable and reversibly interconvertible with vascular smooth muscle.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The juxtaglomerular cell is the CAUSE in renovascular hypertension and other secondary hypertension where renin oversecretion drives blood pressure up, and in the rare reninoma (juxtaglomerular cell tumour) and Bartter-type states with secondary renin excess. In chronic renin-angiotensin blockade, its recruitment and the associated arteriolar wall thickening can be a MARKER (and a mechanism) of arteriolar remodelling and anaemia effects. It is a CASUALTY in conditions destroying the juxtaglomerular apparatus. Its selective vulnerability is functional, tied to renal perfusion pressure and macula densa salt signalling, not to a distinct destructive process.

================================================================================

PART TWO: THE 139 LAYER 9 REGIONAL HEADINGS, ENTRY BY ENTRY

Keyed to LAYER_09_NATURES_COGNITIVE_ARCHITECTURE_MANUSCRIPT, sections 2.1 through 2.11.

ENTRY: 2.1 THE CEREBRAL CORTEX

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The entire cerebral cortex arises from the dorsal telencephalon, the pallium, at the front end of the neural tube. Cortical neurogenesis in the human begins around the fifth week after conception, peaks between roughly weeks 8 and 16, and is largely complete by midgestation, with progenitor activity declining but still detectable to about week 28.

The six laminae are assembled inside-out: the earliest-born neurons form the deepest laminae, and later-born neurons migrate radially past them along radial glial scaffolds to form the more superficial laminae. A transient subplate beneath the cortical plate hosts the waiting thalamic afferents before they invade lamina 4, and its dissolution in late gestation and early infancy marks the wiring-up of the thalamocortical interface.

Maturation then proceeds along the gradient Flechsig described from myelination: primary sensory and motor cortex first, unimodal association cortex second, and the heteromodal prefrontal, posterior parietal, and lateral temporal cortex last. Synaptic density peaks in primary visual cortex within the first year but in prefrontal cortex only in the second to fourth year, and pruning of the surplus runs through adolescence into the third decade.

The famous claim that the brain matures at 25 is an artefact of studies that stopped scanning in the mid-twenties. Large lifespan imaging finds no cliff at that age; frontal white matter and network organization keep changing into the thirties, and the honest statement is a gradient without a finishing line.

Cortex-wide disease patterns follow the same gradient in reverse: the late-maturing, most expensive association cortex is preferentially attacked by Alzheimer disease and schizophrenia, while the early-maturing primary cortices are comparatively spared, a pattern often summarized as last in, first out. Developmental disorders of migration itself, the lissencephalies and the heterotopias, are cortex-wide CAUSES of epilepsy and intellectual disability.

ENTRY: 2.1.1 PRIMARY VISUAL CORTEX (V1, STRIATE CORTEX, BRODMANN AREA 17)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: V1 forms in the caudal pole of the dorsal telencephalon, its lamination guided by geniculate afferents that wait in the subplate before invading lamina 4C in the third trimester. It is one of Flechsig's earliest-myelinating areas: the stria of Gennari, the myelinated band that names the striate cortex, is visible around birth and matures over the first postnatal months.

Synaptic density in human V1 peaks between about 4 and 12 months of age, earlier than any other cortical region measured, and is then pruned back through childhood.

V1 hosts the best-documented critical period in the human brain. Ocular dominance plasticity, worked out in kittens and monkeys (animal data), has its human counterpart in amblyopia: discordant input in roughly the first 7 to 8 years rewires V1 in favour of the better eye, and stereopsis has an even earlier and narrower sensitive window in infancy. Adult plasticity persists but is far weaker. V1 therefore matures before every visual area downstream of it.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: No major degenerative disease has V1 as its primary seat. In posterior cerebral artery stroke it is a CASUALTY, producing cortical blindness or hemianopia with pupillary reflexes spared.

In amblyopia V1 is the CAUSE of the perceptual loss, although the initiating fault (strabismus or refractive error) lies in front of it; the eye is treatable, the miswired cortex is what resists.

In migraine with visual aura, cortical spreading depression typically ignites in or near V1, making it the CAUSE of the aura though not of the migraine syndrome. In the visual variant of Alzheimer disease, posterior cortical atrophy, occipital cortex is a CASUALTY. Its selective vulnerability profile is low: V1 is early-maturing, heavily myelinated, and largely spared by the late-life degenerations.

ENTRY: 2.1.2 SECONDARY AND HIGHER VISUAL AREAS (V2, V3, V4, V5/MT)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: These areas arise from the same caudal pallium as V1 and mature after it, in feedforward order: V2 shortly behind V1, then V3 and V4, following the general rule that each stage matures after its principal input. The exception is V5/MT, which is unusually heavily myelinated and identifiable very early, one of the first extrastriate areas to mature; it has been argued to develop in parallel with V1 rather than strictly after it.

Their experience-dependent windows inherit V1's critical period: binocular and contour experience in early childhood shapes them through the input V1 supplies. No separate, well-dated human critical period has been established for V4 or MT specifically, and none should be invented.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: No disorder has these areas as its primary seat; their classical pathology is focal loss. V4 territory damage produces achromatopsia and V5/MT damage produces akinetopsia; in both the region is a CASUALTY of stroke, trauma, or tumour.

In posterior cortical atrophy, the visual-variant presentation of Alzheimer disease, dorsal-stream areas including V3 and MT and ventral V4 territory are early CASUALTIES, producing simultanagnosia and motion and colour complaints before memory fails.

Motion-processing abnormalities in these areas are reported as MARKERS in dyslexia (magnocellular findings, contested) and in schizophrenia; the causal status of both is unsettled. Selective vulnerability is otherwise unremarkable.
