DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The RVLM is implicated as a CAUSE-level contributor in neurogenic hypertension, where increased RVLM sympathetic drive, sometimes attributed to vascular compression of the ventrolateral medulla, raises blood pressure; it is a target of research into resistant hypertension. It is a CASUALTY in multiple system atrophy and in brainstem stroke, producing orthostatic hypotension and autonomic failure, and its C1 neurons are affected in the autonomic dysregulation of several neurodegenerative diseases. Its narrow location means small ventrolateral medullary lesions can cause disproportionate cardiovascular collapse.

ENTRY: 2.4.3.7 THE NUCLEUS AMBIGUUS

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The nucleus ambiguus is a branchiomotor and cardiac-parasympathetic column in the ventrolateral medulla, its neurons requiring Phox2b for their identity (rodent data). It supplies the pharyngeal and laryngeal muscles through the vagus and glossopharyngeal nerves and provides the parasympathetic slowing of the heart. Its motor topography for swallowing and phonation is laid down prenatally, and the cardiac vagal neurons form part of the developing baroreflex.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The nucleus ambiguus is a CASUALTY in lateral medullary (Wallenberg) stroke, producing hoarseness, dysphagia, and loss of the gag reflex, and in bulbar and pseudobulbar syndromes. Its lower motor neurons degenerate in the bulbar-onset form of amyotrophic lateral sclerosis, where their loss causes dysarthria, dysphagia, and eventually fatal airway compromise, a CASUALTY of motor neuron disease. Its cardiac vagal component is affected in autonomic neuropathies and contributes to arrhythmia risk when vagal tone is disturbed.

ENTRY: 2.4.3.8 THE PRE-BOTZINGER COMPLEX

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The pre-Botzinger complex is the small knot of ventrolateral medullary neurons that makes each breath begin, and it is the defined core of the respiratory rhythm generator. Its rhythmogenic interneurons derive from Dbx1-expressing progenitors, and in mouse the deletion of Dbx1 prevents the complex from forming, causing death at birth (rodent data). The rhythm is autonomous, since an isolated slice continues to burst, and it is functional before birth. Its coupling to arousal, emotion, and vocalisation matures alongside the wider brainstem.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The pre-Botzinger complex is central to congenital central hypoventilation syndrome (PHOX2B, a CAUSE), to the sudden infant death syndrome (impaired autoresuscitation, a CAUSE-level vulnerability), and to opioid-induced respiratory depression, which acts on its opioid-sensitive neurons. It degenerates in multiple system atrophy, contributing to central sleep apnoea and sudden death, where it is a CASUALTY. Selective loss of its neurokinin-one-receptor and somatostatin-expressing neurons abolishes stable breathing in animal models, underscoring the small margin of cells on which life depends.

ENTRY: 2.4.4 THE ASCENDING AROUSAL SYSTEM NUCLEI (PUC)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: This ensemble is not a single structure but the assembled set of neuromodulatory nuclei, the noradrenergic locus coeruleus, serotonergic raphe, histaminergic tuberomammillary nucleus, dopaminergic ventral tegmental area, cholinergic pedunculopontine, laterodorsal tegmental and basal forebrain groups, orexinergic lateral hypothalamus, and glutamatergic parabrachial nucleus, each with its own origin described in its own entry. What assembles across development is the sleep-wake flip-flop: the mutual inhibition between these arousal nuclei and the ventrolateral preoptic sleep-promoting neurons, stabilised by orexin. Consolidated sleep-wake cycling matures across infancy as this switch and its circadian and homeostatic drives mature; the orexin system in particular is established in late gestation.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The signature disorder is narcolepsy type one, caused by the loss of the roughly seventy thousand orexin (hypocretin) neurons, an autoimmune destruction that removes the stabiliser of the switch so that it flips uncontrollably between states, producing sleepiness, cataplexy, and intrusions of rapid-eye-movement sleep; here the orexin neurons are the CASUALTY and their loss is the CAUSE of the syndrome. Collective damage to these nuclei produces coma and the disorders of consciousness. Their individual degeneration in Alzheimer, Parkinson, and related diseases underlies much of the sleep and arousal disturbance of neurodegeneration.

ENTRY: 2.5 THE CEREBELLUM

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The cerebellum has two germinal zones and a strikingly protracted development. The Purkinje cells and deep nuclear neurons are born early, from the ventricular zone of the fourth ventricle roof, and the Purkinje cells are the first cerebellar cortical neurons to be generated. The granule cells, by contrast, are born from a second germinal zone, the external granular layer on the cerebellar surface, derived from the rhombic lip; these progenitors proliferate under Sonic hedgehog secreted by the Purkinje cells and then migrate inward past the Purkinje layer to form the internal granular layer.

This granule-cell proliferation is heavily postnatal. In humans the external granular layer is still dividing months after birth, with dividing cells present as late as the fifth postnatal month and the layer not disappearing until around the eleventh postnatal month, so the human cerebellum builds most of its neurons after birth and remains vulnerable throughout that window. The cerebellum contains more neurons than the rest of the brain combined, and its uniform circuit is templated everywhere.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The cerebellum is a CAUSE-of-failure site in the inherited developmental disorders (pontocerebellar hypoplasias, Joubert syndrome, Dandy-Walker malformation) and, because so much building is postnatal, it is uniquely vulnerable to prematurity, where cerebellar haemorrhage and impaired growth are common and link to later motor and cognitive impairment. It is a CASUALTY in the spinocerebellar ataxias and Friedreich ataxia, in alcoholic and paraneoplastic cerebellar degeneration (Purkinje cells are selectively vulnerable), in stroke and medulloblastoma (the commonest malignant childhood brain tumour, arising from granule-cell precursors, a CAUSE). Purkinje-cell loss is a consistent finding in autism, implicating cerebellar development. Damage produces ataxia, and posterior lobe damage produces the cerebellar cognitive affective syndrome.

ENTRY: 2.6 THE SPINAL CORD

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The spinal cord is the archetype of dorsoventral patterning: Sonic hedgehog from the notochord and floor plate ventrally, and bone morphogenetic proteins and Wnts from the roof plate dorsally, set up opposing gradients that specify discrete progenitor domains, so motor neurons form ventrally, sensory interneurons dorsally, and the many interneuron classes in between at precise dorsoventral positions. Motor neurons are among the earliest-born neurons. The neural tube closes in the fourth week of human gestation, and failure of closure produces the neural tube defects. The central pattern generators for locomotion are built into the cord, which is why it can generate stepping without the brain.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The spinal cord is a CAUSE-of-failure site in the neural tube defects (spina bifida, anencephaly), whose risk is lowered by periconceptional folate. Its motor neurons are the CASUALTY in spinal muscular atrophy (SMN1 loss, a CAUSE) and in amyotrophic lateral sclerosis. Its long tracts are the CASUALTY in multiple sclerosis, in subacute combined degeneration (vitamin B twelve deficiency), in tabes dorsalis (neurosyphilis attacking the dorsal columns and roots), and in Friedreich ataxia (dorsal root ganglia, dorsal columns, and spinocerebellar tracts). It is the site of traumatic injury with its level-dependent syndromes, and of poliovirus, which selectively destroys anterior horn motor neurons. Selective vulnerability tracks the largest, longest, most metabolically demanding motor and sensory neurons.

ENTRY: 2.7 THE PERIPHERAL GANGLIA

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The peripheral ganglia are overwhelmingly derived from the neural crest, a migratory population that delaminates from the dorsal neural tube and streams to the periphery. The sensory ganglia (dorsal root and much of the trigeminal) come from neural crest, with some cranial sensory ganglia also drawing on ectodermal placodes; the sympathetic chain and prevertebral ganglia come from trunk neural crest; the parasympathetic and enteric ganglia come from vagal, sacral, and cranial crest. Crest identity and target-derived neurotrophins (nerve growth factor for many sensory and sympathetic neurons) direct their survival and wiring.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The sensory ganglia are the latency reservoir of herpesviruses (varicella-zoster in the dorsal root and trigeminal ganglia, reactivating as shingles) and the CASUALTY in sensory neuronopathies (paraneoplastic anti-Hu, Sjogren, cisplatin toxicity) and in Friedreich ataxia. The neural crest origin makes these ganglia and the adrenal medulla the source of neuroblastoma and pheochromocytoma, and of the RASopathy and neurofibromatosis tumours; here the developmental lineage is a CAUSE of tumour predisposition. Failure of crest migration underlies the neurocristopathies, most notably Hirschsprung disease of the enteric ganglia.

ENTRY: 2.7.1 THE PREVERTEBRAL GANGLION INTEGRATIVE CLUSTER (PUC)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The celiac, superior mesenteric, and inferior mesenteric ganglia derive from trunk neural crest and, unusually among sympathetic ganglia, receive convergent input from both the central preganglionic neurons and the enteric nervous system, and they contain interneurons, so they perform genuine peripheral integration. Their crest origin follows the same programme as the sympathetic chain, but their integrative circuitry, including the enteric afferents that let them compute without the brain, is not developmentally characterised in detail, and no precise assembly timeline for this integrative property can be stated honestly.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The prevertebral ganglia are a CASUALTY in autonomic neuropathies (diabetic, amyloid, paraneoplastic) that disrupt gut motility, secretion, and splanchnic blood flow, and in the autonomic failure of multiple system atrophy and pure autonomic failure. They can be the site of neuroblastoma and other crest-derived tumours. There is no named degeneration specific to this integrative cluster as such; its disease role is that of the sympathetic autonomic outflow it carries. This is a deliberately short entry, matching the thin developmental literature on its integrative function.

ENTRY: 2.8 THE ENTERIC NERVOUS SYSTEM

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The enteric nervous system, the myenteric and submucosal plexuses containing on the order of hundreds of millions of neurons, is colonised by vagal neural crest cells that enter the foregut and migrate in a rostral-to-caudal wave along the entire gut, with a contribution from sacral crest to the hindgut. This is one of the longest and most demanding crest migrations, and it depends critically on the RET receptor and its ligand GDNF, on the endothelin-three and endothelin-receptor-B signalling system, and on the transcription factors SOX10 and PHOX2B (worked out in mouse and other models, rodent and model data). The wave must reach the distal colon; if it stalls, the terminal gut is left aganglionic.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Failure of the crest migration to reach the distal bowel is the CAUSE of Hirschsprung disease, an aganglionosis of the terminal colon (RET, EDNRB, SOX10 mutations) that produces functional obstruction in the newborn. The enteric nervous system is a CASUALTY in Chagas disease, where Trypanosoma cruzi destroys enteric neurons and causes megacolon and megaoesophagus, and in achalasia (loss of inhibitory myenteric neurons of the lower oesophageal sphincter). It is an early and possibly initiating site of alpha-synuclein pathology in Parkinson disease, supporting the gut-first hypothesis, so here it may be both a MARKER accessible by biopsy and an early station in the spread.

ENTRY: 2.9 THE ENDOCRINE GLANDS AS LAYER 9 STRUCTURES

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The endocrine glands have mixed developmental origins that the framework treats on the same footing as brain nuclei. The anterior pituitary forms from Rathke's pouch, an upgrowth of oral ectoderm, while the posterior pituitary is a downgrowth of hypothalamic neural tissue, so the gland is a fusion of two germ layers. The adrenal medulla and much of the sympathoadrenal system are neural crest, its chromaffin cells being modified sympathetic neurons that receive direct preganglionic input, which is the strongest single argument for treating the neuroendocrine system as one. The thyroid, parathyroids, and endocrine pancreas arise from endoderm; the gonads from the genital ridge. Thyroid hormone is indispensable to brain development itself.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: These glands are CAUSE sites in the classic endocrinopathies: type one diabetes (autoimmune destruction of pancreatic beta cells, a CASUALTY that causes the disease), Addison disease and Cushing syndrome (adrenal), Graves and Hashimoto disease (thyroid), and the pituitary adenomas. Congenital hypothyroidism, if untreated, causes severe developmental brain injury, making the thyroid a developmental CAUSE of neurological disability, which is why newborn screening exists. The neural-crest adrenal medulla and sympathetic system give rise to pheochromocytoma and neuroblastoma. The hypothalamic-pituitary-adrenal axis is a MARKER and modulator across stress-related psychiatric disease.

ENTRY: 2.10 THE LARGE-SCALE COGNITIVE NETWORKS (LAYER 10)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The large-scale networks are not born fully formed; they emerge and segregate across development in a stereotyped order. Functional imaging of infants shows a maturation sequence that runs from the primary sensorimotor and auditory networks, to the visual network, then to the attention and default-mode networks, and finally to the executive control network, so the networks that serve raw perception mature first and those that serve abstract control mature last. Across childhood and adolescence the networks shift from a more locally clustered organisation to a more distributed one, strengthening long-range within-network connections and, importantly, developing anticorrelation (segregation) between competing networks.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Because these networks assemble late and over a long window, they are the level at which many developmental and psychiatric disorders are now described. Failures of network segregation and integration are implicated as MARKERS, and sometimes as mechanisms, in autism, attention deficit hyperactivity disorder, schizophrenia, and mood disorders, and network breakdown is a hallmark of the neurodegenerative diseases, each of which tends to target a specific network (see the individual entries). The general lesson is that disorders once localised to regions are increasingly read as disorders of network coupling.

ENTRY: 2.10.1 THE DEFAULT MODE NETWORK (DMN)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The default mode network is one of the later networks to mature. Its nodes exist in infancy but are only weakly and incompletely connected; the strong integration between the posterior cingulate and medial prefrontal hubs, and the anticorrelation between the default mode network and the task-positive executive network, develop across childhood and adolescence, with marked change between roughly ages ten and thirteen. This late maturation parallels the development of self-referential thought, autobiographical memory, and mentalizing, and its coupling continues to be shaped by experience and disrupted by adversity.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The default mode network is the network whose topography most closely matches the deposition of amyloid and the hypometabolism of Alzheimer disease, and its connectivity breaks down early in the illness, making it both a CASUALTY and an early MARKER. It is implicated in depression, where excessive default-mode connectivity is linked to rumination, a CAUSE-level contributor to the disorder's cognitive style. Altered default-mode connectivity is a consistent finding in schizophrenia (impaired self-other boundaries) and in autism (altered mentalizing), where it functions largely as a MARKER of the underlying condition.

ENTRY: 2.10.2 THE SALIENCE NETWORK / VENTRAL ATTENTION NETWORK (SN / VAN)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The salience network, anchored in the anterior insula and dorsal anterior cingulate, matures across childhood and adolescence and is critical because it switches control between the inward default-mode and the outward executive networks. A distinctive developmental feature is that the anterior insula and anterior cingulate contain the von Economo (spindle) neurons, a large projection cell class that appears late in gestation and increases postnatally, and which is prominent in humans and great apes; these cells are thought to support the fast interoceptive and social signalling of the network.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The salience network is the CASUALTY that defines behavioural-variant frontotemporal dementia, which selectively targets the von Economo neurons of the anterior insula and anterior cingulate early, producing the loss of empathy and social conduct; here the selective vulnerability of a specific cell class is the hallmark. Salience-network dysfunction is a CAUSE-level contributor in psychosis (aberrant assignment of salience to irrelevant stimuli, a leading model of delusion formation) and is implicated in addiction, anxiety, and pain. Its switching failure is invoked as a MARKER across several psychiatric conditions.

ENTRY: 2.10.3 THE DORSAL ATTENTION NETWORK (DAN)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The dorsal attention network, centred on the frontal eye fields and intraparietal sulcus, supports the voluntary, goal-directed aiming of attention. It builds on the earlier-maturing sensorimotor and visual systems and continues to strengthen through childhood as top-down attentional control improves; its top-down gain over visual cortex and its coordination with the executive network are refined into adolescence in step with the maturation of sustained and selective attention.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The dorsal attention network is a CASUALTY in disorders of attention. Its dysfunction contributes to the impaired top-down attentional control seen in attention deficit hyperactivity disorder and in the attentional deficits of many neuropsychiatric conditions, where it is largely a MARKER. Damage to its parietal nodes contributes to disorders of spatial attention, although the dramatic clinical neglect syndrome localises more to the right-lateralised ventral attention system and right parietal cortex than to the dorsal network itself, a distinction worth preserving. It has no primary degenerative disease of its own.

ENTRY: 2.10.4 THE FRONTOPARIETAL CONTROL NETWORK / CENTRAL EXECUTIVE NETWORK (FPN / CEN)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The frontoparietal control network is the last major network to mature, tracking the prolonged development of the prefrontal cortex, whose myelination and synaptic refinement continue into the third decade of life. Its dorsolateral prefrontal and parietal hubs strengthen their coupling and their control over the other networks across adolescence, which parallels the well-documented late maturation of working memory, cognitive control, and reasoning. Its reliance on prefrontal dopamine, which follows an inverted-U dose-response, makes its function sensitive to dopaminergic tone.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The frontoparietal network is a CASUALTY and a MARKER across a wide range of psychiatric disease: reduced frontoparietal control connectivity is among the most reproducible findings in schizophrenia (a substrate of the cognitive deficits), and it is implicated in depression, bipolar disorder, and attention deficit hyperactivity disorder. Because it underlies fluid intelligence and cognitive control, its disruption is a common final pathway for cognitive impairment. Its late, prolonged maturation makes it especially sensitive to insults of adolescence and to the emergence of psychosis in that window.

ENTRY: 2.10.5 THE CINGULO-OPERCULAR NETWORK (CON)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The cingulo-opercular network, anchored in the dorsal anterior cingulate and anterior insula/operculum, holds a task-set steady across time and provides tonic alertness. It overlaps anatomically with the salience network and is distinguished by its sustained, tonic maintenance signal. Developmentally it separates from the more transient control networks across childhood, and this segregation of stable-maintenance from moment-to-moment control is part of how children come to sustain attention over longer periods.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The cingulo-opercular network is implicated as a MARKER in attention deficit hyperactivity disorder and in the lapses of sustained attention seen with fatigue, sleep loss, and ageing. Its error-monitoring function links it to obsessive-compulsive disorder and anxiety, where over-active performance monitoring is a CAUSE-level contributor to the symptom of doubt. It shares the von Economo-bearing anterior insula and cingulate with the salience network and is therefore partly a CASUALTY in behavioural-variant frontotemporal dementia. It has no primary degeneration uniquely its own.

ENTRY: 2.10.6 THE LANGUAGE NETWORK

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The language network, spanning Broca's and Wernicke's areas and their connecting arcuate fasciculus, develops under a strong critical period: infants are born able to discriminate the sounds of all languages and narrow to their native language within the first year, and native-like grammatical competence depends on exposure within roughly the first decade, after which acquisition is measurably harder. The network is usually left-lateralised, but that lateralisation is not fixed at birth and can shift to the right hemisphere if the left is injured early, a striking developmental plasticity. Myelination of the arcuate fasciculus continues through childhood.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The language network is the CASUALTY in aphasia from left-hemisphere stroke (Broca's, Wernicke's, conduction, and global aphasias mapping onto its nodes and tracts) and in primary progressive aphasia, a neurodegenerative disorder that selectively erodes the network. It is implicated in developmental language disorder and in developmental dyslexia, where atypical development of the network's reading-related components is a CAUSE-level substrate. Because of early plasticity, childhood injury to the network is far better compensated than adult injury, a selective-vulnerability feature that runs opposite to most systems.

ENTRY: 2.10.7 THE SENSORIMOTOR NETWORK (SMN)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The sensorimotor network is among the earliest networks to mature, present and relatively well organised in the newborn, consistent with the early functional need to move and to sense the body. Its somatotopic organisation (the hand, foot, and mouth sub-networks) is laid down early and refined by motor experience, and its coupling to the cerebellum and basal ganglia matures as skilled movement develops through childhood. Motor learning continues to reshape it throughout life.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The sensorimotor network is the CASUALTY in stroke affecting the motor or somatosensory cortex, producing hemiparesis and sensory loss, and its recovery depends on the plasticity of the surviving network. Its cortical motor neurons degenerate in amyotrophic lateral sclerosis (upper motor neuron signs), and it is disrupted in movement disorders through its basal-ganglia and cerebellar loops. Altered sensorimotor connectivity is reported as a MARKER in autism and schizophrenia. It has no single primary degeneration of the network as a whole; most disease reaching it is focal (stroke, tumour) or part of a wider motor-system disease.

ENTRY: 2.10.8 THE VISUAL NETWORK

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The visual network matures early but has one of the most sharply defined critical periods in the brain. The ocular dominance and orientation architecture of primary visual cortex is refined by patterned visual experience in early postnatal life; deprivation of one eye during this window permanently reorganises the cortex in favour of the open eye, the classic Hubel and Wiesel result (established in cat and monkey and confirmed clinically, carnivore and primate data). This is the mechanism behind human amblyopia, and it is why early treatment of childhood cataract or strabismus is urgent.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The visual network is a developmental CAUSE-and-CASUALTY site in amblyopia, where early monocular deprivation drives a lasting cortical reorganisation. It is a CASUALTY in cortical blindness from occipital stroke (with the residual collicular route giving blindsight), and in posterior cortical atrophy, a visual-predominant variant of Alzheimer disease that selectively degrades the higher visual areas and dorsal and ventral streams, producing visual disorientation with relatively preserved memory. Its critical-period plasticity means the timing of insult, not just its severity, determines the outcome.

ENTRY: 2.10.9 THE AUDITORY NETWORK

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The auditory network matures early: the cochlea and brainstem auditory pathway are functional in the third trimester, so the fetus hears in utero, and the tonotopic organisation of auditory cortex is present near birth and refined by experience. There is a sensitive period for auditory and speech-sound learning in the first years, which is why the timing of cochlear implantation in congenitally deaf children strongly determines the outcome: earlier implantation gives the developing auditory cortex the input it needs before the sensitive period closes.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The auditory network is a CASUALTY in cortical deafness and auditory agnosia from bilateral temporal lesions, and its higher stages are involved in central auditory processing disorders. Aberrant activity in the network is implicated in tinnitus, where the loss of peripheral input drives maladaptive central reorganisation, a CAUSE-level central mechanism. Deprivation during the sensitive period (untreated congenital deafness) impairs the network's development, a developmental CASUALTY whose reversibility depends on the timing of restored input. It has no primary neurodegeneration of the network itself.

ENTRY: 2.10.10 THE LIMBIC NETWORK

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The limbic network of value and feeling, spanning orbitofrontal and ventromedial prefrontal cortex, temporal pole, amygdala, hippocampus, and ventral striatum, develops on a long trajectory in which the subcortical emotional nodes mature relatively early while their prefrontal regulators mature late. This developmental mismatch, an early-online limbic drive under still-immature prefrontal control, is a leading account of adolescent risk-taking and emotional intensity. The network is shaped by early experience and is highly sensitive to early-life stress, which alters amygdala and prefrontal connectivity lastingly.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The limbic network is the CASUALTY, CAUSE, or MARKER across the affective and stress-related disorders: it is implicated in depression (altered orbitofrontal and ventromedial valuation), in post-traumatic stress disorder (amygdala over-reactivity with impaired prefrontal regulation), in anxiety, and in addiction (dysregulated ventral-striatal reward). Its medial temporal nodes are the epicentre of temporal lobe epilepsy and are the earliest cortical target of Alzheimer disease. Early-life adversity acting on this network is a developmental CAUSE-level risk factor for later psychiatric disease.

ENTRY: 2.11 THE COGNITIVE SYSTEMS AND PATHWAYS (LAYER 10)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: These constructs are recurring pathways and loops rather than single regions or whole networks, so their assembly is the assembly of the connections among their member regions: the cortico-striatal, thalamocortical, and limbic tracts that form them are laid down prenatally and myelinated and refined across childhood and adolescence in the same protracted trajectory as the regions they link. Their functional emergence therefore tracks the maturation of their slowest component, which for the executive and limbic loops is the late-maturing prefrontal cortex.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Because these are circuits, their disorders are circuit disorders: a lesion at any node can produce the syndrome, and the same loop can be pushed in opposite directions by opposite pathology (as in Parkinson versus Huntington disease within the basal-ganglia loops). They are treated in detail in the individual entries below. The general point is that these pathways are the level at which the connectome framework must instantiate loops and rules rather than boxes, and where circuit-level treatments such as deep brain stimulation act.

ENTRY: 2.11.1 THE CORTICO-BASAL-GANGLIA-THALAMO-CORTICAL LOOPS (CBGTC)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The cortico-basal-ganglia-thalamo-cortical loops assemble as the cortex, striatum, pallidum, subthalamic nucleus, substantia nigra, and thalamus wire together. The striatum develops from the ganglionic eminences of the ventral telencephalon, its projection neurons migrating radially while many of the cortex's inhibitory interneurons migrate tangentially from the same eminences. The segregation into parallel motor, oculomotor, cognitive, and limbic loops is preserved from early development, and the dopaminergic innervation that tunes them extends and matures into adolescence, especially in the prefrontal-linked cognitive loop.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: These loops are the CASUALTY in the basal-ganglia diseases, each striking a different point of the loop: Parkinson disease (loss of nigral dopamine tips the loops toward suppression of movement), Huntington disease (loss of striatal neurons, a CAUSE from the huntingtin mutation), dystonia, and Tourette syndrome. The cognitive and limbic loops are implicated in obsessive-compulsive disorder and in addiction. Because the loops are anatomically segregated, deep brain stimulation can be targeted to one loop, which is why it treats motor Parkinsonism and some psychiatric disorders through the same circuit logic.

ENTRY: 2.11.2 THE DIRECT, INDIRECT, AND HYPERDIRECT PATHWAYS

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The three basal-ganglia pathways are defined by the striatal neuron classes and their receptor complements: the direct pathway from dopamine-D1 striatal neurons, the indirect pathway from dopamine-D2 neurons through the external pallidum and subthalamic nucleus, and the hyperdirect pathway from cortex straight to the subthalamic nucleus. The D1 and D2 medium spiny neurons and their opposite responses to dopamine are specified during striatal development, and the balance between the go and no-go pathways matures with dopaminergic innervation, which is relevant to the maturation of impulse control.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: In Parkinson disease, dopamine loss overactivates the indirect (no-go) pathway and underactivates the direct (go) pathway, producing bradykinesia; here the pathways are the CASUALTY of nigral degeneration. In Huntington disease the indirect-pathway D2 neurons degenerate first, releasing movement into chorea, a CAUSE traced to the mutation and a striking selective vulnerability of one pathway's neurons. A subthalamic lesion abolishing the hyperdirect and indirect brake causes hemiballismus. The hyperdirect stop pathway underlies the deep-brain-stimulation target and is implicated in the impaired action-cancellation of impulse-control disorders.

ENTRY: 2.11.3 THE PAPEZ CIRCUIT

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The Papez circuit is the closed limbic loop, hippocampus to fornix to mammillary bodies to anterior thalamus to cingulate and back through the entorhinal cortex. Its member structures develop as part of the limbic and medial temporal system; the hippocampal formation has a protracted development with ongoing dentate neurogenesis, and the circuit's connections mature across infancy in step with the emergence of episodic memory, which is why the earliest years are covered by infantile amnesia. The mammillothalamic tract and fornix are white-matter links whose myelination continues into childhood.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The Papez circuit is the circuit whose breakage at any node produces amnesia, so it is a CASUALTY across several diseases: Korsakoff syndrome (thiamine deficiency destroying the mammillary bodies and anterior thalamus, a CAUSE from nutritional or alcohol-related depletion), thalamic and fornix strokes, and Alzheimer disease, which attacks the entorhinal-hippocampal entry to the loop earliest. Because the loop is serial, a single well-placed lesion (a small mammillothalamic or anterior thalamic infarct) can produce a dense amnesia out of all proportion to its size, a selective vulnerability of a bottleneck circuit.

ENTRY: 2.11.4 THE TRIPARTITE SYNAPSE

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The tripartite synapse, the presynaptic terminal, the postsynaptic cell, and the enwrapping astrocyte, assembles as astrocytes are generated after the main wave of neurogenesis and then elaborate their fine perisynaptic processes during the peak period of synapse formation. Astrocyte-secreted factors (thrombospondins, hevin, glypicans) actively induce and mature synapses, so astrocytes are not passive latecomers but participants in synapse formation, and their D-serine supply gates the plasticity of the postsynaptic receptors. This assembly is largely postnatal and experience-sensitive (much of the mechanism established in rodent, rodent data).

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The tripartite synapse is a CASUALTY and a mechanism in disorders of synaptic function: astrocyte dysfunction contributes to epilepsy (impaired glutamate and potassium buffering lowering seizure threshold, a CAUSE-level contribution), to Alzheimer disease (reactive astrocytes around plaques), and to the D-serine and glutamate abnormalities implicated in schizophrenia. Because the astrocyte gates plasticity, its failure impairs learning independently of neuronal loss, which is the framework's point that a model of neurons alone omits a partner that gates learning. Astrocytes are also the cell of origin of astrocytomas.

ENTRY: 2.11.5 THE GLIA AS COGNITIVE PARTICIPANTS (ASTROCYTES, MICROGLIA, OLIGODENDROCYTES)

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The three glial classes have distinct origins and timings. Astrocytes and oligodendrocytes are macroglia generated from neural-tube progenitors, largely after neurogenesis, with oligodendrocyte precursors arising in ventral then dorsal waves and myelinating in a protracted, experience-dependent process that continues into the third decade in humans. Microglia are entirely different: they are not neural at all but derive from the embryonic yolk sac and colonise the brain early, before birth, then serve as its resident immune and pruning cells. Microglia sculpt circuits by activity-dependent synaptic pruning, and adaptive myelination by oligodendrocytes tunes conduction speed with learning.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Oligodendrocytes are the CASUALTY in the demyelinating diseases, above all multiple sclerosis (autoimmune attack on myelin) and the leukodystrophies (inherited, a CAUSE), and their failure slows and desynchronises conduction. Microglia are increasingly seen as a CAUSE-level driver of neurodegeneration and psychiatric disease: complement-tagged over-pruning of synapses is implicated in schizophrenia (the C4 risk gene acts through microglial pruning) and in Alzheimer disease, while under- or mis-pruning is implicated in autism. Astrocytes participate as above. The larger point is that glia are not support cells but active CAUSES and CASUALTIES across the disease spectrum.

================================================================================

PART THREE: THE 301 NEURON CHAPTERS, BY DEVELOPMENTAL LINEAGE CLASS

Covered by class rather than individually, with a keyed index mapping every one of the 301 chapters to its class, per the scope decision stated in the front matter.

THE 301 NEURON CHAPTERS COVERED BY DEVELOPMENTAL LINEAGE CLASS

This companion groups the 301 neuron-type chapters into 27 developmental lineage classes. The grain is developmental origin, which is a property of the lineage that produces a cell rather than of the terminal type the cell becomes. Two neurons that fire very differently in the adult brain can share one progenitor domain, one transcription-factor cascade, and one birth window, and it is that shared history the class captures.

A note on honesty. Where a birth window is drawn from rodent fate-mapping it is marked as rodent, because rodent embryonic timings do not convert cleanly to human gestation. Where a lineage is genuinely unsettled, the entry says so by name and states what evidence would settle it. Several chapters near the end of Volume 5 name cells by what they compute rather than by where they come from. Those are refused as a lineage and explained in Class 27.

Abbreviations spelled out on first use are kept in parentheses for reuse. Germ zones referenced repeatedly: the ventricular zone (VZ) is the proliferative sheet lining the embryonic ventricles, the subventricular zone (SVZ) is the second proliferative layer above it, the ganglionic eminences are transient subpallial bulges (medial, MGE; lateral, LGE; caudal, CGE), the rhombic lip is the dorsal edge of the hindbrain, and the neural crest is the migratory population that peels off the dorsal neural tube.

================================================================================

PART A. THE 27 DEVELOPMENTAL LINEAGE CLASSES

CLASS 1. CORTICAL EXCITATORY PROJECTION NEURONS (DORSAL PALLIUM RADIAL GLIA LINEAGE)

This class covers VOL1 chapters 1 to 16: the intra-telencephalic, extra-telencephalic, cortico-thalamic and near-projecting classes, generic pyramidal cells, spiny stellate and star pyramid cells, and the large deep-layer specialists (Betz, Meynert, Von Economo, and fork cells).

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: These glutamatergic neurons arise from radial glia in the ventricular zone of the dorsal pallium (the roof of the embryonic telencephalon). The specifying cascade is sequential: Pax6 in the radial glial progenitor, Tbr2 (Eomes) as the cell becomes an intermediate progenitor in the subventricular zone, then Tbr1 as it becomes a postmitotic neuron, with Ctip2 (Bcl11b), Fezf2 and Satb2 sorting deep-layer versus upper-layer and corticofugal versus callosal identity. Birth follows an inside-out order: deep-layer neurons first, upper-layer neurons last, in the rodent from about embryonic day 11 to embryonic day 17 (rodent), with a comparable human window spanning roughly the sixth to the eighteenth gestational week. Migration is radial along glial fibres. Maturation of dendrites, spines and myelinated axons continues for months after birth in rodent and for years in human. No members are generated after birth. There is no meaningful physiological replacement of these neurons once lost.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: As the principal output cells of the cortex these are CASUALTY neurons in most cortical neurodegeneration: layer-specific pyramidal loss in Alzheimer disease, and selective vulnerability of the giant Betz cells and other large pyramidal outputs in amyotrophic lateral sclerosis and frontotemporal dementia. Von Economo and fork cells are early CASUALTY targets in behavioural-variant frontotemporal dementia, an established selective vulnerability. In malformations of cortical development (lissencephaly, focal cortical dysplasia) a migration or specification defect makes these neurons a CAUSE. Their layer identity is a reliable MARKER used to read cortical damage.

CLASS 2. CAJAL-RETZIUS AND SUBPLATE TRANSIENT CORTICAL NEURONS

This class covers VOL1 chapters 17 and 18: Cajal-Retzius cells and subplate neurons.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: Both are among the earliest-born cortical neurons and both are largely scaffolding populations that guide the assembly of the permanent cortex. Cajal-Retzius cells arise not from the general pallial sheet but from focal sources at the borders of the pallium, chiefly the cortical hem (caudomedial, adjacent to the hippocampal primordium), with contributions from the pallial septum and ventral pallium, and they spread by tangential migration across the marginal zone. Their defining product is Reelin, the guidance protein that sets the inside-out order used by Class 1. Subplate neurons are the first cortical-plate-related neurons born, sitting below the developing cortical plate and pioneering thalamocortical wiring. In rodent both are generated very early, before the bulk of Class 1. Most Cajal-Retzius cells are eliminated during the early postnatal period; subplate neurons largely die back, though a minority persist as interstitial white-matter neurons. Neither is replaced.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Loss of Reelin signalling is a CAUSE of lissencephaly with cerebellar hypoplasia (RELN mutations) and reeler-type layering defects. Subplate injury is a CASUALTY mechanism in periventricular white-matter injury of prematurity, where these neurons are selectively vulnerable during the window they are most numerous. Reelin reduction is also a MARKER in schizophrenia cortex without being established as a cause.

CLASS 3. MEDIAL GANGLIONIC EMINENCE CORTICAL INTERNEURONS

This class covers VOL1 chapters 19 to 24: cortical basket cells, chandelier (axo-axonic) cells, translaminar cells, Martinotti and non-Martinotti cells, and SST-CHODL cells.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: These GABAergic interneurons are born in the medial ganglionic eminence (and the adjacent preoptic area) of the subpallium, not in the cortex where they end up. The cascade is Nkx2-1 in the progenitor, which activates Lhx6, which is necessary for nearly all parvalbumin-expressing and somatostatin-expressing cortical interneurons. They are generated in the rodent across roughly embryonic day 9.5 to embryonic day 15, peaking near embryonic day 13.5 (rodent), then migrate a long tangential route into the cortex before turning radially to their layer. Functional maturation of the fast-spiking parvalbumin cells, including their perineuronal nets, is slow and continues well into postnatal life. None are generated after early development in primates, and they are not replaced.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Parvalbumin fast-spiking cells are a leading CAUSE candidate in the excitation-inhibition imbalance of schizophrenia and are a CASUALTY of that same disorder (reduced parvalbumin and GAD67). Chandelier and basket interneuron dysfunction is a CAUSE in several genetic epilepsies. Interneuronopathies from Nkx2-1 or ARX mutations make the whole class a CAUSE of epilepsy with intellectual disability. Reduced parvalbumin interneuron markers are a robust MARKER of cortical disease.

CLASS 4. CAUDAL GANGLIONIC EMINENCE CORTICAL INTERNEURONS

This class covers VOL1 chapters 25 to 36: bipolar cells, double bouquet cells, VIP-type small basket cells, cholecystokinin-expressing basket cells, cortical neurogliaform cells, single bouquet cells, canopy cells, alpha7 cells, LAMP5-LHX6 cells, rosehip cells, bitufted cells, and horizontal cells of layer 1.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: These interneurons come mainly from the caudal ganglionic eminence, which is Nkx2-1 negative and marked by Prox1 and Sp8, and they ultimately express the serotonin receptor 5HT3a. They include the vasoactive intestinal peptide (VIP) interneurons and reelin-positive multipolar and neurogliaform cells. In rodent the caudal ganglionic eminence contributes interneurons later than the medial eminence, from about embryonic day 13.5 onward and extending latest into development (rodent). Migration is tangential then radial, with a bias toward upper layers. They are not replaced.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: This class is a departure-rich group for disease because several members straddle origins. As a class it is a CASUALTY in disorders that thin upper-layer inhibition, and VIP interneuron dysfunction is an emerging CAUSE candidate in autism-spectrum circuit imbalance. The human-enriched rosehip cell has no settled disease profile; that gap is honest and reflects how recently it was described. LAMP5-LHX6 cells are a departure: they carry Lhx6 and are MGE-derived despite their transcriptomic kinship here, so their vulnerability tracks Class 3.

CLASS 5. HIPPOCAMPAL, DENTATE AND ENTORHINAL EXCITATORY NEURONS

This class covers VOL1 chapters 37 to 45: dentate granule cells, semilunar granule cells, mossy cells, CA3, CA2 and CA1 pyramidal cells, subicular pyramidal cells, entorhinal stellate cells, and entorhinal fan cells.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: These glutamatergic neurons arise from the medial pallium (the hippocampal and entorhinal ventricular zone) under a cascade sharing Pax6, Tbr2 and Tbr1 with the neocortex but shaped by Prox1 in the dentate granule lineage. Most are born embryonically in an inside-out-like sequence (rodent), but the dentate gyrus is the standout: its granule cells are generated across a long window that continues after birth. In rodent, dentate granule neurogenesis persists lifelong in the subgranular zone, with Tbr2-positive intermediate progenitors producing new Prox1-positive granule cells. Whether adult hippocampal neurogenesis persists at a meaningful rate in adult humans is genuinely unsettled: some groups report ongoing production into old age and others report it falls to undetectable after childhood, and the disagreement turns on tissue fixation and marker specificity. That controversy is reported here rather than resolved.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: CA1 pyramidal cells are the textbook CASUALTY of global ischaemia and are among the earliest CASUALTY targets in Alzheimer disease; entorhinal stellate and fan cells in layer 2 are the earliest CASUALTY site of Alzheimer tau pathology, an established selective vulnerability. Dentate granule cell dispersion is a CAUSE-adjacent MARKER in temporal lobe epilepsy. CA2 is relatively resistant, itself a useful MARKER contrast.

CLASS 6. HIPPOCAMPAL GABAERGIC INTERNEURONS

This class covers VOL1 chapters 46 to 56 and VOL2 chapters 57 to 59: parvalbumin and cholecystokinin basket cells, axo-axonic cells, bistratified cells, oriens-lacunosum moleculare cells, ivy and hippocampal neurogliaform cells, the HIPP, HICAP, MOPP and trilaminar cells, back-projection cells, hippocampo-septal projecting cells, and radiatum-retrohippocampal cells.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: Like their neocortical counterparts these interneurons are born in the ganglionic eminences and preoptic area, not in the hippocampus. Parvalbumin and somatostatin subtypes (including the oriens-lacunosum moleculare and hippocampo-septal cells) are Nkx2-1 then Lhx6 medial-eminence cells; cholecystokinin, ivy and neurogliaform subtypes derive substantially from the caudal eminence. Fate-mapping confirms hippocampal somatostatin interneurons originate in the embryonic medial eminence and preoptic area (rodent). Birth window and tangential migration match Classes 3 and 4 (rodent embryonic day 9.5 to 15). They are not replaced.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Loss of hippocampal parvalbumin and somatostatin interneurons is a CAUSE of the disinhibition that sustains temporal lobe epilepsy and is a CASUALTY of the same seizures, a two-way relationship the per-disorder classification is meant to capture. Somatostatin interneuron loss is an early MARKER in Alzheimer models.

CLASS 7. AMYGDALA, CLAUSTRUM AND OLFACTORY CORTEX NEURONS

This class covers VOL2 chapters 60 to 65: amygdala principal neurons, intercalated cells, central amygdala neurons, piriform cortex semilunar and superficial pyramidal cells, claustrum projection neurons, and the harm-converging gatekeeper neuron.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: This is a deliberately mixed-origin regional class, because the amygdala is a developmental mosaic. The glutamatergic principal neurons of the basolateral amygdala and the piriform and claustral projection neurons are pallial, sharing the Tbr1-type cascade of Classes 1 and 5. The intercalated cells and the GABAergic central amygdala neurons are subpallial, arising from the dorsal lateral ganglionic eminence and adjacent domains under Dlx and Foxp2. The class is grouped by circuit and region; its origins are stated per member rather than forced into one cascade. Birth is embryonic (rodent). Not replaced.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Basolateral principal neurons are a CAUSE-and-CASUALTY node in anxiety and post-traumatic stress circuits and a CASUALTY in temporal lobe epilepsy and in some tauopathies. The harm-converging gatekeeper neuron (chapter 65) is a speculative composite; its lineage cannot be stated honestly and no timing can be given for it.

CLASS 8. STRIATAL PROJECTION NEURONS (LATERAL GANGLIONIC EMINENCE LINEAGE)

This class covers VOL2 chapters 66 to 68 and chapters 80 to 82: medium spiny neurons, the D1 direct-pathway and D2 indirect-pathway subtypes, nucleus accumbens medium spiny neurons, and the two speculative accumbens neurons named for quieting both pathways and damping dashed hope.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: Medium spiny neurons are the GABAergic output cells of the striatum and are born in the ventral lateral ganglionic eminence. The cascade runs Gsx2 in the progenitor, which upregulates the proneural factor Ascl1 (Mash1), then Dlx1/2, with Ctip2 (Bcl11b) required for medium spiny neuron differentiation and striatal patch-matrix architecture and Foxp1 and Foxp2 marking the lineage and the striosomal compartment. Birth is embryonic with a patch-then-matrix compartmental order (rodent embryonic day 11 onward). Not replaced.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Medium spiny neurons are the primary CASUALTY of Huntington disease, and the indirect-pathway D2 population is selectively vulnerable early, an established selective vulnerability. In Parkinson disease they are CASUALTY-by-deafferentation as their dopamine input fails. The speculative accumbens neurons (chapters 81 and 82) have no established lineage and no honest timing.

CLASS 9. STRIATAL INTERNEURONS

This class covers VOL2 chapters 69 to 73: cholinergic (tonically active) interneurons, parvalbumin fast-spiking interneurons, the somatostatin/nitric-oxide-synthase/neuropeptide-Y low-threshold-spiking interneurons, calretinin interneurons, and tyrosine-hydroxylase-expressing interneurons.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: These interneurons do not share the lateral-eminence origin of the projection neurons they sit among. The large cholinergic interneurons and the parvalbumin and somatostatin GABAergic interneurons derive from the medial ganglionic eminence and preoptic area under Nkx2-1 and Lhx6/Lhx8, arriving by tangential migration. The calretinin interneurons, enriched in the human striatum, derive largely from the caudal eminence. The tyrosine-hydroxylase interneurons have a debated origin. Birth is embryonic (rodent). Not replaced.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Cholinergic interneurons are relatively spared in Huntington disease (a MARKER contrast against the dying medium spiny neurons) but are a CAUSE node in dystonia. Parvalbumin fast-spiking striatal interneuron dysfunction is a CAUSE candidate in Tourette syndrome, where post-mortem counts are reduced, making them also a MARKER.

CLASS 10. PALLIDAL, SUBTHALAMIC, NIGRAL AND MIDBRAIN DOPAMINERGIC NEURONS

This class covers VOL2 chapters 74 to 79: globus pallidus prototypic and arkypallidal neurons, subthalamic nucleus neurons, substantia nigra pars reticulata neurons, substantia nigra pars compacta neurons, and ventral tegmental area neurons.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: This class pools the deep basal ganglia by circuit, and its origins genuinely differ by member, which is stated rather than smoothed. Globus pallidus prototypic neurons are medial-ganglionic-eminence GABAergic cells (Nkx2-1, Lhx6); arkypallidal neurons carry a lateral-eminence signature (Foxp2). The substantia nigra pars reticulata is medial-eminence-related GABAergic. The subthalamic nucleus is a departure: it is diencephalic, arising from the caudal hypothalamic-to-subthalamic domain under Otx2 and Pitx2, not from the subpallium. The substantia nigra pars compacta and the ventral tegmental area are the dopaminergic half of the class and arise from the midbrain floor plate: the cascade is Otx2, then Foxa1/2 and Lmx1a/b in the floor-plate progenitor, then Ngn2 and Nurr1 (Nr4a2) in the postmitotic neuron, with Pitx3 and Engrailed 1/2 for late maturation and survival, born in rodent about embryonic day 10 to embryonic day 13.5 (rodent). Not replaced.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The substantia nigra pars compacta dopamine neurons are the defining CASUALTY of Parkinson disease, with a marked selective vulnerability of the ventral tier over the ventral tegmental area, and their loss is the disease-defining MARKER. Subthalamic overactivity is a CAUSE of parkinsonian rigidity and the target of deep-brain stimulation. Ventral tegmental dopamine neurons are a CAUSE-and-CASUALTY node in addiction.

CLASS 11. DIENCEPHALIC THALAMIC COMPLEX (THALAMUS, PRETHALAMUS, EPITHALAMUS)

This class covers VOL2 chapters 83 to 89: thalamocortical relay neurons, core and matrix relay neurons, thalamic local interneurons, thalamic reticular nucleus neurons, habenular neurons, and zona incerta neurons.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The excitatory relay neurons arise from prosomere 2 of the diencephalon under Gbx2, which maintains thalamic identity and represses habenular character; virtually all thalamic neurons are born between rodent embryonic day 10.5 and embryonic day 16.5 (rodent). Two members are departures within the class: the habenula is epithalamic (also prosomere 2 but Gbx2-repressed), and the thalamic reticular nucleus, the local interneurons and the zona incerta are prethalamic and GABAergic (prosomere 3, a Dlx domain). The class is diencephalic throughout, which is why they are kept together. Not replaced.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Thalamic relay neurons are CASUALTY in fatal familial insomnia (anterior and dorsomedial nuclei) and in thalamic stroke. Thalamic reticular neurons are a CAUSE node in absence epilepsy through spindle and spike-wave generation. Habenular hyperactivity is a CAUSE candidate in depression.

CLASS 12. HYPOTHALAMIC NEUROENDOCRINE AND NEUROMODULATORY NEURONS

This class covers VOL2 chapters 90 to 112: magnocellular neurosecretory cells and their oxytocin and vasopressin subtypes, parvocellular neurosecretory cells, the releasing-hormone neurons (corticotropin-releasing, thyrotropin-releasing, gonadotropin-releasing, growth-hormone-releasing), somatostatin and tuberoinfundibular dopamine neurons, the arcuate AgRP/NPY and POMC/CART neurons and paraventricular melanocortin-4-receptor neurons, orexin and melanin-concentrating-hormone neurons, tuberomammillary histaminergic neurons, KNDy neurons, suprachiasmatic clock neurons, preautonomic and preoptic thermoregulatory neurons, mammillary body neurons, and two speculative cells (a thirst-sampling and a novelty neuron).

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: These neurons arise from the hypothalamic neuroepithelium under a branching network of factors: Nkx2-1 and Rax broadly, with Otp and Sim1 essential for the paraventricular and supraoptic magnocellular and parvocellular neuroendocrine cells, and Dlx1/2 with Otp balancing arcuate GHRH and AgRP production. Birth is embryonic (rodent), with peptidergic identity refined perinatally. One clear departure: gonadotropin-releasing-hormone neurons (chapter 96) are not born in the hypothalamus at all but in the olfactory placode, migrating into the brain along the terminal nerve, a route they share with chapter 227. Not replaced.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Orexin neurons are the defining CASUALTY of narcolepsy type 1, an autoimmune destruction, and their loss is the diagnostic MARKER (low cerebrospinal-fluid orexin). AgRP and POMC neuron dysfunction is a CAUSE of monogenic obesity (POMC and MC4R mutations). Suprachiasmatic neuron degeneration is a MARKER of circadian breakdown in dementia. Gonadotropin-releasing-hormone neuron migration failure is a CAUSE of Kallmann syndrome.

CLASS 13. CEREBELLAR GABAERGIC NEURONS (VENTRICULAR ZONE, PTF1A LINEAGE)

This class covers VOL2 chapters 113, 115 and 116, and VOL3 chapters 117, 118, 120, 121, 123, 124, 125 and 126: Purkinje cells, Golgi cells, Lugaro cells, globular cells, candelabrum cells, stellate and cerebellar basket cells, the GABAergic nucleo-olivary neurons, glycinergic neurons and small local interneurons of the deep nuclei, and the speculative hush-lifting learning neuron.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: Every GABAergic neuron of the cerebellum arises from the cerebellar ventricular zone, defined by the transcription factor Ptf1a, which is necessary and sufficient for the GABAergic fate and mutually represses the glutamatergic determinant Atoh1 next door. Purkinje cells are born first, at the ventricular zone in rodent about embryonic day 11 to embryonic day 13 (rodent), and migrate radially to form their monolayer. The molecular-layer interneurons (stellate, basket) are born later, postnatally in rodent, from a Ptf1a-derived pool in the prospective white matter. Not replaced.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Purkinje cells are the central CASUALTY of the hereditary spinocerebellar ataxias and of alcohol- and paraneoplastic cerebellar degeneration, an established selective vulnerability, and their loss is the defining MARKER of cerebellar cortical disease. Purkinje number reduction is a replicated MARKER in autism post-mortem tissue.

CLASS 14. CEREBELLAR GLUTAMATERGIC NEURONS (RHOMBIC LIP, ATOH1 LINEAGE)

This class covers VOL2 chapter 114 and VOL3 chapters 119 and 122: cerebellar granule cells, unipolar brush cells, and the large glutamatergic projection neurons of the deep cerebellar nuclei.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: All glutamatergic cerebellar neurons arise from the upper rhombic lip under Atoh1 (Math1). In rodent, Atoh1 directs the deep-nuclei projection neurons first (about embryonic day 10.5 to embryonic day 13.5) and then the external granule layer (embryonic day 13.5 to birth). The granule cells are the standout for postnatal generation: the external granule layer is a secondary germinal zone that proliferates vigorously after birth (rodent, roughly the first two postnatal weeks; in human, into the first postnatal year) before the cells migrate inward past the Purkinje layer. Cerebellar granule cells are therefore the largest population of neurons in the brain and are generated substantially after birth. They are not replaced once mature.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The proliferating external granule layer is the cell of origin of medulloblastoma, making this lineage a CAUSE compartment when Sonic hedgehog signalling is deregulated. Granule cells are CASUALTY in some cerebellar ataxias but are generally more resistant than Purkinje cells, a MARKER contrast.

CLASS 15. CRANIAL MOTOR NEURONS

This class covers VOL3 chapters 127, 128, 129 and 131: general somatic efferent motor neurons, special visceral (branchial) efferent motor neurons, general visceral efferent preganglionic parasympathetic neurons, and mesencephalic trigeminal nucleus neurons.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: Cranial motor neurons are born in the ventral hindbrain in a rhombomere-segmented pattern. Two programs split the class: somatic efferent motor neurons (to eye and tongue muscles) use Isl1 with Lhx3 and Hb9 (Mnx1), the same somatic-motor code as the spinal cord; branchiomotor and visceral efferent neurons require Phox2b, a direct Hox target, together with Isl1. Birth is embryonic (rodent). One departure: the mesencephalic trigeminal nucleus (chapter 131) is not a motor nucleus but a proprioceptive sensory nucleus that sits inside the midbrain, unusual for a primary sensory neuron; its exact origin (neural crest versus a midbrain neural-plate source) is debated, and that uncertainty is stated rather than hidden. Not replaced.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Cranial somatic motor neurons are a CASUALTY in amyotrophic lateral sclerosis and bulbar palsies, though the oculomotor neurons are notably resistant, a well-known selective sparing that serves as a MARKER contrast. Phox2b-dependent neurons connect to congenital central hypoventilation as a CAUSE.

CLASS 16. BRAINSTEM MONOAMINERGIC, CHOLINERGIC AND MODULATORY NEURONS

This class covers VOL3 chapters 130, 132 to 136, and 157 to 159: sensory relay neurons of the trigeminal, solitary, cochlear and vestibular nuclei, locus coeruleus neurons, raphe nuclei neurons, pedunculopontine and laterodorsal tegmental neurons, ventrolateral medullary adrenergic neurons, periaqueductal grey neurons, and three speculative cells (a body-alarm, a nausea, and a dream-sleep stillness neuron).

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The monoaminergic members share ventral hindbrain origins tied to the floor plate and the isthmic organiser. Serotonergic raphe neurons arise from ventral rhombomeres under Nkx2-2, the ETS factor Pet1 (Fev), Lmx1b and Gata2/3; noradrenergic locus coeruleus neurons arise dorsally in rhombomere 1 under Phox2a/b, Mash1 and Tfap2. Birth is embryonic (rodent). The cholinergic pedunculopontine and laterodorsal tegmental neurons are isthmic/hindbrain. The three speculative cells (chapters 157 to 159) are composites whose lineage cannot be stated honestly. Not replaced.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Locus coeruleus noradrenergic neurons are among the very earliest CASUALTY sites in Alzheimer and Parkinson disease, an established selective vulnerability and a proposed prodromal MARKER. Raphe serotonergic neurons are a CAUSE-and-MARKER node in depression. A load-bearing figure worth guarding: the dorsal raphe contains on the order of 165,000 serotonergic neurons in human, not the roughly 235,000 that is the total neuron count of the nucleus; the two numbers are not interchangeable.

CLASS 17. HINDBRAIN AND MIDBRAIN RELAY, PRECEREBELLAR, AUDITORY AND VESTIBULAR NEURONS

This class covers VOL3 chapters 137 to 156: the cochlear nucleus cell types (spherical and globular bushy, T- and D-stellate, octopus, fusiform, giant, cartwheel), superior olivary complex and olivocochlear efferent neurons, inferior olive neurons, pontine nuclei and reticular formation neurons, the pre-Botzinger and retrotrapezoid respiratory neurons, red nucleus, superior and inferior colliculus, and vestibular nuclei neurons.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The excitatory precerebellar and auditory relay neurons derive largely from the rhombic lip: the ventral cochlear nucleus bushy and stellate cells and the pontine nuclei are Atoh1-dependent lower-rhombic-lip derivatives that migrate long distances. A departure worth flagging: the inferior olive arises from the lower rhombic lip but is Atoh1-independent, developing instead from a Ptf1a-linked domain. The colliculi are midbrain tectum (Otx2, Pax7 dorsally). The respiratory pre-Botzinger and retrotrapezoid neurons are ventral hindbrain, the retrotrapezoid depending on Phox2b for its carbon-dioxide-sensing identity. Birth is embryonic (rodent). Not replaced.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The class is heterogeneous, so disease is per member. Retrotrapezoid Phox2b neurons are a CAUSE of congenital central hypoventilation syndrome. Inferior olive neurons are a CASUALTY in some ataxias and hypertrophy (rather than atrophy) after deafferentation, an unusual MARKER. Superior olivary and cochlear relay neurons are CASUALTY in age-related and noise-induced central auditory decline.

CLASS 18. SPINAL MOTOR NEURONS (PMN DOMAIN LINEAGE)

This class covers VOL3 chapters 160 to 167: alpha motor neurons and their fast-fatigable, fast-fatigue-resistant and slow subtypes, gamma motor neurons and their static and dynamic subtypes, and beta (skeletofusimotor) motor neurons.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: Somatic motor neurons of the spinal cord arise from the pMN progenitor domain of the ventral neural tube, specified by a Sonic hedgehog concentration gradient. The cascade is Olig2 in the progenitor, which drives Ngn2 and then the postmitotic motor code Isl1, Lhx3 and Hb9 (Mnx1). In rodent they are among the earliest-born neurons, about embryonic day 9 to embryonic day 11 (rodent), and they settle into motor columns and pools matched to their target muscles. Gamma motor neurons are born slightly later and depend on distinct trophic support. Not replaced.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Alpha motor neurons are the defining CASUALTY of amyotrophic lateral sclerosis and spinal muscular atrophy, with a well-established selective vulnerability of the fast-fatigable subtype early and relative sparing of the slow and of oculomotor neurons. Gamma motor neurons are relatively spared in amyotrophic lateral sclerosis, a MARKER contrast. In spinal muscular atrophy the SMN1 defect is the CAUSE.

CLASS 19. CENTRAL AUTONOMIC PREGANGLIONIC NEURONS

This class covers VOL3 chapters 168 and 169 and VOL5 chapter 259: sympathetic preganglionic neurons, sacral parasympathetic preganglionic neurons, and cranial-and-sacral parasympathetic preganglionic neurons.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: These are the central autonomic output neurons whose cell bodies sit in the spinal cord and brainstem, not in the peripheral ganglia. Sympathetic and sacral preganglionic neurons arise in the intermediate and lateral spinal neural tube; the cholinergic autonomic identity depends on factors overlapping the motor program, and the visceral-motor cranial members require Phox2b. Birth is embryonic (rodent). Not replaced.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Preganglionic sympathetic neurons in the intermediolateral column are a CASUALTY in multiple system atrophy and in progressive autonomic failure, and their loss is a MARKER of central autonomic degeneration. Spinal cord injury interrupts them as a CAUSE of autonomic dysreflexia.

CLASS 20. SPINAL CORD INTERNEURONS (DORSAL AND VENTRAL PROGENITOR DOMAINS)

This class covers VOL3 chapters 170 to 181 and VOL4 chapters 182 to 191: Renshaw cells, Ia and Ib inhibitory interneurons, propriospinal and commissural interneurons, central pattern generator interneurons, the dorsal founder classes dI1 to dI6 and dILA/dILB, the ventral founder classes V0 to V3, and the dorsal horn processing neurons (lamina I projection, islet, vertical/stalked, radial, central, antenna, lamina V wide dynamic range, Clarke column, lamina X, and Lissauer tract interneurons).

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: This class is organised around the progenitor-domain map that is one of the clearest lineage stories in the nervous system. Along the dorsoventral axis a Sonic hedgehog gradient ventrally and BMP/Wnt dorsally partition the neural tube into cardinal domains. The ventral domains p0 to p3 give V0 (Dbx1, Evx1), V1 (En1, including Renshaw and Ia inhibitory cells), V2 (Chx10/Vsx2 and Gata3), and V3 (Nkx2-2, Sim1). The dorsal domains dI1 to dI6 use Atoh1, Ngn1/2, Ascl1 and their downstream homeodomain codes; the late-born dILA/dILB add touch and pain processing. Motor neurons come from the intervening pMN domain (Class 18). Birth is embryonic (rodent), ventral before dorsal. Not replaced.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: The class is a CAUSE compartment in inherited hyperekplexia and spasticity when inhibitory V1/V2-derived circuits fail. Lamina I projection neurons are a CAUSE-and-MARKER node in chronic pain sensitisation. Renshaw and Ia inhibitory interneurons are CASUALTY early in amyotrophic lateral sclerosis, contributing to the excitotoxic loss of the motor neurons they normally restrain.

CLASS 21. RETINAL LINEAGE (OPTIC-CUP NEUROEPITHELIUM)

This class covers VOL4 chapters 192 to 219: rod and cone photoreceptors and the S, M and L cone subtypes, rod and cone bipolar cells and their ON, OFF, midget, diffuse, blue-cone and giant subtypes, horizontal cells (H1, H2, H3), the amacrine cells (AII, starburst, A17, dopaminergic, polyaxonal, interplexiform), and the ganglion cells (midget, parasol, small bistratified, intrinsically photosensitive, direction-selective, and the wide-field rare types).

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: Every retinal neuron arises from a single pool of multipotent retinal progenitor cells in the optic-cup neuroepithelium, a diencephalic outgrowth. Fate is set by birth order from a shared progenitor: the sequence is retinal ganglion cells first (requiring Atoh7/Math5), then cones, horizontal cells and most amacrine cells, then rods, bipolar cells and Muller glia last, an order conserved across mammals with heavy overlap between phases. Each cell's birthdate, defined by its final S phase, strongly biases its identity. Birth spans embryonic into early postnatal life (rodent). Human photoreceptor and foveal maturation continues for years after birth. Not replaced in mammals.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Rod photoreceptors are the primary CASUALTY of retinitis pigmentosa (with secondary cone loss) and cones the CASUALTY of the macular and cone dystrophies, both with strong monogenic CAUSE components (rhodopsin and dozens of other genes). Retinal ganglion cells are the CASUALTY of glaucoma and of hereditary optic neuropathies. The intrinsically photosensitive ganglion cells are relatively resistant, a MARKER contrast preserving circadian light input in some blindness.

CLASS 22. OLFACTORY SYSTEM NEURONS

This class covers VOL4 chapters 220 to 227: olfactory sensory neurons, mitral and tufted cells, periglomerular, short-axon, granule and Blanes cells of the olfactory bulb, and the migratory gonadotropin-releasing-hormone neurons.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: This is a mixed-origin regional class. Olfactory sensory neurons arise in the olfactory epithelium (from the olfactory placode) and are the standout regenerating neuron of the mammalian nervous system: basal stem cells replace them throughout life, so this class contains a genuinely replaceable member. The bulb projection neurons (mitral, tufted) arise from the telencephalic neuroepithelium embryonically. The bulb interneurons (granule, periglomerular) are a second standout: they are produced from the subventricular zone and delivered along the rostral migratory stream, robustly across life in rodent but with adult human bulb neurogenesis largely absent or contested. The migratory gonadotropin-releasing-hormone neurons (chapter 227) are a departure shared with chapter 96: placode-born, migrating into the hypothalamus.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Olfactory sensory neuron loss and bulb involvement are among the earliest MARKER changes in Parkinson and Alzheimer disease (hyposmia as a prodromal sign). Because olfactory sensory neurons regenerate, their loss is often recoverable, unlike central members. Migratory gonadotropin-releasing-hormone failure is a CAUSE of Kallmann syndrome.

CLASS 23. CRANIAL SENSORY GANGLION NEURONS (PLACODE AND NEURAL CREST)

This class covers VOL4 chapters 228 to 237: type I and type II spiral ganglion neurons, vestibular (Scarpa) ganglion neurons and their regular- and irregular-firing afferents, and the geniculate, petrosal, nodose, jugular and trigeminal ganglion neurons.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: Unlike the dorsal root ganglia, cranial sensory ganglia are dual-origin, built from both neurogenic placodes and neural crest. The spiral and vestibular ganglia derive from the otic placode; the geniculate, petrosal and nodose ganglia derive from the epibranchial placodes; the jugular ganglion and much of the trigeminal ganglion derive from neural crest. Neurogenesis depends on Neurog1 (dominant in the trigeminal) and Neurog2 (dominant in the epibranchial-derived ganglia), with redundancy in the petrosal and nodose, followed by NeuroD. Birth is embryonic (rodent). Not replaced.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Spiral ganglion neurons are a CASUALTY of sensorineural hearing loss and the target population for cochlear implantation; their survival is the limiting MARKER for implant success. Vestibular ganglion neurons are CASUALTY in vestibular neuritis. Trigeminal ganglion neurons are a CAUSE-and-MARKER node in trigeminal neuralgia and are the latency reservoir for herpes simplex virus.

CLASS 24. SOMATOSENSORY DORSAL ROOT GANGLION NEURONS (NEURAL CREST)

This class covers VOL4 chapter 238 and VOL5 chapters 239 to 255: proprioceptors, the low-threshold mechanoreceptors (SA1, SA2, RA1, RA2, A-delta D-hair, C-LTMR), the nociceptors (peptidergic, non-peptidergic, A-delta, C-fibre polymodal, A-beta, silent), thermoreceptors, pruriceptors, and visceral afferents.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: Dorsal root ganglion sensory neurons arise entirely from the trunk neural crest, unlike the dual-origin cranial ganglia. Neurogenesis comes in two waves: an early Neurog2 wave produces the large-diameter mechanoreceptors and proprioceptors, and a later Neurog1 wave produces the small-diameter nociceptors and thermoreceptors, all downstream of Sox10 in the crest. Diversification then runs through the neurotrophin receptors and Runx factors: Runx3 with TrkC sets proprioceptors, Runx1 with TrkA sets the non-peptidergic nociceptors, and the peptidergic nociceptors retain TrkA and CGRP. Birth is embryonic (rodent, roughly embryonic day 9.5 to embryonic day 13). Not replaced.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Nociceptors are the CAUSE-and-MARKER population of chronic and neuropathic pain, and channel mutations (SCN9A/Nav1.7) make them a CAUSE of both congenital insensitivity to pain and inherited pain syndromes. Large sensory neurons are CASUALTY in Friedreich ataxia and in platinum-drug and diabetic sensory neuropathies, with a length-dependent selective vulnerability.

CLASS 25. PERIPHERAL AUTONOMIC, PARAGANGLIONIC AND PARANEURON CELLS (NEURAL CREST)

This class covers VOL5 chapters 256 to 258, 260 to 265, and 281 to 284: sympathetic postganglionic and specialised (sudomotor, vasoconstrictor and related) neurons, parasympathetic postganglionic neurons, adrenal chromaffin and small intensely fluorescent cells, intrinsic cardiac, pelvic, and airway/bladder/pancreatic ganglion neurons, carotid- and aortic-body glomus-associated afferents, Merkel cells, pinealocytes, and paraneurons.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The autonomic and paraganglionic members share the sympathoadrenal neural-crest lineage. Crest cells migrate to the dorsal aorta, meet high BMP, and switch on the noradrenergic cascade Ascl1 (Mash1), Phox2b, Hand2, Gata2/3 and Insm1; Phox2b is essential for the whole program and Gata3 for tyrosine-hydroxylase onset and survival. Sympathetic neurons and adrenal chromaffin cells share a common crest progenitor, recently traced to a Schwann-cell-precursor intermediate for the medulla. Two departures: Merkel cells (chapter 282) are of epidermal origin in mammals rather than neural crest, and pinealocytes (chapter 283) are diencephalic pineal cells of a photoreceptor-like lineage, not crest at all. Birth is embryonic (rodent). Peripheral autonomic neurons show limited turnover; central replacement does not occur.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Chromaffin and sympathetic-lineage cells are the CAUSE compartment of phaeochromocytoma, paraganglioma and childhood neuroblastoma, the last driven by MYCN and ALK. Sympathetic and parasympathetic postganglionic neurons are CASUALTY in diabetic and amyloid autonomic neuropathy and in Lewy-body disease (cardiac sympathetic denervation is an early MARKER). Carotid-body glomus cells are a CAUSE node in sleep-disordered breathing.

CLASS 26. ENTERIC NERVOUS SYSTEM NEURONS (VAGAL AND SACRAL NEURAL CREST)

This class covers VOL5 chapters 266 to 280: intrinsic primary afferent (enteric sensory) neurons, generic and ascending and descending interneurons, excitatory and inhibitory muscle motor neurons, secretomotor and vasomotor neurons, intestinofugal neurons, the Dogiel type I, II and III morphologies, filamentous neurons, and dendritic type II neurons.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: The enteric nervous system, the largest peripheral neuronal collection, is colonised mostly by vagal neural crest (with a sacral contribution to the hindgut). Crest-derived cells migrate the full length of the gut and depend on RET-GDNF and endothelin-3-EDNRB signalling and on the transcription factors Sox10, Phox2b and Ascl1 (Mash1), with Hand2 for neuronal differentiation. Migration and neurogenesis run embryonically and continue after birth as the gut grows (rodent). The enteric system retains some capacity for neurogenesis from resident progenitors, so this class is partially self-renewing, more so than most central classes.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Failure of crest colonisation of the distal bowel is the CAUSE of Hirschsprung disease, with RET, SOX10, PHOX2B and EDNRB mutations; the aganglionic segment is the defining MARKER. Enteric neuron loss is a CASUALTY in Parkinson disease (where alpha-synuclein pathology may begin in the gut), in diabetic gastroparesis, and in Chagas disease.

CLASS 27. FUNCTIONALLY DEFINED EMERGENT CELL TYPES (LINEAGE REFUSED)

This class covers VOL5 chapters 285 to 301: place, grid, head-direction, border and boundary-vector, speed, time and object-vector cells, concept cells, mirror neurons, face cells, orientation- and direction-selective cells, simple and complex cells, command neurons, sparse-coding neurons, error and reward-prediction-error neurons, and nociceptive-specific neurons.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY: This class is refused as a developmental lineage, and the refusal is the honest answer. These are not a progenitor cohort. They are physiological categories, defined by what a neuron computes in the behaving animal (where it fires in space, whether it responds to a face, whether it reports a prediction error), and the cells that carry these labels are ordinary neurons drawn from lineages already covered above. Place, grid, head-direction, border, speed, time and object-vector cells are hippocampal and entorhinal excitatory neurons of Class 5 with interneuron partners from Class 6. Concept, face, mirror, simple, complex and orientation-selective cells are neocortical pyramidal and interneuron types of Classes 1, 3 and 4. Reward-prediction-error signalling belongs largely to the midbrain dopamine neurons of Class 10, and nociceptive-specific neurons are dorsal-horn projection neurons of Class 20. There is no separate germ zone, no separate transcription-factor cascade and no separate birth window to state for a functional label, and inventing one would be exactly the failure this project forbids. What would change this verdict is evidence that any of these functional identities is specified by a distinct developmental lineage rather than acquired through experience-dependent tuning of ordinary neurons; no such evidence exists at present. Their birth windows are those of their host lineages.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS: Disease attribution follows the host lineage. Place and grid cell disruption is a MARKER of early entorhinal-hippocampal degeneration in Alzheimer disease (tracking the Class 5 selective vulnerability). Reward-prediction-error signalling failure is a CAUSE-and-MARKER node in Parkinson disease and addiction through the Class 10 dopamine neurons. Mirror and face cell accounts of autism and prosopagnosia remain unsettled and are not asserted here as causal.
